**Phase-4 AUTO-01a–e DONE — AUTO-01 epic COMPLETE** (autonomous operations / closed-loop ops) — new `kanz/services/autopilot/`: the signals already existed (DATA-07 quality events, DATA-05 reconciliation divergence, model drift, OBS-01 SLO burn, PRED-07 inference circuit state); this adds the actuation layer. **AUTO-01a** (`internal/controller` + `internal/signal`): a bus consumer normalizes heterogeneous operational events into one `Signal` (the DATA-07 `DataQualityEvent` is decoded for its exact gap/staleness/drift kind + severity; the others recognized by event-type convention — non-signal traffic, the vast majority, is acked-and-ignored so the loop only ever sees actionable input), then a control loop matches each to a condition and dispatches. **AUTO-01b** (`internal/remediate`): data quarantine (on a DATA-05 divergence or a critical gap — halt the subject so bad data stops propagating) + model auto-rollback on drift (MLOPS-01e promotion in reverse), behind `Quarantiner`/`ModelRoller` seams (log-by-default impls, a deployment wires the command publisher — the DEBT-02 "inject the side-effect" stance). **AUTO-01c** (`internal/actuate`): scale-out + DR-01d regional failover behind `Scaler`/`Failover` seams, deciding WHEN to pull the levers INFRA-01c KEDA + DR-01d opened (scale ingest on staleness, scale inference on a tripped circuit, scale risk-engine on SLO burn). **AUTO-01d** (`internal/runbook` + `internal/plan` + `kanz/docs/runbooks/autopilot.md`): runbook-as-code — each condition maps to an ordered remediation runbook in one readable policy table; **conservative by design** — severity floors mean only CRITICAL data/drift/staleness/SLO conditions auto-remediate (softer signals escalate), a failed remediation step stops the runbook and **escalates to a human** rather than retrying blindly, and the hardest-to-reverse action (regional failover) is **gated off by default** (a critical SLO burn auto-scales and escalates the failover decision; `AUTOPILOT_AUTO_FAILOVER=true` opts in). **AUTO-01e**: a simulated broker stall (staleness/circuit), model drift, and data gap are each auto-remediated (quarantine/rollback/scale, asserted on the log-remediators' recorded state); escalation fires ONLY on unrecognized conditions (sub-threshold WARNING gap → escalate + no action; failed remediation step → escalate); plus signal classification (DQE kinds + event-type conditions, non-signals ignored) and failover gating both ways. **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean; `go test ./services/autopilot/...` green. No `-race` locally (no cgo on the dev box); CI runs it. **Carried-forward** (seams delivered): concrete remediators/actuators — a quarantine/rollback COMMAND publisher on the bus and a k8s client (patch Deployment/ScaledObject) + the DR-01d failover trigger — are deployment composition-root work behind the interfaces; the control loop + policy + escalation are delivered. **Next strategic-bet epic** per the Phase-4 roadmap order: DEVX-01 (developer productivity platform) — the last Phase-4 epic.

**Phase-4 LIN-01a–e DONE — LIN-01 epic COMPLETE** (data lineage & governance catalog) — new `kanz/services/lineage/`, the **dataset-level** sibling of the audit service: AUDIT-01c reconstructs the *event* causal chain (which event_id caused which), LIN-01 aggregates the same envelope substrate (correlation/causation/source/schema_ref, already on every event via EVT-17c) into a *dataset*-level graph + OpenLineage. **LIN-01a** (`internal/openlineage` + `internal/harvest` + `internal/graph`): a bus consumer (NATS `>`, one group, like audit — a dropped event is a hole in the graph) maps each envelope to its dataset (`kanz.{domain}`/`{MessageType}` — the SAME identity lake-sink tables (LAKE-01a) key on), links it to the dataset of its causation parent, and emits a spec-shaped OpenLineage `RunEvent` (job=source service, run=correlation_id, inputs=parent dataset, outputs=this dataset + a `kanzSchema` facet carrying the EVT-16 ref). `Emitter` seam: `LogEmitter` default, `HTTPEmitter` (POST /api/v1/lineage to Marquez/DataHub) when configured. The `graph.Memory` (interface + default, audit-store stance) answers transitive `Upstream` (cycle-safe, deterministic), the queryable provenance substrate. **LIN-01c** (`internal/governance`): a `Classifier` tags datasets PII (by name/id or schema-ref substring, mounted-file config); a `Governor` gates PII access through the **AUTH-01b** authorizer **deny-by-default**, binding the principal's tenant onto the resource so the **MT-01d** cross-tenant guard runs, and — by wrapping the authorizer in `auth.AuditedAuthorizer` + a `DecisionRecorder` — **every PII access decision (allow AND deny) is logged** into the observation stream (AUTH-01d, feeds AUDIT-01). Public datasets are allowed without an authz round-trip or a log entry. **LIN-01d** (`internal/query`): "where did this number come from" — governed provenance over the graph; the queried PII dataset is deny-by-default (403 + logged), upstream PII the requester can't read is returned **redacted** (topology shown — it's metadata — schema ref blanked), so an authorized steward sees the full chain and an unauthorized viewer sees a governed view. HTTP API (`internal/server`): catalog listing + per-event/per-dataset provenance, principal from the AUTH-01a OIDC ctx. **LIN-01b** (`kanz/infra/catalog/`): a DataHub ingestion recipe stitching four facets onto each dataset — lineage (the OpenLineage events), schema (EVT-16 registry via the `payloadSchemaRef` facet), ownership (kanz-schemas CODEOWNERS), PII classification (mirrors the LIN-01c governance config; DataHub is the read view, governance is the enforcement source of truth). **LIN-01e**: any output's full upstream lineage is queryable (a harvested 3-hop cascade resolves the leaf's transitive provenance); PII access governed (deny-by-default without the role) + logged (recorder asserts the decision count); upstream-PII redaction; PII-target-forbidden+logged; graph cycle-safety/self-loop-skip. **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean; `go test ./services/lineage/...` green; catalog YAML + example JSON configs parse. No `-race` locally (no cgo on the dev box); CI runs it. **Carried-forward** (seams delivered): a durable graph backend (Neo4j/DataHub) behind the `graph.Graph` interface, and the DataHub pattern-tag transformer wiring the per-dataset PII tags from the governance config. **Next strategic-bet epic** per the Phase-4 roadmap order: AUTO-01 (autonomous ops), then DEVX-01 (developer platform).

**Phase-4 LAKE-01b–e DONE — LAKE-01 epic COMPLETE** (the backtesting/research plane on top of the 01a sink). The whole plane turns on one load-bearing property — **point-in-time correctness, no future leakage** — carried by the MODEL-01b bitemporal store's `AsOf` knowledge horizon. **LAKE-01b** (`internal/lake/dataset/`): a `Materializer` that, for a `Sample{instrument, AsOf}`, builds a feature row (`spot`, trailing `ret_*`, `vol`) reading the store **only** as of that knowledge horizon, so a late vendor correction stamped with a later `KnowledgeTime` is invisible. Crucially it derives features with the **same** `compute.StoreReturnsProvider`/`ReturnsVolModel` the live engine uses, so a materialized value is bit-for-bit the live value — the hinge LAKE-01e depends on. The MLOPS-01f feature store (not built yet) joins via a `FeatureSource` seam, queried at the same `AsOf`. Placed at top-level `internal/lake/` rather than `services/lake-sink/internal/` because `tools/backtest` became a second consumer — the EVT-16a "promote when a second consumer appears" rule. **LAKE-01c** (`tools/backtest/`): a harness driving the EVT-20 replay `Source` (reused directly; `SliceSource` for offline/tests) through a deterministic `Strategy`, exposing each decision only a `PointInTime` pinned to the event's `event_time`. Offline + single-threaded: it `Collect`s the range into a deterministic order (event_time, then partition, offset) before processing, so a run is reproducible regardless of Kafka's cross-partition arrival order (event-class-rules §1). **LAKE-01e** (`tools/backtest/*_test.go`): the three properties — (1) determinism (same range twice ⇒ identical `[]Decision`), (2) ordering (a shuffled source yields the same stream), (3) **backtest reproduces live bit-for-bit** — a restatement learned after every event's `event_time` is invisible to the point-in-time reads so backtest decisions equal live decisions, while the same correction *does* surface once its knowledge horizon passes (the bitemporal read is correct both ways; a naive latest-value store would leak and diverge). **LAKE-01d** (`infra/lakehouse/`): Trino over the lakehouse — a Hive catalog reading the lake-sink NDJSON landing directly (queryable the moment events land, columns evolve as the EVT-16-decoded payloads do) + an Iceberg catalog for promoted/compacted tables (columnar, snapshot isolation, `FOR VERSION AS OF` time-travel); coordinator+worker manifests under a restricted-PSS `kanz-lakehouse` namespace, **read-only by posture** (managed writes disabled, non-root, read-only rootfs, secret-mounted creds); a Jupyter notebook pod pre-wired with the Trino client; external-table DDL + sample queries (incl. a decode-error triage query). **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean; `go test ./internal/lake/... ./tools/backtest/... ./services/lake-sink/...` green; lakehouse YAML parses. No `-race` locally (no cgo on the dev box); CI runs it. **Carried-forward**: the NDJSON→Iceberg compaction/commit job (the Iceberg catalog's producer) and the MLOPS-01f feature-store concrete `FeatureSource` are deployment/epoch-later work; the seams + the always-available raw Hive view are delivered. **Next strategic-bet epic** per the Phase-4 roadmap order: LIN-01 (lineage/governance), then AUTO-01 (autonomous ops), DEVX-01 (developer platform).

---

**Phase-4 LAKE-01a DONE** (CDC/sink → lakehouse, schema-evolution-aware) — new `kanz/services/lake-sink/`, the analytical-store half of the backtest-ready design (the system was backtest-ready by EVT-21d replay + PRED-11 point-in-time, but had no analytical store to exploit it). Streams the **durable Kafka log** (EVT-09, the system of record — not the live NATS spine, so a replay rebuilds the lake deterministically) on one consumer group and lands every event. **Schema-evolution seam** (`internal/decode`): resolves each envelope's `payload_schema_ref` against the EVT-16 registry (`GET /schemas/{ref}` → raw `FileDescriptorSet`), decodes the payload with `dynamicpb` + `protojson` to a JSON object, and caches descriptors per-ref forever (ref→descriptor is immutable, EVT-16c ships them `Cache-Control: immutable`). The point: a field added additively to a payload schema becomes a new lake column with **no redeploy** — the table tracks the published schema, not a compiled-in type (no new deps; `google.golang.org/protobuf` already vendored). **Landing** (`internal/sink`): a `Sink` interface with a default `FileSink` writing Hive-partitioned NDJSON (`{root}/{domain}/{entity}/dt=YYYY-MM-DD/part-{instance}.ndjson`, entity derived from the ref message name, per-process part-files so replicas never interleave) — the staging layer a catalog ingest (Iceberg/Delta via Trino/Spark) commits as a table; concrete table-format commits plug in behind `Sink` so the binary carries no heavy table-format dep (the repo's "seam, decouple from the concrete client" pattern, as DEBT-02). **Delivery** (`internal/cdc`): at-least-once (dups share `event_id`, downstream-compacted); decode outcomes split transient-vs-permanent — a registry 5xx/network error NAKs (bus redelivers, never lose the decoded payload) while an unknown-ref/malformed-payload lands the row envelope-only with `decode_error` set and acks (a poison message can't block the partition, and the lake still records the event occurred). Buffered writes made durable on a `FLUSH_INTERVAL` ticker + on shutdown. Sink-landing + decode-class metrics on the OBS-01c registry. **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean; `go test ./services/lake-sink/...` green — decode round-trip over a real `common.v1.Decimal` FDS, transient/permanent classification, immutable-ref caching, Hive layout + path-segment sanitization (hostile `domain`/`entity` can't escape the root), and the three CDC decode outcomes. No `-race` locally (no cgo on the dev box); CI runs it. **Next in LAKE-01**: 01b (point-in-time materialization joining MODEL-01b history + MLOPS-01f feature store), 01c (backtest harness over the EVT-20 replay pipeline), 01d (Trino + notebooks), 01e (bit-for-bit live-vs-backtest determinism test).

**Phase-4 DEBT-03 epic COMPLETE** (Python CI hardening — stop the `kanz-py` path rotting). **DEBT-03a** (`kanz-schemas/buf.gen.yaml`): wired the `buf.build/grpc/python:v1.66.0` remote plugin (out `gen/python`) alongside the existing `protocolbuffers/python` + `pyi` plugins, so the `buf generate` the `schema-release.yml` Python job runs now emits the `*_pb2_grpc.py` service stubs — the Python analog of the protoc-gen-go-grpc gap. Without it the **published** `kanz-schemas` PyPI SDK couldn't satisfy `inference.v1.InferenceService` (PRED-06) that the PRED-08 servicer implements; the CI *test* job already side-stepped this via `grpc_tools.protoc --grpc_python_out`, so only the release pipeline was missing the stub. Hardened the release `Verify wheel` step to `pip install … grpcio` and `import inference.v1.inference_service_pb2_grpc`, so a regression that drops the stub fails the release. **DEBT-03b/c** were already delivered under CICD-01b (verified, not re-done): `kanz-py/tests/test_consumer.py` already passes `Message(subject=…)` (17 sites) and the `kanz-ci.yml` `python` job already runs `pytest kanz-py/tests -q` gated in CI — checked off here to close the epic. **Verified locally**: `buf generate` with the three Python plugins resolves `grpc/python:v1.66.0` and emits `inference/v1/inference_service_pb2_grpc.py`; after the release job's `__init__.py` touch, `import inference.v1.inference_service_pb2_grpc` exposes `InferenceServiceStub` + `InferenceServiceServicer` (grpcio installed). DEBT-03 epic closed; the still-deferred Phase-4 DEBT-01 (external SDK publishing) remains blocked.

**Phase-4 DEBT-02 epic COMPLETE** (distributed coordination — DEBT-01 left deferred/blocked on external SDK publishing, skipped by user direction). Pattern throughout: keep the per-process default, add a SEAM to inject shared state, decouple from any concrete client (the repo's `NATSConfig.TLSConfig`-takes-a-ready-`*tls.Config` stance). **DEBT-02a** (`pkg/bus`): extracted a `Deduper` interface (`Seen`/`Record`); `DedupWindow` stays the default; new `RedisDedup` over a minimal 2-method `bus.RedisClient` interface (NO go-redis dep in the module — the deployment writes a ~6-line adapter), **fail-open** on Redis error (degrade to no-dedup, never block), best-effort (Seen/Record non-atomic ⇒ idempotent handlers remain the floor). Consumer gained `WithDeduper` (precedence over `WithDedupWindow`); field is now `Deduper`, nil-safe via boxed nil-receiver. Tests: `dedup_redis_test.go` (fake client — cross-instance share, TTL, fail-open, nil-safe). **DEBT-02d** (`pkg/bus`): `nreplica_dedup_test.go` — 4 replicas sharing one `RedisDedup` under round-robin redelivery chaos ⇒ exactly 1 side-effect/key; **contrast test** proves per-instance `DedupWindow` leaks duplicates across replicas (documents why the shared mode exists). **DEBT-02b Go** (`internal/integrity`): refactored `Reconciler` to hold a `PendingStore` interface (in-memory default preserves exact prior semantics + all existing tests); new `NewReconcilerWithStore` = the shared-state mode; `ClaimOrMatch` documented as MUST-be-atomic-per-key (Redis ⇒ one Lua script). `reconcile_shared_test.go`: two reconcilers sharing a store match a NATS sighting on pod A against the Kafka sighting on pod B; contrast test shows separate stores leave both Pending (false discrepancies). **DEBT-02b Python** (`kanz_inference/registry`): `coordination.py` — `CoordinatedRegistry` wraps the process-local `Registry`, mirrors register/record_validation/unregister onto `platform.model` (serializable `RegistryEvent`, not the live `Model`) and `apply`s inbound peer events, rematerializing the `Model` via the existing `ModelLoader`; `platform.model` (append log, infinite retention) replayed from 0 rebuilds the cache; origin-stamp loop prevention; publish keyed by model_id keeps validation-before-promote order for the MLOPS-01a gate. `RegistryPublisher` Protocol is the transport seam. 5 new tests (convergence, self-skip, unregister propagation, no-op-no-publish, wire round-trip). **DEBT-02c**: `kanz/docs/coordination.md` — best-effort (per-process + idempotent handlers) vs strong (shared-state) guarantee classes, per-component table, when-to-use, and the go-redis/Lua/`platform.model` wiring sketches. **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt`, `go test ./pkg/bus/ ./internal/integrity/` all green; Python `pytest test_model_registry.py test_registry_coordination.py` = 34 passed (PYTHONPATH = gen/python + kanz-py). No `-race` locally (no cgo); CI runs it. **Carried-forward**: concrete Redis impls (`bus.RedisClient` adapter, Lua-atomic `PendingStore`) + the `platform.model` consumer/publisher bus-wiring are deployment composition-root work (the seams + docs are delivered, the transport bindings land where the services are wired). DEBT-02 epic closed.

**Phase-4 SEC-02 epic COMPLETE** (supply-chain & runtime hardening, a–e) — extends the CICD-01d build-side gate to runtime + dependencies. **SEC-02a** (`infra/security/runtime/`): `network-policies.yaml` (default-deny ingress+egress in `kanz-services` + minimal allows — DNS, NATS/Kafka, OTLP, gateway→risk-engine, ingress→gateway, scrape); `pod-security.yaml` (namespace pinned to the **restricted** Pod Security Standard, `enforce-version: v1.31`, + the canonical hardened securityContext as policy-as-data); and the hardening wired onto the real workload — `infra/deploy/risk-engine-rollout.yaml` now sets pod+container `securityContext` (runAsNonRoot/65532, readOnlyRootFilesystem + `/tmp` emptyDir, allowPrivilegeEscalation false, drop ALL caps, seccomp RuntimeDefault). gVisor noted as a per-RuntimeClass override. **SEC-02b**: cosign-signature admission already existed (CICD-01d `cluster-image-policy.yaml`); added the CVE half — `release.yml` now also produces a `cosign-vuln` attestation, and the policy requires BOTH SBOM + vuln-scan attestations. Critical-CVE blocking is enforced at the choke point (release.yml trivy `--exit-code 1` on CRITICAL *before* signing ⇒ unsigned ⇒ admission rejects); the vuln attestation gives SEC-02c's re-scan a baseline for CVEs disclosed post-release (the case inline admission can't see). **SEC-02c** (`.github/`): `dependabot.yml` (gomod ×2, pip, npm, github-actions, docker ×3, grouped weekly); `security.yml` `dependency-scan` job (scheduled govulncheck + trivy fs, fail on HIGH/CRITICAL fixable); `SECURITY.md` (remediation SLA — Critical 3d / High 14d / Medium 30d — + reporting + gates). **SEC-02d**: `security.yml` `gitleaks` job (full-history detect on PR/push) + `kanz/.githooks/pre-commit` (`gitleaks protect --staged`, skip-with-warning if not installed since CI is the hard gate) + `.gitleaks.toml` (default rules + allowlist for `_test.go`/`testdata`/`fixtures`/placeholder creds). **SEC-02e** (`infra/security/test/`): `gitleaks-planted-secret.sh` (plants a non-allowlisted secret, asserts gitleaks exit==1 detected — wired as the `secret-scan-selftest` CI job; guards against an over-broad allowlist silently disabling the gate) + `verify-admission.sh` (cosign verify reproducing the webhook's signature+SBOM+vuln checks; unsigned ⇒ fails ⇒ the "rejected at admission" proof). **Design choices**: Dependabot over Renovate (zero-infra, native to the host); deterministic build-gate + post-deploy re-scan for CVEs rather than a fragile inline admission CUE parse of the vuln predicate; gitleaks self-test gates exit-code 1 specifically and skips (not false-passes) when no live scanner/daemon. **Verified locally**: all new YAML parses (pyyaml), all shell `sh -n` clean, the self-test runs + cleans up (SKIPs honestly here — no live gitleaks/docker daemon; runs for real in CI). **Not runnable locally**: k8s admission/PSS/NetworkPolicy + cosign (need a cluster/registry) — exercised in CI / by the scripts. SEC-02 epic closed.

**Phase-4 LATENCY-01 epic COMPLETE** (01a–d). **LATENCY-01d DONE** (regression guard + burst test): added `kanz/internal/risk/compute/regression_test.go` with (1) `TestPositions_WarmCacheIsZeroAllocs` — locks F1 (warm `Positions()` allocates 0/op); (2) `TestComputeMeasures_AllocsConstantInN` — locks F2/F3 by asserting warm-cache `ComputeMeasures` allocs are O(1) in position count (n=1024 within +4 of n=16, abs ceiling 32) via deterministic `testing.AllocsPerRun`; (3) `TestComputeMeasuresP99UnderBurst` — drives the measures query under 10× CPU oversubscription (10×GOMAXPROCS workers, clone-per-call realistic path) and asserts p99 ≤ 250ms (generous vs the ~35ms observed — p99 there is scheduler/GC jitter, not compute; catches an O(n²)/lock-convoy regression that would hit seconds, stays under the 500ms end-to-end SLO). **CI wiring**: the alloc guards are ordinary tests caught by the existing kanz-ci `go test -race ./...` gate (deterministic — chosen over a flaky timing benchstat-vs-baseline); added a `benchmarks` job to `.github/workflows/latency.yml` that also runs the `testing.B` benchmarks (`-benchtime=10x`) so they stay compilable and their numbers surface per PR. **Design note**: 01d deliberately uses `testing.AllocsPerRun` (deterministic) as the actual regression GATE rather than timing comparisons, because allocations/op was the measured 01c win and is stable on noisy shared runners; timing is left to the generous burst budget + the LATENCY-01a end-to-end k6 smoke. **Verified**: the three tests pass; full compute suite green. **LATENCY-01 epic closed** — next Phase-4 epic per the roadmap order is SEC-02 (supply-chain & runtime hardening).

---

**Phase-4 LATENCY-01c DONE** (compute hot-path optimizations — implemented the F1/F2/F3 wins the 01b profile measured, rather than the task line's speculative bus-side object-pooling/batched-framing, which the profile gave no evidence for and which would be premature). **F1** — `domain.Portfolio` now lazily memoizes the sorted `Positions()` slice (`sortedCache`, invalidated to nil by `SetPosition`/`Forget`/`ClearPositions`): the ~8 calls per `ComputeMeasures` query sort+allocate once, not 8×. Race-free without a lock because every reader holds a per-query `Clone` (state.Store.Snapshot) owned by one goroutine; the shared live copy is only read under the per-aggregate lock via `Clone`, which copies the map directly and never populates the cache. **F2/F3** — a new allocation-free `decAccum` (running coefficient/exponent, in `compute/decimal.go`) replaces the per-position `addDecimal`/`addMoney` fold in `sumInBaseCurrency` and `ComputeExposure`'s currency buckets: `O(n)`→`O(1)` Decimal allocations. Byte-identical results (the accumulator's zero value == the old `zeroDecimal()` seed), so no measure value changes — all existing tests pass untouched. Added `domain/portfolio_test.go` locking the cache-invalidation contract (read→mutate→read reflects the mutation; Forget/Clear invalidate). Benchmarks updated to **clone per iteration** (realistic per-query cold-cache path, incl. the snapshot clone). **Measured (n=1024)**: `ComputeMeasures` **3,624→19 allocs/op, 1.51ms→0.30ms (~5×)** despite the 01c run now also paying the clone the 01b baseline omitted; `sumInBaseCurrency` 1,029→1 alloc; `ComputeExposure` 6,161→2,071 allocs (the residual is genuine per-instrument `Money` output). Compute arithmetic is now effectively free — what remains per query is the snapshot `Clone` map-copy + the single `O(n log n)` sort (both state-layer, not compute). Results + remaining levers in `compute/profiling.md` "Results (LATENCY-01c)". **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/risk/... ./services/risk-engine/...` all green (no `-race` locally — no cgo on the dev box; CI runs `-race`). **Next**: LATENCY-01d (`testing.B` regression guard in CI over these benchmarks + the 10× burst p99 test) — closes the LATENCY-01 epic.

**Phase-4 LATENCY-01b DONE** (profile the compute hot paths). Added benchmarks co-located in `kanz/internal/risk/compute/`: `bench_test.go` (black-box `ComputeExposure`/`ComputeMeasures`/`Positions` over 16/256/1024-position books) and `decimal_bench_test.go` (white-box `package compute` micro-benchmarks for the unexported `addDecimal`/`mulDecimal`/`absDecimal`/`sumInBaseCurrency`). Findings written up in `profiling.md`. **Headline (measured + pprof-confirmed at n=1024)**: `domain.(*Portfolio).Positions` dominates — **74.9% of allocations, ~69% of CPU** — because it allocates a fresh `[]Position` and runs an `O(n log n)` reflection `sort.Slice` on *every* call, and one `ComputeMeasures` over `DefaultRegistry` calls it ~8× (each of the 4 measures calls both `sumInBaseCurrency` and `sumUncertaintyInBaseCurrency`, each calling `p.Positions()`); the sort is pure overhead for order-independent summing. **Second**: the proto `Decimal` helpers heap-allocate one 64 B `*Decimal` per op → `O(n)` allocs per sum (`addDecimal` = 23.7% of allocations); together Positions+addDecimal ≈ 98.7% of all allocations. **Third**: `ComputeExposure` churns ~6 Money/Decimal allocs per position (the currency-key map+sort is NOT hot). **Perspective**: at the placeholder math `ComputeMeasures` n=1024 ≈ 1.5 ms (inside the 500 ms budget) but ~950 KB / ~3.6k allocs *per query*, run on every debounced recompute, is the GC-pressure/tail-latency driver the LATENCY-01 p99 budget guards — and it compounds with MODEL-01's real per-position math. **Feeds LATENCY-01c** (the optimizations): F1 share one position slice per query + drop the compute-path sort; F2 value-type Decimal accumulator (O(n)→O(1) allocs); F3 same accumulator for exposure buckets. **Verified**: `go vet` + `go test` green; benchmarks run clean. **Next**: LATENCY-01c (implement the F1/F2/F3 optimizations), then 01d (`testing.B` regression guard in CI + 10× burst p99 test).

**Phase-4 LATENCY-01a DONE** (low-latency hot path, subtask a — the CI load-test gate). The SRE-01e harness scripts (`config.js`/`baseline.js`/`soak.js`) and the budgets (p99≤500ms / p95≤250ms / err≤0.1%, the shared ORCH-01f number) already existed; what was missing was a stack to run them against and a workflow to run them. Added: (1) `kanz/test/load/docker-compose.yml` — the minimal read-path slice (NATS JetStream + `risk-engine` in-memory-state+gRPC + `api-gateway` with auth/signing/quotas OFF, plaintext upstream; no Postgres/Kafka). (2) `kanz/services/api-gateway/Dockerfile` (mirrors the risk-engine distroless/static image; build context = repo root so it COPYs the generated `kanz-schemas/gen/go` SDK). (3) `kanz/test/load/seed/` — a tiny `go run`-able publisher that puts one `PortfolioSnapshot` (PF1, 4 positions w/ MV, tenant `load-test`) on the spine via the production `bus.Producer`, because an unseeded portfolio returns `ErrPortfolioNotFound`→404 and would trip the error budget; it hardcodes the `risk.portfolio.snapshot` subject rather than importing `internal/risk` (RISK-02 arch boundary). (4) `kanz/test/load/smoke.js` — a ~60s constant-arrival-rate run reusing `budgetThresholds`. (5) `.github/workflows/latency.yml` — per-PR `load-smoke` (buf-generate → compose up → seed → poll exposure 200 → k6 smoke via `grafana/k6` on `--network host` → teardown) on hot-path/harness changes; manual `capacity-baseline` runs the full `baseline.js` ramp against a real `base_url` (the 4000-rps ramp is only meaningful off a multi-node deploy, not the single-node compose stack). **Design choice (asked + confirmed)**: short smoke per-PR + manual full baseline, not a per-PR full-ramp gate (p99 on shared runners is noisy). **Latency SLOs**: the component latency SLOs already exist in `infra/observability/slo/slo.yaml` (api-gateway query-latency + risk-engine recompute-latency, both 99%@500ms); no new fabricated series — an explicit e2e ingest→publish SLI needs a series that isn't instrumented yet (left to LATENCY-01b/c profiling+optimizations). **Verified locally**: `go build ./test/load/seed` passes against the current `gen/go`. **Not run locally**: the compose stack / k6 (no Docker on the dev box) — it runs in CI. **Next in LATENCY-01**: 01b (profile compute hot paths), 01c (pooling/batching/single-writer), 01d (`testing.B` regression guard + 10× burst p99 test).

**Phase-3 MODEL-01 epic COMPLETE** (real risk analytics, 01a–i). **MODEL-01a–i DONE**: (01a) `reference.v1` schemas; (01b) market-data ingestion + bitemporal point-in-time `store.Store`; (01c) `StoreReturnsProvider` + `BindReturns` seam; (01d) historical-sim VaR; (01e) Monte-Carlo VaR (correlated factor-shock); (01f) the `factor.Classifier` instrument→sector/asset-class lookup + `factor.SectorExposure`/`ComputeExposure` completing the RISK-06 ExposureBySector deferral; (01g) `compute.VolModel`/`ReturnsVolModel` + `PopulateUncertainty` enrichment wiring real per-position 1σ MV uncertainty (=`|MV|×σ`, σ = sample-stdev of the same MODEL-01c return series) into the RISK-08 propagation at the four engine snapshot boundaries (`EngineImpl` query path + `Recomputer`), filling the previously-always-nil `Measure.UncertaintyAbs` (incl. the VaR band); (01h) stress/scenario `library` + new `v1.SectorShock` (sector-differentiated, resolved against the 01f factor model) + `scenario.WithClassifier`/`engine.WithClassifier` dispatch — `GFC_2008`/`COVID_2020` named full-GICS-curve scenarios via `Named`/`Names`, `SectorCurve` the hypothetical-curve primitive; (01i) the VaR verification suite — Kupiec-band backtest (deterministic, 17/1050≈1.62% exceptions, LR=3.42<3.841), independent delta-normal reconciliation (MC ≤2.5%, historical ≤8%), and end-to-end point-in-time/no-future-leakage through the bitemporal store. **Next: the MODEL-01 epic is closed** — the Phase-3 successor is **MLOPS-01** (model validation/explainability/governance, kanz-py) or **SRE-01**/**AUDIT-01**; the open production-wiring integration below is the prerequisite to make 01's models actually serve. **Engine-wiring seam (still open, an integration task)** — the one piece that turns the real models on in production: the risk-engine must construct a `compute.StoreReturnsProvider` over a `marketdata/store` (and a `factor.Classifier` from reference data) and call `varmodel.Register`/`RegisterMonteCarlo` + use `factor.ComputeExposure` + pass `engine.WithVolModel`/`WithRecomputeVolModel(compute.NewReturnsVolModel(provider, 0))`; the engine builds no market-data/reference store today, so `DefaultRegistry` still serves the `compute.VaR99` placeholder + 2-dimension exposure + nil vol model (no uncertainty band). **Carried-forward seams still open**: (a) `Observation.CurrencyCode` left empty by ingestion — reference-data join fills it; (b) `MarketDataBatch` carriage un-wired; (c) `reference.v1` still not in `gen/go` — 01f used a Go-native `factor.Classification` (like `store.Observation`) so it didn't force a regen, but a reference-data STORE/service (the reference mirror of 01b, feeding both the `Classifier` and the `Observation.CurrencyCode` join) is the missing piece, likely its own task; (d) the price store is platform-global (not tenant-scoped); (e) VaR tail-aligns returns by recency, not date; (f) the 01g vol model is delta-normal (`|MV|×σ`, per-position independent under RISK-08's default propagation) — it does NOT yet use MODEL-01e's full covariance for cross-position correlation in the band, and is wired-but-off in production until the engine-wiring seam above lands.

### Cloud Foundation, GitOps & Autoscaling

- [x] **INFRA-01e** AZ-kill + autoscaler-reaction validation, **closes the INFRA-01 epic** — new `kanz/infra/chaos/az-kill.yaml` + `autoscale-validation.md`, extends `verify.sh`. **AZ-kill** (Chaos Mesh PodChaos, `nodeSelectors: topology.kubernetes.io/zone`, mode all) is the AZ-scale companion to the SRE-01c single-pod eviction: hypothesis = a whole-zone loss leaves the system serving (the INFRA-01d multi-AZ spread keeps replicas in surviving zones, the PDB held quorum, the KEDA floor + reschedule restore capacity). `verify.sh az-kill` asserts the cross-cutting no-`SLOFastBurn` invariant + flat 5xx + advancing recompute + no data gap. **Autoscaler reaction** (`verify.sh autoscale` + the validation doc): inject lag, assert `kanz_bus_pending_messages` elevated AND the HPA scaled above its floor of 3 — proving INFRA-01c actually reacts (the PRED-14 premise), then scales back after cooldown. A fuller infra AZ loss uses AWSChaos ec2-stop; the pod-level kill exercises the same app-resilience contract. YAML + `sh -n` validated · `kanz/infra/chaos/`
- [x] **INFRA-01d** Multi-AZ topology spread + PDB + quotas — `kanz/infra/deploy/availability.yaml` + topologySpreadConstraints on the risk-engine Rollout. **Spread**: replicas across `topology.kubernetes.io/zone` (maxSkew 1, `ScheduleAnyway` so a saturated zone degrades placement rather than blocking a scale-up/rollout) — so a single-AZ loss (INFRA-01e) leaves survivors serving. **PodDisruptionBudget** (minAvailable 2) bounds VOLUNTARY disruption (drains/upgrades/chaos) below quorum; involuntary AZ loss is covered by spread + the KEDA floor. **ResourceQuota** caps namespace compute so a runaway INFRA-01c scale-out or bad deploy can't starve the shared node pool; **LimitRange** gives every container schedulable defaults + a ceiling. Sized above the KEDA maxReplicaCount headroom · `kanz/infra/deploy/`
- [x] **INFRA-01c** KEDA autoscaling on the OBS-01c backpressure signal — `kanz/infra/deploy/risk-engine-scaledobject.yaml`. **The PRED-14 premise made operational**: when the consumer falls behind, add workers to drain the backlog; idle back when caught up. Scales the **Argo Rollout** (not a Deployment — KEDA drives replicas while the CICD-01e canary analysis still gates revisions) on a **Prometheus trigger over `kanz_bus_pending_messages{group="risk-engine"}`** — risk-engine consumes NATS, so pending (not Kafka lag) is its signal; reading the OBS-01c series via Prometheus keeps the scale signal identical to what the dashboards/alerts show (one source of truth; Kafka-consuming services use `kanz_bus_consumer_lag` with the same shape). Floor 3 (never below the INFRA-01d quorum/spread), ceiling 12, fast scale-up (30s) / slow scale-down (300s) to avoid flapping · `kanz/infra/deploy/`
- [x] **INFRA-01b** GitOps delivery (Argo CD) — new `kanz/infra/gitops/` (`bootstrap.yaml`, `projects.yaml`, `applicationset.yaml`, README). **App-of-apps via a matrix ApplicationSet**: one Application per (environment × component), where the environment list (`primary`, `dr` — the DR-01 active/passive pair) IS the per-environment overlay dimension (own destination cluster + env label), and components are the in-cluster workloads (NATS, Kafka, the `infra/deploy` delivery layer, security). Adding a component or an environment is a one-line list entry. **`bootstrap.yaml`** is the single hand-applied Application — it syncs the gitops dir so Argo manages its own config from git thereafter. **`projects.yaml`** AppProject is an allowlist (repo + namespaces + cluster-scoped kinds), the deny-by-default posture matching AUTH-01b/MT-01c. `selfHeal`+`prune` make git the source of truth. **Deliberate boundaries**: the Terraform foundation isn't here (cloud, not k8s); Prometheus-rule/Grafana artifacts ship as their own PrometheusRule/ConfigMap wrapping (not raw-synced); templated/one-shot manifests (`tenancy.yaml`, `*-job.yaml`, smoke tests) are excluded so a Job isn't perpetually recreated · `kanz/infra/gitops/`

Multi-AZ VPC (≥3 AZs, one NAT per AZ so an AZ loss strands no other); EKS with a `general` stateless pool + a tainted `stateful` pool (dedicated capacity for broker/DB anti-affinity + local-storage PVs); a **COMPLIANCE Object-Lock + KMS backup bucket** (DR-01b WAL/PITR + AUDIT-01b WORM — immutable backups are what make "provable RPO" real) with a scoped backup IRSA role. Community `terraform-aws-modules` for vpc/eks/iam (idiomatic, no bloat); remote S3 state per env. terraform not installed in-env (HCL written against the pinned module APIs) · `kanz/infra/terraform/`

### Disaster Recovery & Backup

- [x] **DR-01e** Quarterly DR drill, **closes the DR-01 epic** — `kanz/docs/runbooks/dr-drill.md`. A scheduled, supervised **live failover** that proves RPO ≤ 1min / RTO ≤ 15min is real (the DR analog of the SRE-01d GameDay — a drill that doesn't actually cut over proves nothing). Quarterly, business hours, IC + DB + platform on-call. **Measured gates, not vibes**: RPO = max replication lag at T0 (Kafka heartbeats topic + `cnpg status` Postgres lag) must be < 1min BEFORE cutting over; RTO = `time(failover.sh)` to services-Ready + a synthetic end-to-end txn (gateway query → risk response) ≤ 15min; plus post-failover integrity (`/v1/audit/verify` 200) and no data gap across the cutover. Records the measured numbers as the audit evidence the continuity control is tested; a missed gate is a DR regression fixed before next quarter · `kanz/docs/runbooks/dr-drill.md`
- [x] **DR-01d** Failover automation + runbook — `kanz/infra/dr/failover.sh` + `kanz/docs/runbooks/dr.md`. **Runbook-as-code**: `failover.sh` IS the procedure, executable — ordered + idempotent, resumable per `STEP=`. **Cutover order is load-bearing** (state → log/spine → compute → traffic, because a service up before its DB/spine just errors): (1) `cnpg promote` the Postgres warm standbys to writable primaries; (2) verify DR Kafka present (DR-01a) + bring up NATS streams; (3) run the DR-01c `nats-rebuild` Job to repopulate the live spine; (4) scale up services (they read the promoted DSN from their SEC-01d CSI secret via the DR overlay + consume the rebuilt spine); (5) shift DNS/global-LB to DR, gated on api-gateway readiness. Region failover is a HUMAN decision (not auto-triggered); the runbook covers when-to-declare, confirm-recovered, and **failback as a planned change** (re-replicate the now-stale primary from DR, cut back in a window — never just flip). Indexed in the runbooks README · `kanz/infra/dr/`, `kanz/docs/runbooks/dr.md`
- [x] **DR-01c** NATS spine reconstruction — new `kanz/tools/natsrebuild/` (`rebuild.go` + test + `cmd/nats-rebuild`) + `kanz/infra/dr/nats/` (Job + README). NATS is the short-retention live tier, **not** a system of record — so DR doesn't replicate it, it REBUILDS it from the Kafka log. **Reuses the EVT-20 read-only `replay.Reader` as the Kafka source but is the inverse of replay**: replay isolates onto `replay.{run}.*` + stamps `QUALITY_FLAG_REPLAYED` (live sinks reject it); reconstruction republishes onto the **original** subject (`envelope.EventType`) with the envelope **unchanged**, so live consumers accept it as normal (idempotent handlers, DEBT-02, make re-consuming the window safe). A test pins exactly this: published to the live subject, headers/key propagated, envelope unmutated, **no REPLAYED flag**. The one-shot Job drains a bounded recent window (24h default, matching the live max-age — older history stays in Kafka) onto the spine; Kafka read plaintext in-cluster (kafka:9092, like the provisioning Jobs), NATS publish over SEC-01c mTLS (the rebuild's SVID). Documented RTO contribution: streams up (~1–2min) → rebuild window (minutes, shardable) → start consumers. `go test ./tools/natsrebuild/...` green · `kanz/infra/dr/nats/`
- [x] **DR-01b** Postgres PITR + cross-region replicas — new `kanz/infra/dr/postgres/` (`cluster.yaml`, `replica.yaml`, README). The two stateful DBs (PERS-01 risk state + EVT-16 schema registry) as CloudNativePG clusters; the append-only/WORM stores (market history, audit log) have their own retention and aren't restated here. **Continuous WAL archiving to a cross-region object store** (`archive_timeout=30s` bounds the archived-WAL RPO to ≤30s even idle) + daily base backups ⇒ **PITR** (restore to any instant in 30d — the defense against logical corruption a streaming replica alone can't give, since a bad DELETE replicates too). **DR-region warm-standby replica clusters** replay that WAL (`replica.enabled` + `bootstrap.recovery` from the object store, so they survive a primary-region partition); promote on failover (`cnpg promote`), services repoint their SEC-01d CSI DSN at the promoted cluster. CloudNativePG over bespoke cron (K8s-native barman/PITR/replica-cluster; managed RDS/Cloud SQL is the documented drop-in equivalent). RPO ≤ 1min; bucket should be object-locked (same WORM posture as AUDIT-01b). YAML validated · `kanz/infra/dr/postgres/`
- [x] **DR-01a** Kafka cross-region replication (MirrorMaker 2) — new `kanz/infra/dr/kafka/` (`mirrormaker2.yaml` + README). Replicates the **log of record** (the `kanz-messaging` Kafka cluster) into a DR region so a region loss costs at most the replication lag — the foundation DR-01c (rebuild NATS from the replicated log) and DR-01d (fail consumers over) stand on. **Active/passive, primary→dr only**, with MM2 running **in the DR region pulling from primary** (`connect-mirror-maker.sh --clusters dr`): the replicator must survive a primary-region outage, and a remote consumer on a flaky cross-region link is MM2's design point. **IdentityReplicationPolicy** keeps topic names IDENTICAL across regions (`risk.portfolio` stays `risk.portfolio`, not `primary.risk.portfolio`) so failover repoints the bootstrap with no topic remapping and DR-01c reads the same names — safe because replication is one-directional (no active/active loop). Replicates every domain topic + snapshot + `dlq.*` (the DLQ is part of the record) minus MM2/Connect internals; `sync.topic.configs` on, and **consumer-group offsets translated to DR via checkpoints + `sync.group.offsets`** (5s interval) so a failed-over consumer resumes near where it left off — inside the RPO ≤ 1min target, measurable from the heartbeats topic. **SEC-01c mTLS to BOTH clusters via one SVID** (both trust `kanz.internal`; spiffe-helper init+sidecar mirrors the broker's, empty endpoint-identification ⇒ verify the SPIFFE URI SAN). 2-worker Deployment (HA, coordinates via DR-side internal topics, RF 3). **Operational deps documented**: set the primary cross-region bootstrap; grant MM2's SVID READ-all on primary + admin on DR (deny-by-default MT-01c, else it silently mirrors nothing); stand up the DR cluster first. YAML validated (4 docs) · `kanz/infra/dr/kafka/`

### Auditability & Regulatory Reporting

- [x] **AUDIT-01e** Audit integration tests, **closes the AUDIT-01 epic** — `kanz/services/audit/internal/audit/e2e_test.go` (+ per-package tests in chain/audit/lineage/report). The three board behaviors as ONE end-to-end flow over the real projector + store + lineage + report + chain (nothing mocked): a realistic correlated cascade is projected (market tick → model DecisionLog → order COMMAND → CommandOutcome FACT, linked by causation_id), then **(1) reconstruct-in-<1min** — `lineage.Reconstruct("outcome")` returns the root-first ancestry [tick,signal,order,outcome] with the decision's reasoning intact, asserted under the 1min budget (microseconds in practice — map-keyed, no scan); **(2) tamper-detected** — silently rewriting a stored record's summary makes `report.Verify` fail (the folded-in prev-hash breaks every later hash); **(3) report-end-to-end** — the `command-outcomes` template generates with the right body + a *verified* attestation + CSV render. Per-package: chain (determinism, intact-verify, content-tamper-at-index, reorder), classify (all 6 kinds incl. the metric-vs-decision wire-ambiguity guard), projector idempotency + store chain integrity, lineage ancestry/tree/not-found, report attestation/CSV/retention+legal-hold. `go test ./services/audit/...` green; full `go build ./...` green · `kanz/services/audit/`
- [x] **AUDIT-01d** Regulatory report generation + retention/legal-hold — `kanz/services/audit/internal/report/` (`report.go`, `retention.go`, `templates.go`). **Configurable templates as data**: a `Template` is a named `audit.Filter` + `Format` (JSON/CSV), so adding a report is config not code (same policy-as-data shape as the AUTH-01b authz config); ships `authz-decisions`/`command-outcomes`/`data-quality`/`full-log` built-ins. **Every report carries a tamper-evidence attestation**: `Verify` runs the AUDIT-01b chain over the WHOLE log at generation time, so a report states "here are the records AND the log they came from is intact" — exported because it's also the `/verify` endpoint and the AUDIT-01e check. CSV embeds the attestation as header rows so a single artifact is self-attesting. **Retention/legal-hold as pure policy** (`EvaluatePurge`): `RetentionPolicy.Retain` (zero = keep forever, the safe regulatory default) classifies records past their window as purge-eligible; a `LegalHold` (by correlation_id/tenant_id) overrides retention and pins them. No mutation/deletion here — the log is WORM; this produces the decision a privileged lifecycle job acts on · `kanz/services/audit/internal/report/`
- [x] **AUDIT-01c** Causal-chain reconstruction — `kanz/services/audit/internal/lineage/lineage.go`. Given any `event_id`, `Reconstruct` returns the full upstream lineage: the **ancestry** (walk `causation_id` parent-by-parent to the root trigger, returned root-first) plus the **correlation tree** (every record sharing the `correlation_id`, linked into a causation tree — the whole cascade one trigger produced, not just the direct line). Two store lookups (a Get-walk + one Query), map-keyed, so it stays far under the AUDIT-01e <1min budget on large cascades. Defensive: a `visited` set breaks malformed lineage cycles; a cause outside the audit window ends the walk (best-effort to the edge); multiple tree roots are wrapped in a synthetic holder so nothing is dropped · `kanz/services/audit/internal/lineage/`
- [x] **AUDIT-01b** Tamper-evidence: hash chain + WORM — `kanz/services/audit/internal/chain/` (`chain.go`, README) + the `0001_audit_log.sql` WORM trigger. **Two halves, both required**: (1) the hash chain — `hash = sha256(prev_hash ‖ canonical(record))` with a domain separator, so altering/reordering/deleting any record breaks its hash and every later one; `Verify` is a linear walk returning the first broken index. (2) WORM storage — the migration installs a `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` trigger that raises, making the table insert-only even to the app role (object-lock/COMPLIANCE mode is the equivalent at the export tier, documented). Detection without prevention is weak (attacker rewrites record + chain); prevention without detection is weak (WORM misconfig) — together they're tamper-*evident*. **Payload-agnostic by design**: chain hashes a caller-supplied `[]byte` and never imports `audit`, so `audit` depends on `chain` not the reverse; `Record.Canonical()` is the deterministic serialization (sorted attr keys, UTC RFC3339Nano) so the hash is stable across runs/machines. A `Genesis` constant anchors the first record · `kanz/services/audit/internal/chain/`
- [x] **AUDIT-01a** Append-only audit projection — new `kanz/services/audit/` (Go service mirroring the market-data layout: `cmd/audit`, `internal/{config,server,audit}`, `migrations/`). A bus consumer (`Projector.Handle`, matches `bus.EventHandler`) that materializes the backbone into a queryable `Store`. **Records EVERYTHING** (a dropped event is an audit gap), enriching recognized payloads via ordered classifiers: authz-decision (AUTH-01d `platform.authz.decision`), data-quality (OBSERVATION on `data` domain), command-outcome (FACT `.outcome`), command (COMMAND class), model-decision (OBSERVATION whose `payload_schema_ref` names DecisionLog), else generic. **Load-bearing classification call**: #5 keys on `payload_schema_ref`, NOT a blind decode — a `MetricObservation` is wire-compatible with `DecisionLog` (both string field 1), so decode-alone misreads a metric as a decision (caught by a test). **Two stores behind one interface**: `Memory` (reference/dev) + `Postgres` (durable, WORM via the migration), append-only and **idempotent on event_id** so at-least-once redelivery never duplicates or forks the chain; the Postgres append serializes seq+hash assignment under an advisory lock. Read API: query/get/lineage/verify/reports. The projection is durable-grade (store errors returned so the bus retries/DLQs) despite riding observation-class inputs · `kanz/services/audit/`

### Reliability — SLOs, Error Budgets, Chaos

- [x] **SRE-01e** Load/soak harness, **closes the SRE-01 epic** — new `kanz/test/load/` (`config.js`, `baseline.js`, `soak.js`, README). **k6 over JMeter/Locust**: single Go binary, scripts in JS, **native p99 thresholds that exit non-zero on breach** (the exact CI primitive LATENCY-01a needs) + open-model arrival-rate executors that measure *RPS sustained within budget*, not fixed-VU throughput — no infra to stand up (no dependency bloat). **One source for the budget**: `config.js` holds `P99_BUDGET_MS=500` (the SAME LATENCY-01/ORCH-01f number the CICD-01e canary p99 gate and the SRE-01a gateway query-latency SLO use — load testing must not invent its own target) + the request mix over the API-01c read surface (exposure/measures, ~1-in-5 scenario POST). **baseline.js** ramps `ramping-arrival-rate` through plateaus with `abortOnFail` thresholds → the RPS at the last passing stage IS baseline capacity; 429/503 tracked in a separate `requests_shed` counter so MT-01e quota/admission load-shed reads as a capacity ceiling, not a failure. **soak.js** holds `constant-arrival-rate` at ~60% capacity for hours with a dedicated `soak_req_duration` trend — catches p99 DRIFT / leaks a burst can't. `node --check` clean on all three · `kanz/test/load/`
- [x] **SRE-01d** Scheduled GameDay suite + runbooks-as-code — new `kanz/infra/chaos/gamedays/` (`gameday-workflow.yaml`, `schedule.yaml`, README) + `kanz/docs/runbooks/` (5 runbooks + template + README). **GameDay = supervised recurring drill, gated on verification**: a Chaos Mesh `Workflow` runs the four SRE-01c faults SERIALLY (not parallel — a failed hypothesis is then unambiguously attributable, and a real on-call can follow the runbooks), each fault followed by a `verify-*` Task that runs SRE-01c's `verify.sh` from the `chaos-verify` ConfigMap; **a violated invariant fails the Task and ABORTS the drill** (the GameDay proves resilience, doesn't just inject faults), with `Suspend` recovery dwells between blasts. A `Schedule` (Chaos Mesh) fires it weekly Wed 14:00 UTC (on-call staffed). **Single source of truth**: the Schedule embeds a WorkflowSpec inline (can't `$ref`), so its body is filled from `gameday-workflow.yaml` via a documented `yq` merge at apply time rather than duplicated. **Runbooks-as-code**: in-repo, source-controlled, YAML-frontmatter'd (`alert`/`severity`/`slo`/`gameday`) so alerts link via `runbook_url` and each names the SRE-01c experiment that rehearses it — the drill IS the runbook's live test, and a GameDay surprise is treated as a runbook bug. Five scenarios: error-budget burn, broker outage, broker partition→degraded-mode, inference latency→breaker, pod loss · `kanz/infra/chaos/gamedays/`, `kanz/docs/runbooks/`
- [x] **SRE-01c** Chaos experiments — new `kanz/infra/chaos/` (4 experiment manifests + `verify.sh` + README). **Chaos Mesh (CNCF) over Litmus/Gremlin**: its `PodChaos`/`NetworkChaos` CRDs cover every needed fault with no sidecar/app change and compose into the SRE-01d Workflow. **Each experiment carries a steady-state HYPOTHESIS, not just a fault** (annotation + during/after signals): `broker-kill` (pod-kill NATS/Kafka → EVT-17e DLQ catches terminal failures, consumers reconnect, lag drains, no event loss); `network-partition` (isolate risk-engine ⇄ brokers → RISK-11 staleness crosses 30s→5m thresholds → serves last-known-good tagged stale, no crash, recovers on heal — 8m duration past `DefaultDegradedThreshold`); `latency-injection` (1500ms delay into inference → PRED-07 circuit breaker opens → degraded fallback `circuit_open`, caller latency bounded); `pod-eviction` (pod-kill risk-engine + container-kill api-gateway → survivors serve, no SLO breach). Blast radius deliberately small (`mode: one`, single namespace; staging/GameDay only). **`verify.sh` is the assertion half**: queries the OBS-01 Prometheus for each hypothesis's signals + the invariant common to all — no `SLOFastBurn` firing (the disruption fits the 28d budget) — and a data-loss guard (`kanz_data_gap_missing_total`); POSIX-sh so it runs both at a dev shell and in the SRE-01d verify Task. Real labels/namespaces (`kanz-messaging` app: nats|kafka, `kanz-services` app: risk-engine) · `kanz/infra/chaos/`
- [x] **SRE-01b** SLO recording rules + multi-window burn-rate alerts — new `kanz/infra/observability/slo/{slo.recording.rules.yaml,slo.alerts.rules.yaml,slo_test.yaml}` (+README). **Compiles the SRE-01a catalog** into Prometheus rules over the existing OBS-01 RED series (no new instrumentation). Recording layer: per-SLO error budget (`slo:error_budget:ratio`, a labeled `vector()` literal) + the SLI ERROR ratio (`slo:sli_error:ratio_rate<window>{service,slo}`, `1 − good/total`) across the seven burn-rate windows (5m/30m/1h/2h/6h/1d/3d); freshness is a cheap `avg_over_time` of a once-per-cycle `slo:data_unfresh:ratio` base rule, NOT a costly `[w:1m]` subquery. **Alerts are GENERIC**: one rule per MWMBR burn pair (`SLOFastBurn` 14.4×/1h+5m, `SLOSlowBurn` 6×/6h+30m, `SLOBudgetBurnTicket` 3×/1d+2h, `SLOBudgetBurnChronic` 1×/3d+6h) fires for ANY `{service,slo}` exceeding `factor × budget` on both windows via `on(service,slo)` matching — adding a service needs only its recording rules, zero alert edits; the short window is the reset guard. Extends the DATA-09 alerts onto the same Alertmanager (`layer="slo"` routing, severity = page/ticket); a silent service (0/0=NaN) burns no budget. `slo_test.yaml` promtool unit test pins the wiring (5% 5xx ⇒ availability error 0.05 ⇒ fast-burn fires; healthy ⇒ silent). All YAML parses clean (promtool unavailable in env) · `kanz/infra/observability/slo/`
- [x] **SRE-01a** Per-service SLI/SLO catalog + error-budget policy — new `kanz/infra/observability/slo/slo.yaml` + `README.md`. **Definition-before-wiring**: `slo.yaml` is the machine-readable CONTRACT SRE-01b compiles into recording rules + multi-window burn-rate alerts — it scrapes/alerts nothing itself, the same "define the surface first" discipline as the DATA-08 metric contract and the CICD-01e canary template. **Six SLOs, every SLI bound to a series that already exists** (no new instrumentation): api-gateway availability (non-5xx, 99.9%) + query-latency (<500ms, 99%) over `kanz_gateway_*` (MT-01e); risk-engine recompute-success (99.5%) + recompute-latency (<500ms, 99%) over `kanz_risk_*` (OBS-01c); event-bus delivery-success (99.9%) over `kanz_bus_consume_total{result}`; market-data freshness (≤10s, 99%) over `kanz_data_staleness_lag_seconds` (DATA-02). **Load-bearing objective choices**: steady-state SLOs are deliberately tighter than the CICD-01e canary gate (same metric, two purposes — a deploy may run hotter than the 28d promise); latency thresholds REUSE the ORCH-01f 500ms budget rather than inventing one; **admission 503 is NOT carved out of gateway availability** (a deliberate load-shed is still client-experienced unavailability — it must burn budget and force capacity, the INFRA-01c premise); a **DLQ-routed message counts as a bus error** (the DLQ is the SRE-01c safety net, not a success). **Error-budget policy**: 28d rolling window, tiered posture (>50% normal velocity → <10% change-freeze → exhausted = freeze + mandatory postmortem feeding SRE-01d), with the standard MWMBR burn-rate pairs (14.4×/6× page, 3×/1× ticket) declared in `defaults.burn_rates` for SRE-01b to emit; `severity` routes via Alertmanager exactly as the DATA-09 rules. Per-subject/per-tenant budgets are deployment overrides, not catalog edits · `kanz/infra/observability/slo/`

### Model Validation, Explainability & Governance (MLOps)

- [x] **MLOPS-01h** MLOps governance integration tests, **closes the MLOPS-01 epic** — `kanz-py/tests/test_mlops_governance.py`. The four board-named behaviors as cross-component integration (real registry + Validator + ShadowExecutor + ShadowComparisonStore + Promoter + DriftTrigger + ExplainingModel wired together, not mocked): **(1) promotion-blocked-without-validation** — a challenger that out-performs on live shadow traffic is blocked by the Promoter until its MLOPS-01b validation is run and recorded, then promoted (proves validation is the gating factor end-to-end through executor→store→promoter); **(2) drift-triggers-revalidation** — a `data.feature.drift_detected` event drives the DriftTrigger to emit a RevalidationRequest for the affected model, and actuating it (re-run the Validator, refile the record) restores current validation; **(3) explanation-present-on-every-prediction** — an `ExplainingModel`-wrapped model carries a non-empty per-feature `explanation` on every NORMAL prediction; **(4) champion/challenger selection correctness** — two validated challengers run through the real `ShadowExecutor`→`ShadowComparisonStore`; the Promoter selects the lowest-RMSE one and demotes the rest to shadow. 4 integration tests. Full kanz-py suite 251 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/tests/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01g** Model cards + approval workflow + lineage — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/model_card.py` (+`__init__` exports). The model-risk record of *what a model is and who signed off on it*. **`LineageRecord`**: model→feature→training-data provenance (model_id, feature_set_ref, the consumed feature names, training-data snapshot refs) — the reproducibility spine. **`ModelCard`** (frozen): Google-style structured doc — owner, intended_use, limitations, the lineage, and the MLOPS-01a `ValidationRecord` evidence; a revision is a new card. **`ApprovalWorkflow`**: the sign-off state machine DRAFT → PENDING_REVIEW → APPROVED|REJECTED — `submit`/`approve`/`reject` with illegal transitions raising `ApprovalError`; **approval is gated on the card carrying a passing, non-expired validation** (no sign-off without evidence) and records approver + timestamp (injected clock); `reject` records who+why; resubmission allowed from REJECTED; an in-review/approved card can't be silently overwritten (a revision must not discard sign-off). Deliberately **NOT wired into `Registry.register`** — that's 01a's validation gate; approval is an additional human layer a deployment queries via `is_approved` (keeps the two gates separate). Tests (13): lineage/model_id match, DRAFT on register, full approve flow records approver+time, rejection+resumbit, approve gated on passing/non-expired/present validation, state-machine guards (approve-before-submit, unknown card, overwrite-approved), approver-required, unknown-status-none · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01f** Durable point-in-time feature store — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/featurestore/sqlite_store.py` (+`store.py` validation extracted, `__init__` export). The real store the PRED-11 boundary was landed to swap in, behind the **unchanged `FeatureStore` protocol** (zero caller changes). **`SqliteFeatureStore`** persists feature vectors keyed by `(subject_id, feature_set_ref, as_of_seconds, as_of_nanos)` (serialized proto BLOB) with point-in-time `get` (`WHERE as_of <= target ORDER BY as_of DESC LIMIT 1`, `(seconds,nanos)` lexicographic — never a later vector) and idempotent `put` (`INSERT OR REPLACE` ⇒ same as_of overwrites, new as_of extends history). File- or `:memory:`-backed. **Why SQLite, not Feast** (the board's "or equivalent"): Feast pulls a heavy dep tree + external online/offline infra, and the kanz-py suite deliberately runs without infra (only NATS/Kafka tests skip) — same dependency-bloat rule that kept `shap`/`gonum` out; mirrors the Go market-data plane's Memory+Postgres split (durable impl on a dependency-free engine). A Feast/warehouse/Redis-online impl plugs in behind the identical `get`/`put` contract; the point-in-time semantics proven here are what any such store must honour. **Async over a sync driver**: `sqlite3` calls dispatched via `asyncio.to_thread` (event loop never blocked), single shared connection (`check_same_thread=False`) guarded by a `threading.Lock` since the pool may run calls on different threads. Shared `_require_put_fields` extracted in store.py so both impls reject malformed materialisations identically. Tests (18): the full PRED-11 point-in-time contract re-run against the durable store (effective-not-latest, exact-inclusive, before-all-none, nanos ordering, out-of-order put, overwrite, subject/feature-set isolation, defensive copy both directions, the 3 validation guards) **plus durability across reopen** (write→close→reopen at the same file path still serves latest + point-in-time — the real-store difference) and protocol-shape. Full kanz-py suite 234 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/featurestore/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01e** Champion/challenger promotion — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/promotion.py` (+`__init__` exports). Turns PRED-10 shadow evaluation into an automated, gated promotion. **`ShadowComparisonStore` IS a PRED-10 `ShadowObserver`** (structural; `isinstance` passes the runtime_checkable protocol): on `observe` it accumulates per-`(primary_id, challenger_id)` behavioral metrics (observation count, mean abs divergence, opposite-sign disagreement rate) — everything computable without labels — and stashes the pair's predictions per subject. **`record_outcome(subject_id, actual)`** is the outcome-join: when a realized value arrives (a later consumer feeds it), it credits squared error to both models, so **live RMSE — real out-performance on production traffic — falls out** (the prediction pair alone can't say which model is better; that needs ground truth). `stats(primary_id, challenger_id) → ComparisonStats`. **`Promoter.evaluate(feature_set_ref)`** promotes the best challenger only when **all three gates** hold: (1) **soak** — `observations ≥ min_observations` AND `scored ≥ min_scored` (don't promote on a handful / on unjoined predictions); (2) **validation** — a current passing MLOPS-01a `ValidationRecord` (and `register(primary=True)` re-checks the gate on promotion); (3) **out-performance** — `challenger_rmse ≤ champion_rmse·(1−improvement_margin)` (a marginal win doesn't justify switch risk). On promotion it re-registers the winner primary and **demotes the old champion to shadow** (keeps running for comparison); returns a `PromotionDecision{promoted, previous_primary, reasons}` with a per-candidate explanation either way. Pure decision + one registry mutation, safe to call on a schedule. Tests (13): store-is-observer, behavioral + RMSE accumulation, disagreement rate, unknown-outcome no-op, happy-path promote + demote, best-of-several, each gate blocking independently (soak/scored/validation/out-performance/below-margin), no-primary, constructor guards. Full kanz-py suite 218 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01d** Prediction explainability — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/explain/{explainer.py,__init__.py}` (new `explain` package). Populates the `PredictionEnvelope.explanation` map (SHAP-like per-feature contributions, the field present since PRED-01) on the inference path. **`PermutationExplainer`**: model-agnostic Shapley-value estimation by **permutation sampling** (Štrumbelj–Kononenko) — for each random feature ordering, add features one at a time to a baseline coalition and credit each with the model-output change as it joins; average over orderings. **No `shap` dependency** (the dep-bloat rule) — only needs `model.predict`. Two properties hold *exactly* regardless of sample count (per-ordering credits telescope): **efficiency** — contributions sum to `pred(full) − pred(baseline)` (the proto's "sum need not equal `value`" = value minus the baseline prediction), and **determinism** — fixed `seed` + stable sorted-feature ordering ⇒ reproducible attribution for replay/audit (EVT-21d). Coalition predictions memoized per `frozenset` within one `explain` to bound the model-call count. **Baseline** = "feature absent": default drops the feature from the vector (the proto's omit-⇒-absent semantics); an optional `baseline` map supplies per-feature reference values for models that refuse partial input. **`ExplainingModel`** decorates any `Model` (satisfies the protocol, drops into the StreamingWorker/interactive path in place of the bare model): returns the inner prediction with `explanation` filled. Only **NORMAL** predictions are explained (attributing a degraded/zero fallback would mislead), and explanation is **best-effort** — if attribution raises (e.g. a model that rejects perturbed vectors) the prediction returns unexplained rather than failing inference. Tests (10): linear model attributes `w·x`, efficiency sum on a non-additive interaction model, seed determinism, empty-vector→empty, baseline shifts attribution, samples≥1 guard, decorator populates/skips-degraded/best-effort-on-failure/nil-guards. Full kanz-py suite 205 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/explain/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01c** Drift→revalidation loop — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/{drift_trigger.py,__init__.py}` (new `governance` package). **Closes the loop DATA-04 (drift) + MLOPS-01a/b (validation) left open**: when a feature a model depends on drifts, its recorded validation is no longer trustworthy, so the model must be revalidated. `DriftTrigger.handle(env, payload)` consumes the DATA-07 `data.feature.drift_detected` `DataQualityEvent` (same `EventHandler(env, payload)` shape as `StreamingWorker.handle`), acts only on the `drift` oneof variant (gap/staleness + sub-`min_severity` events are no-op acks), and fans out a `RevalidationRequest` per affected model. **Two pluggable boundaries** (the drift event names a single `feature`, but the registry indexes by `feature_set_ref` — there is no feature→model index in it): `ModelResolver`/`StaticModelResolver` (feature→model_ids, the deployment's feature catalog; the in-memory stand-in mirrors MODEL-01f's `StaticClassifier`) and `RevalidationSink`/`LoggingRevalidationSink` (where the signal goes — bus command, retrain job, MLOps ticket; mirrors PRED-05's `PredictionPublisher` — this package owns the trigger, not the delivery). `RevalidationRequest` carries the full drift evidence (model_id, feature, metric/score/threshold, severity, subject/partition_key, baseline_ref, reason, requested_at). **Per-(model, feature) cooldown** (default 1h, injected clock) collapses a drift detector's per-window re-emits into one signal — the revalidation-storm guard (same intent as the Go recompute debounce). `run(subscriber, group, subject=SUBJECT_DRIFT_DETECTED)` wires the subscription; `handle` is public so tests drive it without a subscriber. Tests (12): evidence-carrying request, fan-out to all affected, unused-feature no-op, gap-variant ignored, below-severity ignored, empty-feature skip, cooldown suppress-then-fire + per-(model,feature) keying, run subscribes to the drift subject, malformed-payload raises (DLQ path), nil-collaborator guards, logging-sink smoke. Full kanz-py suite 195 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/governance/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01b** Model validation suite — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/validation/{validator.py,__init__.py}`. **The evidence producer behind the MLOPS-01a gate**: scores a model over a labeled holdout, computes the model-risk metrics, decides pass/fail, and emits a `registry.ValidationRecord` via `ValidationResult.to_record(now, validity)`. `Validator(thresholds).validate(model, samples)` is async (awaits `model.predict` per `ValidationSample{feature_vector, actual}`, in time order). Three metric families per the board: **(1) holdout accuracy** — RMSE/MAE over (predicted `value`, realized `actual`); **(2) calibration** — Expected Calibration Error of the self-reported `confidence` vs realized correctness (`|value−actual| ≤ correct_tolerance`), binned (10 bins). This drives **PRED-02 §2.3**: `_recommend_threshold` returns the lowest confidence at which accuracy reaches `target_accuracy` for a calibrated model, and **1.0 (every prediction DEGRADED) when uncalibrated** — where uncalibrated = all-zero confidence (the proto's "no calibrated confidence" sentinel) **or** ECE > `max_calibration_error`; **(3) stability + bias** — bias = mean signed error (systematic over/under-prediction); stability = 2nd-half/1st-half RMSE ratio over the time-ordered holdout (catches error degrading across the window even when the aggregate looks fine). **Pass/fail**: `min_samples` (auto-fail below — no sign-off on a handful of points), optional `max_rmse`/`max_abs_bias`, `max_stability_ratio` gate primary-ness; **uncalibrated does NOT fail** the model (it may serve, just always-DEGRADED via threshold 1.0) — matching the degraded-contract intent. Pure metric core (`_evaluate`) split from the async scoring loop so it's testable without a model. Ties to 01a end-to-end: a passing result's record unlocks `register(primary=True)`, a failing one is recorded (audit) but the gate rejects. Does NOT own the validity-period policy (caller passes `validity`, default 90d) or persistence/scheduling (MLOPS-01c). Tests (12): hand-computable RMSE/MAE/bias, too-few-samples auto-fail, rmse/bias bound breaches, all-zero-confidence + overconfident-wrong both uncalibrated→1.0, well-calibrated→usable threshold, stable vs degrading stability, to_record validity window, and the 01a round-trip (pass unlocks / fail blocks primary). Full kanz-py suite 183 passed / 3 skipped · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/validation/`
- [x] **MLOPS-01a** Validation-status gate on the PRED-09 `Registry` — `kanz-py/kanz_inference/registry/registry.py` (+`__init__.py` exports). **SR 11-7 / model-risk gate: no model serves production without recorded, current validation.** New `ValidationRecord(model_id, validated_at, expires_at, passed=True, report_uri="")` (frozen) with `is_valid(now) = passed and expires_at > now` — a validation is *current*, not an open-ended sign-off (the revalidation-cadence the policy mandates). `Registry.record_validation(record)` stores latest-per-model (a revalidation supersedes; failing records kept as audit evidence but never satisfy the gate); `validation_for(model_id)` reads it back for health/audit + MLOPS-01e promotion. **`register(..., primary=True)` is gated**: requires a recorded, non-expired, passing record for `metadata.model_id` else raises **`ValidationError(ValueError)`** (subclasses ValueError so existing reject-as-ValueError callers keep working, while promotion code can catch the gate specifically). **The gate runs after input validation, before any mutation** (a blocked register leaves the registry untouched) and **re-runs on every primary register** incl. hot-reload (an expired validation blocks re-registration). **Shadows are exempt** (`primary=False`): a shadow is under evaluation, not serving — and shadow eval is what *produces* the validation evidence (PRED-10), so the gate bites exactly at promotion-to-primary. Clock injected via `Registry(clock=...)` so the expiry boundary is deterministically testable; defaults to UTC now. Existing PRED-09/PRED-10 tests updated to record a passing validation before primary registration (new `_register_primary` helpers); +12 gate tests: blocked-without/expired/failed validation, valid succeeds, shadow exempt, wrong-model's-validation doesn't count, latest-wins, ValidationError isa ValueError, injected-clock boundary, hot-reload re-check after expiry. ### Real Risk Analytics & Market Data Plane

- [x] **MODEL-01i** VaR verification suite, **closes the MODEL-01 epic** — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/backtest_test.go`. Three properties a real VaR plane must hold, each deterministic via a fixed seed (no flaky statistics): **(1) Kupiec backtest** — rolls a 250-day historical-VaR window across a 1300-day simulated N(0,σ) return path, counts one-day-ahead exceptions (realized loss > the VaR predicted from the *prior, out-of-sample* window), and asserts the Kupiec proportion-of-failures LR statistic < χ²₀.₉₅(1)=3.841 (calibration not rejected at 95%). Observed: 17/1050 exceptions (1.62%), LR=3.42 — a correctly-calibrated 99% VaR breaches ~1% of days. **(2) Independent-reference reconciliation** — both models vs the closed-form delta-normal `VaR=(z₉₉·σ−μ)·value` (z₉₉=2.32635) computed from the *same sample's* moments: Monte-Carlo (200k draws) within 2.5% (it simulates exactly that normal — estimation error isolated by reconciling to the sample moments, not the true ones), historical within 8% (resampling/empirical-quantile sampling error). **(3) Point-in-time / no-future-leakage** — end-to-end through `StoreReturnsProvider`→`Historical`: a late price correction (a past `observation_time` restated with a `knowledge_time` 15 days *after* the backtest horizon) is invisible to the as-of-day-5 read (which matches a store that never had the correction, to 1e-6 — the leakage assertion) and only moves the VaR once the horizon advances past its `knowledge_time` (`late > early`). Reuses the `historical_test.go` helpers (`asOf`/`money`/`portfolio`/`dval`); adds `sliceProvider` (settable window for the rolling backtest), `kupiecLR`, `meanStd`, `portfolioAsOf`. `go test ./internal/risk/...`/`vet` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/`
- [x] **MODEL-01h** Stress/scenario library extending RISK-09 — `kanz/internal/risk/scenario/library/library.go`, `api/v1/scenario.go` (`SectorShock`), `scenario/scenario.go` (dispatch + `WithClassifier`), `engine/engine.go` (`WithClassifier`). **New shock type `v1.SectorShock{Taxonomy,Code,Pct}`**: shocks every position whose instrument classifies into a sector, resolved point-in-time against the **MODEL-01f factor model** at apply time — carried as plain strings (not `factor.Sector`) because `api/v1` is the leaf package outsiders import and can't depend on `internal/.../factor` (would cycle `factor→compute→api/v1`). **Why sector-differentiated, not ParallelShift**: a crisis isn't uniform (2008 financials −55% vs staples −15%; COVID energy −55% vs staples −25%) — only a per-sector curve preserves the dispersion, the whole point of a named scenario. **Dispatch** adds a `case v1.SectorShock` to `applyShock`; `scenario.Evaluate` gained a variadic `...Option` (`WithClassifier`) so all 9 existing 3-arg call sites + the `api/v1` contract are untouched; **no classifier wired ⇒ SectorShock is a silent no-op** (the established unknown-shock/nil-model degradation), as are unclassified instruments. `EngineImpl.EvaluateScenario` passes its `engine.WithClassifier` classifier through. **Library** (`package library`): `GlobalFinancialCrisis2008()`/`Covid2020Crash()` return full 11-sector GICS curves (illustrative drawdown magnitudes — stress inputs, not the MODEL-01b backtest plane); `SectorCurve(taxonomy, map[code]pct)` is the hypothetical-curve-shift primitive the named scenarios are built from + the seam for custom curves (drops nil/zero points, stable sort by code for replay determinism); `Named(name)`/`Names()` is the name→shocks dispatch an API/CLI drives `EvaluateScenario` from (`GFC_2008`, `COVID_2020`). Builders (not cached slices) so each lookup hands back a fresh caller-owned slice. ctx for the classifier is `Background` in the dispatch (point-in-time rides on `asOf`; the static classifier ignores ctx) — same wired-but-off-in-production posture as 01f/01g (the engine builds no reference-data classifier yet). Tests: sector-targeted hit/miss, no-classifier no-op, unclassified skip, full-GICS-curve shape + ascending-code order, nil/zero drop, end-to-end GFC differentiation. `go build ./...`/`vet`/`test ./internal/risk/...` green · `kanz/internal/risk/scenario/library/`
- [x] **MODEL-01g** Real volatility into the RISK-08 uncertainty path — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/volatility.go`, `engine/{engine.go,recompute.go}`. **Closes the "fed nil" gap**: RISK-08 built + tested the propagation through measures (`uncertainty.go`) but every real position carried `MarketValueUncertainty=nil` (no vol model existed), so `Measure.UncertaintyAbs` was always nil in production. **`VolModel` is the reference mirror of the MODEL-01c returns provider**: `Volatility(ctx, instrumentID, asOf) (sigma, ok, err)` — one-sigma *fractional* return vol, point-in-time (asOf bounds the bitemporal knowledge horizon, no future leakage). **`ReturnsVolModel`** estimates it as the unbiased (n-1) sample stdev of the **same return series that feeds historical/MC VaR** (`compute.ReturnsProvider`), so the band and the VaR figure come off one consistent source; `DefaultVolMinObs=20` ⇒ `ok=false` below it (no band off a handful of points, vs. fabricating a noisy/zero one). **`PopulateUncertainty(ctx, p, vm)` is the engine-side enrichment seam** (mirror of `factor.Classifier` feeding exposure): given a Store.Snapshot *clone* it fills each position's band = `|MarketValue| × σ` (delta-normal 1σ), mutating the snapshot before compute — **the pure `MeasureFunc`/`ComputeMeasures` contract is untouched**, the band enters via the snapshot not the call site (same discipline as MODEL-01c's `BindReturns`). nil-as-absent preserved end-to-end: nil MV / insufficient history / model error ⇒ band left nil ⇒ RISK-08 reports "no uncertainty propagated", and a nil `vm` is a no-op (engine without a market-data plane behaves exactly as pre-01g). **Wired at all four snapshot boundaries**: `EngineImpl.{exposureSet,measureSet,EvaluateScenario}` (query path, via `engine.WithVolModel`) + `Recomputer.recompute` (async publish path, via `engine.WithRecomputeVolModel`) — two construction seams, one model, distinct names since both live in `package engine`. This also fills the VaR `UncertaintyAbs` band the 01d/01e measures left nil (Gross/Net/VaR/Delta all propagate from the per-position bands). Goes through float64 for the band (σ is a float estimate — acceptable per `decimalToFloat`'s note; money values stay Decimal). **Production-wiring still open** (the same integration task carried since 01c): the risk-engine builds no market-data store, so `main.go` constructs neither a `StoreReturnsProvider` nor the vol model — the option is wired-and-tested but not turned on until that store lands. Tests: sigma==independent sample-stdev, insufficient-history→nil, delta-normal band (incl. short |MV|), point-in-time asOf, end-to-end propagation through `GrossExposure`, nil-model no-op. `go build ./...`/`vet`/`test ./internal/risk/...` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/volatility.go`
- [x] **MODEL-01f** Factor model + instrument→sector lookup, completing the RISK-06 ExposureBySector deferral — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/factor/{factor.go,exposure.go}`, `compute/decimal.go` (3 exported money wrappers). **`Classifier` is the reference-data mirror of the MODEL-01c returns provider**: instrument→`Classification{Sector, AssetClass}`, point-in-time (`Classify(ctx, instrumentID, asOf)` — sector reclassifications are slowly-changing per the reference.v1 `as_of` SCD, so a backtest reads the mapping in effect then). `StaticClassifier` is the in-memory stand-in the engine loads from reference state;
