the production source is a reference-data store (the reference mirror of MODEL-01b, not yet built). `Sector{Taxonomy,Code}.Key()` builds the `"GICS:45"` bucket the domain uses, mirroring `reference.v1.SectorClassification`. **`SectorExposure(ctx, p, c)` completes RISK-06**: buckets base-currency positions by their instrument's sector (the RISK-07 same-currency convention — cross-currency needs FX), gross=Σ|MV| / net=ΣMV per bucket, **`UNCLASSIFIED` catch-all** for unknown/sectorless instruments so the SECTOR decomposition reconciles with the per-instrument total (pinned by `TestSectorExposure_Reconciles`). **`compute.ComputeExposure` signature left untouched** (the deferral note's promise): `factor.ComputeExposure` layers the SECTOR dimension onto compute's instrument+currency set when a `Classifier` is wired; absent one the engine calls the 2-dimension compute version. **Money arithmetic reused, not forked**: exported `compute.AddMoney/AbsMoney/ZeroMoney` thin wrappers keep the single point of truth for exponent-alignment/sign (decimal.go's own warning) rather than duplicating it in factor. **Factor-model scope**: `Classification` carries both factor dimensions (sector consumed now; `AssetClass` carried for the future reduced-rank factor covariance that could replace MODEL-01e's full empirical covariance — no covariance machinery built yet, not speculative beyond the reference field). Tests: GICS bucketing (gross/net incl. shorts), UNCLASSIFIED catch-all, reconciliation, non-base-currency skip, combined-set has all three dimensions. `go build ./...`/`vet`/`test` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/factor/`

- [x] **MODEL-01e** Monte-Carlo VaR + correlated factor-shock model — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/montecarlo.go` (+`Config.Draws/Seed` in `historical.go`). Second VaR model in `package varmodel`, registered identically (`MonteCarlo(cfg) compute.ReturnsMeasure` + `RegisterMonteCarlo` over `MeasureVaR99` — interchangeable with historical-sim). **The covariance IS the factor structure**: fits a multivariate-normal model to the provider's historical returns — per-instrument mean vector + full **Bessel-corrected sample covariance** estimated over the tail-aligned window — and draws `Draws` correlated synthetic scenarios via the **Cholesky factor** (`sim = μ + L·z`, z standard-normal), revalues per-scenario P&L = Σᵢ valueᵢ×simᵢ, and takes `−quantile(P&L, 1−conf)` (reusing the package `quantile`/decimal helpers from 01d). **Diversification falls out of the correlation**: a hedged (anti-correlated) book gets a lower VaR than a concentrated one — the property historical resampling can't model directly, pinned by `TestMonteCarlo_DiversificationReducesVaR`. **Deterministic given inputs + `Config.Seed`** (RNG re-seeded per call, default fixed) so a replayed recompute reproduces the same number (EVT-21d determinism — a random VaR would violate it). **Cholesky `covJitter` (1e-12)** floors a non-positive pivot so a singular/degenerate covariance (perfectly correlated/constant series) factorizes gracefully instead of NaN-ing. Inline linear algebra (mean/cov/Cholesky) — no gonum dep (avoid bloat). Same base-currency filter + insufficient-data→zero (need ≥2 scenarios for a covariance) as historical. Tests: determinism, sane magnitude (~value×σ×z₉₉), diversification, base-currency/insufficient guards, `RegisterMonteCarlo` override. Full factor model (reduced-rank sector/style factors) is MODEL-01f; this uses the full empirical covariance. `go build ./...`/`vet`/`test` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/`

- [x] **MODEL-01d** Historical-simulation VaR — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/historical.go` (new `package varmodel`; dir is `var` but the identifier can't be the keyword), `internal/risk/compute/correctness_test.go` (RISK-12 comment). **`varmodel.Historical(cfg) compute.ReturnsMeasure`** revalues the portfolio under each past return scenario: per-position P&L = Σᵢ valueᵢ × returnᵢ (base-currency positions only — the RISK-07 same-currency convention, cross-currency needs an FX layer), scenarios **tail-aligned to the shortest series** (providers return most-recent-N so the recent tail lines up; exact date-alignment is a MODEL-01b refinement once returns are date-tagged), then **VaR₉₉ = −quantile(P&L, 0.01)** floored at 0 via the empirical linear-interpolated quantile (R-7/NumPy). Insufficient data ⇒ zero-value measure (the MeasureFunc never-error contract; response layer marks degraded). **`varmodel.Register(ctx, r, provider, cfg)`** overrides `MeasureVaR99` with `compute.BindReturns(ctx, provider, Historical(cfg))` — the engine calls it once it has a price-store provider. **The placeholder `compute.VaR99` is NOT deleted**: `varmodel` imports `compute`, so `compute.DefaultRegistry` can't hold the provider-backed model (import cycle) — the placeholder stays as the honest **no-market-data fallback**, and Register overrides it only where a provider exists. **RISK-12 failure-as-documentation**: `TestProperty_VaR99IsOnePercentOfGross` stays green (it pins the surviving placeholder/fallback) but its comment now records that the property holds for the fallback ONLY; the real model's decoupling is pinned by the NEW `varmodel.TestHistorical_DecouplesFromGrossPlaceholder` (hist VaR 98 ≠ 1%×gross 10 on a hand-computable distribution). Tests: empirical-quantile arithmetic (hand-checked), decoupling, base-currency filter, insufficient-data→zero, and an end-to-end Memory-store→`StoreReturnsProvider`→`Register`→`ComputeMeasures` proving the served VaR99 is data-driven, not the placeholder. Local `decimal⇄float` bridge (compute's is package-private). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/risk/... ./internal/marketdata/...` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/var/`

- [x] **MODEL-01c** Historical-returns provider + VaR closure-injection seam — `kanz/internal/risk/compute/marketdata.go`. **The deliverable is the SEAM, not the VaR math** (that's 01d): `BindReturns(ctx, provider, ReturnsMeasure) MeasureFunc` closes a provider-aware computation over a `ReturnsProvider` + a captured ctx, yielding a plain `func(*domain.Portfolio) v1.Measure` the RISK-07 `Registry` stores and `ComputeMeasures` runs **unchanged** — the data dependency enters by closure, the api/v1 contract + MeasureSet shape + every caller stay exactly as RISK-07 designed. ctx is captured at bind time (the recomputer is debounced/async, so the pure signature carries no ctx of its own — same reasoning as the MT-01b `RISK_ENGINE_TENANT` fallback). **`ReturnsProvider`** (consumer-defined interface, idiomatic Go) `Returns(ctx, instrumentID, asOf, window) ([]float64, error)` — returns `double` (a return is a derived statistic, the EVT-14/MODEL-01a rationale), empty (not error) on insufficient history so the VaR caller degrades per the MeasureFunc "never error" contract. **`StoreReturnsProvider`** is the concrete historical-returns provider over the MODEL-01b `store.Store`: reads the close series **point-in-time-correct** (`store.Query{End: asOf, AsOf: asOf}` bounds BOTH observation_time and the bitemporal knowledge horizon at p.AsOf()), keeps the last window+1 prices, differences them into simple/log returns (`ReturnMethod`, default log, default 250-day lookback). **Deliberately registers NOTHING into `DefaultRegistry`** — VaR99 stays the 1%×gross placeholder, so the RISK-12 property test (`VaR99/Gross=0.01`) still passes; 01d writes the `ReturnsMeasure` (historical-sim) and `Register`s `BindReturns(...)` over it, which is where RISK-12 intentionally breaks (failure-as-documentation). Reuses the package-private `decimalToFloat`. Tests: simple/log returns + zero-guard, point-in-time no-future-leakage (a day-9 restatement invisible to an as-of-day-4 read), window-tail truncation, insufficient-history→empty, and the `BindReturns` seam threading provider + captured ctx into the bound `MeasureFunc`. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/risk/... ./internal/marketdata/...` green · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/marketdata.go`

- [x] **MODEL-01b** Market-data ingestion service + point-in-time store — `kanz/internal/marketdata/{store/,ingest.go}`, `kanz/services/market-data/`, `kanz-schemas/proto/reference/v1/price_history.proto` (+`PRICE_KIND_LAST`). **The `store.Store` is bitemporal — the load-bearing MODEL-01i decision**: every `Observation` carries `ObservationTime` (the time a price applies to) AND `KnowledgeTime` (when Kanz learned it); a read scoped to `Query.AsOf` sees only `KnowledgeTime <= AsOf` and, per `ObservationTime`, the latest such version — so a backtest as-of T reproduces exactly what was knowable at T and a late correction never leaks backward (proven by `TestMemory_NoFutureLeakage`). **`Store` is a Go-native record layer over the MODEL-01a proto** (`Observation` ≈ `reference.v1.PriceObservation`), the same two-layer split as `persist.PortfolioRecord`/`domain.Portfolio` — so the service does NOT depend on the not-yet-generated `reference.v1` Go SDK; price stays exact `commonpb.Decimal`. **Memory + Postgres parity** (the EVT-16a schema-registry pattern): `Memory` is the executable reference implementing the full point-in-time contract (restatement collapse, window, kind filter, `LatestAsOf` bounding both axes); `Postgres` mirrors it in SQL via `DISTINCT ON (observation_time, kind) ORDER BY knowledge_time DESC` and an idempotent `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` keyed on the full bitemporal identity. **Timescale over ClickHouse** (the board offered both): Timescale is a Postgres extension so it rides the existing pgx stack with zero new drivers (the `0001_price_history.sql` migration makes a hypertable when the extension is present, plain indexed table otherwise — identical code); **not tenant-scoped** (price history is universal market fact, the schema-registry call). **Ingestion mirrors the RISK-04/05 split**: `marketdata.Ingestor` is a thin `bus.EventHandler` translating the market oneof → mark (**Bar→CLOSE, Trade→LAST, Quote→MID** with an exact `(bid+ask)/2`), stamping `knowledge_time` from envelope `ingestion_time`; an untranslatable event errors → DLQ (market-data loss must be loud). **Service** (`services/market-data/`) is the risk-engine shape: env config, `internal/server` probes, OTel/metrics, NATS-spine subscribe fan-out over `MARKET_DATA_SUBJECTS` (default the `market.>` wildcard), Postgres store when `MARKET_DATA_DATABASE_URL` set else in-memory. `PRICE_KIND_LAST` added to the 01a proto (additive, pre-release) so a trade has an honest mark. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/marketdata/...` green; `buf lint`/`build` clean. **Batch carriage** (`MarketDataBatch`) left as a documented seam (`TranslateBatch` exists; no batching producer / event_type discriminator yet) · `kanz/internal/marketdata/`, `kanz/services/market-data/`

- [x] **MODEL-01a** Reference + market-history schemas — `kanz-schemas/proto/reference/v1/{instrument.proto,price_history.proto}`, `kanz-schemas/CODEOWNERS`, **begins the MODEL-01 epic**. New **payload package `reference.v1`, deliberately NOT a streaming domain** (carries no new `{domain}.{entity}.{event_type}` name — like `common.v1`/`domain.v1`, the proto package ≠ a subject-taxonomy domain; how it's ingested/stored is MODEL-01b's concern, so no architecture-review domain addition here). **Split into two focused files** (the multi-file convention of `common.v1`) even though the board named only `instrument.proto`. **`InstrumentReference`** keys on the same canonical `instrument_id` as `market.v1` + the envelope `partition_key` (a price tick joins to its reference record with no translation); carries `InstrumentIdentifiers` (ISIN/CUSIP/SEDOL/FIGI/RIC/Bloomberg — a closed, meaningful struct, NOT an opaque map), `AssetClass`/`SectorClassification`/`currency_code`/`issuer_id` which **map field-for-field onto the existing `domain.v1.ExposureDimension`** (ASSET_CLASS/SECTOR/CURRENCY/ISSUER) so MODEL-01f's instrument→sector lookup and the RISK-06 `ExposureBySector` deferral are fed from here; `SectorClassification` is structured `{taxonomy,code}` so the `"GICS:45"` exposure bucket is built from the two parts without parsing. **Slowly-changing-dimension `as_of`** versions a reference snapshot (point-in-time reads pick latest `as_of <= query_time` — past backtests see the classification in effect then). **Price/return history is bitemporal** (`observation_time` = the time the value applies to, `knowledge_time` = when it became known) — the load-bearing decision for MODEL-01i point-in-time correctness / no-future-leakage: a backtest as-of T filters `knowledge_time <= T` so a late correction never leaks. **Prices are exact `common.v1.Decimal`** (the money/price/size rule); **returns are `double`** — a return is a dimensionless derived statistic feeding the VaR models, the same EVT-14 rationale that allows `double` for metrics/drift scores. `PriceHistory`/`ReturnSeries` are per-instrument batch carriers (the `market.v1.MarketDataBatch` analogue — shared `instrument_id` == `partition_key`, ordered). CODEOWNERS `/proto/reference/` → `@kanz-eng/market-data @kanz-eng/quant-ml @kanz-eng/architecture`. `buf lint` clean (only pre-existing inference RPC-naming warnings remain) + `buf build` OK; additive new package can't trip the `breaking: FILE` baseline · `kanz-schemas/proto/reference/v1/`

### Multi-Tenancy Foundation

- [x] **MT-01g** Cross-tenant isolation contract tree — `kanz/test/contract/tenancy/isolation_test.go`, **closes the MT-01 epic**. The capstone proof the tenant boundary holds end-to-end: asserts the COMPOSITION of the per-layer mechanisms as one suite, so a regression in any layer that opens a cross-tenant path surfaces here. **Four layers**: (1) **BUS** (pure) — a live FACT is always stamped with its producing tenant; live `Validate` rejects an untenanted event (can't sit on a real tenant's stream) while replay tolerates it; a handler-derived publish inherits the inbound tenant from ctx (proven by giving the producer a DIFFERENT config tenant — ctx must win, not the fallback); (2) **REPLAY** (pure) — legacy untenanted replay maps to `SystemTenant` (__system__), never a real tenant; (3) **API** (pure) — `auth.PolicyAuthorizer` denies a cross-tenant query even for admin `"*"` (tenant isolation beats RBAC), same-tenant allowed; (4) **STORE/RLS** (integration, gated on `TEST_POSTGRES_URL`, skips under a superuser role) — A's rows invisible to B via `Load`/`LoadAll`, and a raw cross-tenant `INSERT` (the RLS-escape) is rejected by `WITH CHECK`. External `_test` package on the public bus/auth/persist surface (frames bodies via `envelopepb.EventFrame`, fakes `bus.Client`/`Subscriber`); pure layers every `go test`, store gated. `go test ./test/contract/tenancy/...` + vet green · `kanz/test/contract/tenancy/`
- [x] **MT-01f** Tenant lifecycle automation — `kanz/infra/tenancy/{tenantctl.sh,tenant.example.env,onboard-job.yaml,README.md}`. **ONE idempotent, dependency-ordered workflow** fans a single tenant definition across every isolation boundary: (1) identity — `tenant-<name>` SPIFFE-enabled namespace + ServiceAccounts ⇒ SEC-01a SVIDs; (2) broker — NATS account (MT-01c) + per-SA Kafka `{tenant}.` prefixed topics & PREFIXED ACLs (renders the MT-01c kafka-tenant Job); (3) state — a Postgres non-superuser login role granted on the RLS state tables (MT-01d needs no per-tenant DDL, only the role so FORCE RLS applies under it); (4) quota — merges the tenant's budget into the gateway overrides ConfigMap (MT-01e). **Order = the dependency chain** (the SVID is the credential the broker mapping + ACL principal + DB role all key on); **offboard reverses it** (quota→state→broker→identity, ACL-revoke before topic-delete so no orphan grant). **Schema registry deliberately excluded** (platform-global, MT-01d). Idempotent throughout (`apply`/`--dry-run|apply`/`IF NOT EXISTS`/`--if-not-exists`); **tenant rows retained on offboard unless `PURGE_ROWS=1`** (deleting client data is a one-way door). Runs locally or in-cluster (`kanz-tenancy` ns + scoped ClusterRole + a Job mounting `tenantctl.sh` from a `--from-file` ConfigMap). Honest edges: NATS dynamic account needs the JWT resolver+operator (else prints the static-append step); DB role no-ops without `ADMIN_DATABASE_URL`. bash `-n` clean; YAML parses · `kanz/infra/tenancy/`
- [x] **MT-01e** Per-tenant quotas + admission control + `tenant` metric label — `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/middleware/{quota,metrics}.go`, `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/config/config.go`, `kanz/services/api-gateway/cmd/api-gateway/main.go`, `kanz/pkg/observability/tenant.go`. **Two complementary noisy-neighbor guards**: the API-01d token bucket smooths request RATE (429), new **admission control caps CONCURRENCY** via a per-tenant in-flight semaphore (`MaxInFlight` → 503, a transient-capacity signal distinct from 429). Both key on the authenticated tenant so isolation holds. **Per-tenant quotas as policy-as-data**: `TenantLimits{Default, Overrides}` — default from env, per-tenant overrides from a JSON file (`API_GATEWAY_QUOTAS_FILE`, `DisallowUnknownFields`), same ConfigMap-mounted shape as AUTH-01b's `risk-authz.json`. Old `RateLimit` kept for back-compat; new `Quota(limits, obs)` in the chain (Version→Signing→Auth→**Measure→Quota**→Idempotency, Measure outside Quota so `requests_total` counts the 429/503 with their codes); token-bucket refill factored to `bucket.take`. **Tenant metric label (extends OBS-01)**: `observability.TenantLabel`+`NormalizeTenant` set ONE platform label convention; gateway is the first adopter — every `kanz_gateway_*` series carries `tenant` (`requests_total{tenant,code}`, `request_duration_seconds`, `rate_limited_total`, `admission_rejected_total`, `inflight_requests`). `GatewayMetrics` implements `QuotaObserver`, nil-safe like `BusMetrics`. **Deliberate scope**: NOT retrofitted onto `kanz_bus_*`/`kanz_data_*`/`kanz_risk_*` (cardinality + OBS-01e/CICD-01e contract churn; per-tenant attribution belongs at the admission edge); `"anonymous"` (≠`__system__`) labels an unauthenticated caller. **Inference admission** (other half named in MT-01e) is a kanz-py follow-up. Tests: per-tenant override isolation, admission concurrency (503 + other-tenant-unaffected), observer balanced-inflight, metrics series/label/codes, nil-safety; `go test ./services/api-gateway/...` + observability green · `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/middleware/`, `kanz/pkg/observability/tenant.go`
- [x] **MT-01d** State isolation via Postgres RLS — `kanz/services/risk-engine/migrations/0002_tenant_rls.sql`, `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/{store,postgres,postgres_test}.go`, `kanz/services/risk-engine/cmd/risk-engine/main.go`, `kanz/services/schema-registry/migrations/0001_init.sql` (note). **The tenant boundary becomes a property of the database, not a query convention** — a read missing a `WHERE tenant_id =` clause still can't leak across tenants. All stateful tables gain `tenant_id` (PKs recomposed to `(tenant_id, …)` so the same `portfolio_id` coexists per-tenant) + **`ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`** + a `tenant_isolation` policy `USING/WITH CHECK (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true))`. **`FORCE` is load-bearing** (subjects the table owner — the engine's role — to the policy, so the engine connects as a **non-superuser**; superusers bypass RLS). **Deny-by-default mirrors AUTH-01b**: GUC unset ⇒ `current_setting(…,true)` NULL ⇒ predicate NULL ⇒ nothing visible/writable (fail-closed). **The GUC = the authenticated session's tenant** = the engine's configured `cfg.Tenant` (MT-01b), set on every pooled connection via `pgxpool.AfterConnect → set_config('app.tenant_id', tenant, false)`; the persist INSERTs also stamp `tenant_id` explicitly via `current_setting(...)`. Single-tenant-per-deployment now (so `LoadAll`/`Load`-by-id are correctly tenant-scoped by RLS); multi-tenant-in-one-process would switch to per-tx `SET LOCAL` (documented future). `PortfolioRecord` gains `TenantID` (read back; DB is authority). **Schema registry deliberately NOT tenanted** (note in its 0001 migration): schemas are universal platform contracts — partitioning per-tenant would fork the contract and break cross-tenant `payload_schema_ref` resolution. Test `TestPostgres_RLSTenantIsolation` proves cross-tenant invisibility + per-tenant id coexistence + tenant stamping (gated on `TEST_POSTGRES_URL`, skips under a superuser role); build + vet + `go test ./internal/risk/state/persist/...` green · `kanz/services/risk-engine/migrations/0002_tenant_rls.sql`, `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/`
- [x] **MT-01c** Broker-level tenant isolation — `kanz/infra/nats/{tenancy.yaml,nats.yaml}`, `kanz/infra/kafka/{tenancy.yaml,kafka.yaml}`, `kanz-schemas/docs/subject-taxonomy.md` §6. **Tenant is an isolation boundary, NOT part of the logical name** — the 3-segment `{domain}.{entity}.{event_type}` contract is untouched; tenant manifests per-broker because their native isolation primitives differ (the per-broker-native stance EVT-08/09 took). **NATS = account-per-tenant (the load-bearing choice)**: an account's subjects are physically invisible to other accounts, so `tls.verify_and_map` binds the client SVID's URI SAN → a per-tenant user → account and **isolation is enforced by which account a connection lands in, not the subject string** — the bus keeps publishing the unchanged 3-segment subject (subject prefixing inside a shared account gives no real isolation). `tenancy.yaml` ships `SYS` + `__system__` (platform/legacy) + an `acme` tenant template, each tenant account JetStream-quota'd (noisy-neighbor bound; MT-01e tightens); `nats.yaml` gains `verify_and_map` + an `include "tenants.conf"`. **Kafka = topic-prefix `{tenant}.{domain}.{entity}` + PREFIXED ACL**: Kafka has no account concept so the topic name is the only ACL handle — the KRaft `StandardAuthorizer` runs **deny-by-default** (`allow.everyone.if.no.acl.found=false`, mirroring AUTH-01b) and each tenant SVID principal gets RW only on its `{tenant}.` prefix + group prefix. **`__system__` reconciles with MT-01a**: pre-tenancy/untenanted events keep the **un-prefixed legacy topics** (no rename, no broken consumers; only new tenants get prefixed topics). **Two seam-ahead deps**: (1) the Kafka producer must prepend `{tenant}.` from envelope `tenant_id` (empty/`__system__` → no prefix) — the bus-wiring follow-up (NATS needs none, account-scoped); (2) SVID certs have empty subject DNs (identity is the URI SAN), so the broker needs a SPIFFE-aware `principal.builder.class` (`io.kanz.kafka.SpiffePrincipalBuilder`, configured not shipped) to surface `User:spiffe://…` as the ACL principal — the SEC-01 companion. PLAINTEXT:9092 `ANONYMOUS` (inter-broker + bootstrap) stays super-user until inter-broker SSL lands. Static accounts now (append+reload); dynamic JWT account-resolver onboarding is the MT-01f path · `kanz/infra/{nats,kafka}/tenancy.yaml`
- [x] **MT-01a/b** Envelope `tenant_id` + tenant propagation through the bus — `kanz-schemas/proto/envelope/v1/envelope.proto` (field 20), `kanz/pkg/bus/{validate,producer,consumer,context}.go`, `kanz/services/risk-engine/{internal/config,cmd}`, **begins the MT epic**. **`tenant_id` added as Envelope field 20, envelope_version bumped 1→2 per envelope-policy §5/§6** (additive-only; it passes both §2 tests — universality + payload-blind tooling, since broker/RLS/metrics isolation all key on it without deserializing). **Asymmetric validation is the load-bearing rule (MT-01a)**: `Validate` (live path) REQUIRES `tenant_id`, but `ValidateReplay` does NOT — pre-tenancy events read from old durable logs carry the empty string, and the consumer maps those to the reserved `SystemTenant` (`__system__`) rather than rejecting, preserving EVT-20 replay determinism; the requirement lives in `Validate` (not the shared `validateEnvelopeFields`) precisely so the replay path stays tolerant. **Propagation (MT-01b) mirrors lineage (EVT-17c)**: Producer stamps with precedence **Event.TenantID > ctx (`TenantIDFromContext`) > `ProducerConfig.Tenant`**; Consumer stashes the inbound tenant onto ctx (empty→`SystemTenant`) so a derived publish inside a handler inherits it. **The risk-engine recomputer is the wrinkle**: it publishes derived FACTs from a background, debounced ctx (not the triggering handler's), so ctx-propagation can't carry the tenant there — it stamps a configured fallback `RISK_ENGINE_TENANT` (default `__system__`); **true per-portfolio tenant stamping (engine carrying portfolio→tenant in state) is a documented MT-01d follow-up** — the codebase's "single-X-now, multi-later bootstrap" pattern. **gen/go is gitignored (EVT-15a), so only the .proto is committed**; the Go SDK was regenerated locally to build/test (buf.exe is Application-Control-blocked on this box, so a throwaway go-built driver drove the local `protoc-gen-go` to regen just envelope.pb.go). Tests: bus-layer `tenant_test.go` (precedence + consumer propagation + replay→`__system__`), `Validate` requires-tenant / `ValidateReplay`-tolerant cases, envelope_version=2 assertion, and ~14 producer/contract test sites updated to supply a tenant. Full suite green · `kanz/pkg/bus/`, `kanz-schemas/proto/envelope/v1/envelope.proto`

### AuthN / AuthZ — RBAC + ABAC

- [x] **AUTH-01e** Authorization contract test suite — rewrote `kanz/pkg/auth/authz_test.go` into the AUTH-01 contract suite, **closes the epic**. **One exhaustive table (`authMatrix`) drives TWO tests** (the load-bearing structure): `TestPolicyAuthorize_Matrix` pins the deny-by-default decision model over role×action×scope — RBAC (reader→read, analyst→read+scenario, admin→`*`, no-role/unknown-role deny, multi-role uses the granting one), **tenant isolation beating RBAC** (admin cross-tenant DENIED), and **portfolio scope beating RBAC** (scoped admin out-of-scope DENIED) — and `TestAuthLayerComposes` replays the SAME matrix through the `AuditedAuthorizer` to prove (a) audit wrapping is **verdict-transparent** (the audited decision always equals the raw one) and (b) each decision records exactly one `DecisionLog` whose recorded verdict matches — the 01b↔01d composition contract. **`TestRealPolicyBundle` is the drift guard**: it loads the ACTUAL shipped `infra/security/policies/risk-authz.json` (not an inline fixture) through the real authorizer and asserts the documented grants — so editing the bundle to grant more/less breaks the build (the PERS-01e "read the real artifact" discipline, here the policy file instead of the migration SQL). `TestForgedIssuerRejection` pins the 01c property (self / forged / ungranted-vs-granted delegation) prominently in the epic suite; deny-by-default edge cases (nil principal, empty action even for admin, principal-without-tenant) and policy-load errors (empty roles, unknown field via `DisallowUnknownFields`, malformed JSON) get dedicated tests. All pure, run on every `go test`; 22 top-level tests across the `auth` package now green (authn OIDC + authz + issuer + audit) · `kanz/pkg/auth/authz_test.go`
- [x] **AUTH-01d** Authz decision logging — new `kanz/pkg/auth/audit.go` (+ tests). **`AuditedAuthorizer` decorates any `Authorizer` so every allow AND deny is recorded as an `observation.v1.DecisionLog`** — the feed AUDIT-01's append-only projection materializes. **Reuses `DecisionLog` (OBSERVATION) over a bespoke authz FACT (the deliberate schema choice)**: it already models "what made a decision / summary / structured factors", which is exactly an authz verdict — no new proto, no kanz-schemas churn. `BuildDecisionLog` is the single Request+Decision→DecisionLog mapping (decider, a human summary, and `attributes`: decision/action/principal.subject+tenant/resource.type+id+tenant/reason) — exported so the bus-backed recorder and tests share one mapping (the RISK-10 "one mapping, not two" rule). **auth stays decoupled from the bus (AUTH-01c stance)**: `audit.go` defines the `DecisionRecorder` seam + ships `SlogRecorder` (a *real* sink — kanz logs are stdout JSON shipped by the platform, OBS-01a — not a stub); the bus-backed recorder that publishes the DecisionLog as an OBSERVATION event lives in the wiring layer (auth never imports transport). **Auditing is best-effort by design**: a recorder failure is logged loudly but does NOT fail the request (an audit-sink outage must not become a service outage; fail-closed-on-allow noted as a high-compliance option), and `Record` SHOULD be non-blocking since `Authorize` is on the request hot path. **Durability note**: general observations are lossy-tolerant (event-class-rules §4), so the recommendation mirrors the FACT-grade `DataQualityEvent` exception — publish on a durable observation subject and treat AUDIT-01's projection as the system of record. Exported envelope constants (`AuthzDecisionDomain`/`AuthzDecisionEventType`) so AUDIT-01 can select authz decisions off the stream. Tests (mapping for allow/deny/anonymous, records-both, recorder-error-doesn't-fail-decision, nil-recorder, SlogRecorder) run every `go test` · `kanz/pkg/auth/audit.go`
- [x] **AUTH-01c** Forged-issuer guard — new `kanz/pkg/bus/command.go` + `kanz/pkg/auth/issuer.go` (+ tests). **Binds a COMMAND's `command.v1.CommandMetadata.issuer` to the authenticated `Principal` at publish; a mismatch is rejected before the command reaches the broker.** **The bus stays decoupled from the auth layer (the load-bearing decision, the SEC-01c precedent where bus took a ready `*tls.Config` not a SPIFFE source)**: `bus` exposes an injectable `CommandIssuerFunc func(ctx, issuer) error` on `ProducerConfig.VerifyCommandIssuer` and owns only the *extraction* + enforcement plumbing; `auth.VerifyCommandIssuer` owns the identity *rule* and satisfies that signature exactly, so wiring is one assignment (`cfg.VerifyCommandIssuer = auth.VerifyCommandIssuer`). **Issuer extraction is generic + payload-blind (EVT-17b preserved)**: `CommandMetadata` is field 1 of every concrete command (command.v1), so the producer reads field 1's bytes via `protowire` and unmarshals just the `CommandMetadata` — no link to any concrete command schema, works across all command types. **The binding rule (`auth.VerifyIssuer`)**: the issuer wire form is `{type}:{id}`; a principal may issue (a) as ITSELF — any issuer whose id-part == `Principal.Subject`, any type prefix (the id is the authenticated identity, what can't be forged), or (b) as a DELEGATED identity listed verbatim in its `issuers` claim (e.g. a strategy it operates). Anything else is `ErrForgedIssuer`; deny-by-default on nil-principal/empty-issuer. **Runs only when a verifier is configured** (backward compatible; non-COMMAND events never invoke it) and a COMMAND with no issuer is `ErrMissingCommandIssuer` (issuer is Required per event-class-rules §2). **Shipped seam-ahead-of-producer**: there is no Go command publisher yet, so the guard is wired into the Producer but its consumer (a real command-issuing path) lands later — the gateway carrying the full `auth.Principal` on the publish ctx is the same integration dep AUTH-01b's wiring needs. Tests: generic extraction (synthetic `dynamicpb` command embedding CommandMetadata at field 1, no-metadata, malformed), producer guard (allowed publishes / forged rejected-and-not-sent / missing-issuer / non-COMMAND-skips), and the `auth` rule matrix (self any-type, forged-id, delegated allow/deny, empty, nil, ctx-principal) — all on every `go test` · `kanz/pkg/bus/command.go`, `kanz/pkg/auth/issuer.go`
- [x] **AUTH-01b** Deny-by-default authorization layer — new `kanz/pkg/auth/authz.go` (+ tests) + `kanz/infra/security/policies/` (`risk-authz.json`, `configmap.yaml`, README). **`Authorizer` interface → `Decision{Allow, Reason}` over a `Request{Principal, Action, Resource}`**, deny-by-default: every gate must pass to allow. **`PolicyAuthorizer` evaluates RBAC role→action grants from a declarative bundle PLUS the ABAC tenant + portfolio scope, checked ISOLATION-FIRST** — tenant and portfolio scope run before the RBAC grant so a role (even the `"*"` admin wildcard) can never widen access beyond the caller's tenant or portfolio allow-list. **Tenant isolation is a CODE invariant, NOT policy data (the load-bearing decision)**: cross-tenant access is an isolation boundary (MT-01), so it must not be expressible as a grant an operator could loosen by editing the bundle — the bundle holds only role→action RBAC; tenant/portfolio scope live in `Authorize`. **Portfolio scope (ABAC)** reads a `portfolios` claim allow-list off the Principal (absent ⇒ any portfolio within the tenant). **Hand-rolled over embedding OPA/Cedar (deliberate, the task named them as the *kind* of engine)**: the decision is structurally narrow (tenant isolation + role→action + portfolio scope), so the Rego/Cedar runtime would be dependency bloat — same call as the gateway's hand-rolled rate limiter + REST transcoding; the `Authorizer` **interface is the seam** a Rego/Cedar engine slots behind if policy ever outgrows this. **Policy bundle is JSON, not Rego/YAML** — stdlib `encoding/json` (`DisallowUnknownFields` so a typo'd field fails loudly), no OPA runtime and no YAML dep; loaded via `LoadPolicyFile` (the ConfigMap-mounted deployment shape, config-as-data like the SEC-01d secret files). `Decision.Reason` is audit-facing — what AUTH-01d will log for every allow/deny. 5 test groups (role×action matrix, deny-by-default on nil-principal/empty-action/unknown-action, tenant isolation incl. admin-can't-cross-tenant + no-tenant-denied, portfolio scope in/out/unrestricted, policy-load errors) run every `go test`. **Shipped seam-ahead-of-consumer**: full wiring into the gateway request path is the integration follow-up (the gateway middleware carries a lossy local principal that drops Claims, so ABAC needs it to carry the canonical `auth.Principal` first) · `kanz/pkg/auth/authz.go`, `kanz/infra/security/policies/`
- [x] **AUTH-01a** OIDC/JWKS authenticator + canonical `Principal` — new `kanz/pkg/auth/` (`principal.go`, `oidc.go` + tests), **begins the AuthN/AuthZ epic** and replaces the API-01d bundled HS256 stand-in with real OIDC behind the SAME `Authenticator` seam. **`auth.Principal` is now the platform-wide identity** (subject/tenant/roles + full custom `Claims` map) carried on ctx via `WithPrincipal`/`PrincipalFromContext`; the gateway's local minimal principal is mapped from it by a thin adapter so the swap touched only the constructor (the promise the API-01d comment made). **`OIDCAuthenticator` verifies asymmetric JWS against JWKS the provider publishes** — `kid`-keyed key lookup, **lazy JWKS (re)fetch on a cache miss so a rotated-in key needs no restart, rate-limited by `MinRefreshInterval` (1m) so a stream of bogus kids can't hammer the provider**; OIDC discovery (`/.well-known/openid-configuration`) resolves `jwks_uri` when not configured, and the discovered `issuer` must equal the configured one. **The allow-list is asymmetric-only (RS/ES/PS) ON PURPOSE — HS\* is refused to close the alg-confusion attack** (a token HMAC-signed with the public key as the shared secret would otherwise verify); the HS256 validator survives only as the gateway's dev-mode fallback. **Network is deferred to first use, not the constructor** (lazy-connect, mirroring the gateway's upstream mTLS dial) so a momentarily-down IdP doesn't fail startup; construction only validates issuer/audience. **Bad token collapses to `ErrUnauthenticated`** (the boundary leaks no reason) while a provider/transport failure is returned distinctly so a future caller can tell 401 from 503 — at the gateway both currently map to 401 (a 503-on-IdP-outage is a noted follow-up, bounded by the ctx-less middleware seam). Roles claim normalizes both a JSON array and a space-delimited `scope` string; tenant/roles claim names are configurable. **`go-jose/v4` promoted from indirect (already pulled in by go-spiffe) to a direct dep — zero new module, satisfies CLAUDE.md dep-hygiene.** Wired into the gateway: `API_GATEWAY_OIDC_ISSUER` set ⇒ OIDC (precedence), else `API_GATEWAY_JWT_SECRET` ⇒ dev HS256, else auth DISABLED. 9 white-box tests (valid→principal + cache-hit-no-refetch, roles-as-scope-string, rejection matrix incl. wrong-aud/iss/expired/no-sub/bad-sig/malformed/**HS256-rejected**, key-rotation pickup, unknown-kid rate-limit, provider-down≠unauthenticated, config validation, ctx round-trip) — pure (httptest IdP, in-memory RSA keys), run on every `go test` · `kanz/pkg/auth/`

### External API Gateway & BFF

- [x] **API-01e** Gateway contract tests — `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/{gateway,middleware}/*_test.go`, **closes the API-01 epic**. `contract_test.go` wires the production-shaped chain (version→auth→rate-limit→idempotency) over the gateway handler against a fake upstream client and asserts the cross-cutting contract: **authz** (no token→401, token-without-role→403, token-with-role→200), **rate-limit** (1-token bucket → second request 429), **version matrix** (absent/`v1`→200, `v2`→406), and a **query p99 budget** (200 in-process queries, p99 < 50ms — a regression guard against accidental blocking work on the hot path). `gateway_test.go` proves **gRPC↔REST transcoding parity**: the REST JSON body protojson-decodes back into a message `proto.Equal` to the canned proto the fake gRPC client returned (no DTO drift), plus as_of/measure-filter forwarding, scenario body decode (path id wins over body), gRPC-code→HTTP-status mapping, and the OpenAPI doc. middleware tests cover the HS256 validator (valid/bad-sig/expired/malformed), per-tenant rate-limit isolation, and idempotency replay (POST deduped, GET not). All run on every `go test` (pure, fake upstream) · `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/`
- [x] **API-01d** Gateway edge controls — `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/middleware/` (`auth.go`, `middleware.go`). **Composable `http.Handler` decorators** wired by `Chain` (outermost-first): **Version** (X-API-Version negotiation, 406 on unsupported), **Signing** (optional HMAC-SHA256 `X-Signature` over method+path+body, 401 on mismatch; body buffered+restored), **Auth**, **RateLimit**, **Idempotency**. **Auth consumes an `Authenticator` interface → typed `Principal` (subject/tenant/roles) on ctx; deny-by-default when a required role is configured** (missing→403, missing token→401, nil authenticator disables auth for dev). **The bundled `JWTAuthenticator` is a deliberately minimal stdlib-only HS256 validator — the stand-in AUTH-01a replaces with real OIDC/JWKS behind the SAME interface** (so the swap touches only the constructor, not the chain). **Per-tenant token-bucket RateLimit** keyed on the authenticated tenant (fallback subject→remote addr) so one tenant can't starve another — hand-rolled lazy-refill bucket, no x/time dep (CLAUDE.md dep-hygiene), 429 + Retry-After on exhaustion. **Idempotency** replays the cached status+body for a repeated `Idempotency-Key` on non-GET (bounded+TTL store), so a client safely retries a scenario POST without re-evaluating · `kanz/services/api-gateway/internal/middleware/`
- [x] **API-01c** api-gateway BFF — new `kanz/services/api-gateway/` (`cmd/api-gateway/main.go`, `internal/{config,gateway}`). **REST↔gRPC transcoding via protojson over the generated `query.v1.RiskQueryServiceClient` interface** — each of the four read routes (`GET /v1/portfolios/{id}/exposure|measures`, `POST .../scenario`, `GET /v1/health`) builds the proto request, calls the gRPC client, and marshals the proto response with protojson, so REST and gRPC are the SAME messages (no hand-maintained DTOs). **Hand-rolled transcoding chosen over grpc-gateway codegen (the load-bearing decision)**: a four-RPC read surface doesn't justify `google.api.http` annotations + generated `.gw.go` + an extra schema-pipeline plugin; a thin protojson adapter keeps deps to the protobuf runtime already in the module, and a static **OpenAPI 3.0 doc** is emitted at `/openapi.json` so clients still get a machine-readable contract. **Depends on the client INTERFACE** so tests inject a fake and main injects an mTLS-dialed client. **main** builds the OBS-01 provider (`/metrics`, trace-correlated logger), dials risk-engine over **mTLS when `API_GATEWAY_SPIFFE_SOCKET` is set** (SEC-01b `AuthorizeMesh`; `grpc.NewClient` lazy-connects so a momentarily-down upstream doesn't fail startup) else plaintext, mounts the `/v1` routes behind the edge chain while keeping `/healthz`/`/readyz`/`/metrics`/`/openapi.json` un-gated, and shuts down gracefully. gRPC-code→HTTP-status mapping mirrors the grpc-gateway table. **Follow-ups**: tighten upstream authz to `AuthorizeServices(risk-engine)`, add the api-gateway to the CICD-01c build matrix + a distroless Dockerfile · `kanz/services/api-gateway/`
- [x] **API-01b** Risk query gRPC server — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/grpcsrv/server.go` (+ tests), wired into the risk-engine main. **A thin ADAPTER, not logic**: implements the generated `query.v1.RiskQueryServiceServer` over the in-process `api/v1.Engine` (the concrete `EngineImpl`, ORCH-01b) — every method decodes the proto request → api/v1 request, calls the Engine, encodes the api/v1 response → proto. All risk decisions (live-vs-cache fallback, staleness flags, scenario eval) stay in the Engine; the server only translates + maps errors to gRPC codes (`ErrPortfolioNotFound`→NotFound, `ErrInvalidRequest`→InvalidArgument, ctx→Canceled/DeadlineExceeded, else Internal). **Reuse, not re-derive**: response payloads go through `publish.ToProtoExposureSet`/`ToProtoMeasureSet` (the RISK-10 converters, exported for this) so a value read over RPC is byte-identical to the same FACT on the bus — one domain→proto mapping, not two. The `api/v1.ExposureSet`/`MeasureSet` interfaces only expose `AsOf()`/`Lookup()` (insufficient to serialize), so the adapter type-asserts the concrete `*domain.ExposureSet`/`*domain.MeasureSet` — sound because the engine always returns those, and **permitted because grpcsrv is under the RISK-02 `riskComposerPrefix` carve-out** (the risk-engine service is the module's composition root). **Wired into main**: shares the live `store` + `cache` + `registry` with the recomputer (so queries see live state + the recomputer's last-known-good cache), constructs `engine.New(store, registry, cache, NewDetector())`, serves on `RISK_ENGINE_GRPC_LISTEN` with **optional mTLS** — `RISK_ENGINE_SPIFFE_SOCKET` set ⇒ `transport.ServerOption(src, AuthorizeMesh())` (SEC-01b, the API-01c gateway dials over this), empty ⇒ plaintext (local/dev) — and `GracefulStop` on shutdown. Scenario shocks decode from the proto `oneof` into the api/v1 `PriceShock`/`ParallelShift`; an empty oneof member is InvalidArgument. Zero response `as_of` elides to a nil timestamp (absent, not epoch). 7 adapter tests (set/flag conversion, measure filter forwarding, shock decode, no-kind-shock rejection, mode mapping, error-code matrix, as_of round-trip + zero-elision); arch boundary test still green · `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/grpcsrv/server.go`
- [x] **API-01a** Risk query gRPC proto — new `kanz-schemas/proto/query/v1/risk_query.proto` + CODEOWNERS entry, **begins the Phase-2 API epic**. `RiskQueryService` is the proto mirror of the `kanz/internal/risk/api/v1.Engine` Go interface: `Exposure`/`Measures`/`EvaluateScenario`/`Health` RPCs, **query + scenario + health ONLY — no Apply/Mutate/Command** (the "all state changes are event-driven" anti-decision holds at the wire surface too). **Reuse over redefinition**: responses embed the SAME `domain.v1.ExposureSet` + `domain.v1.RiskMeasureSet` the bus publishes (a value read over RPC is byte-identical to the FACT on the bus), so only the request envelopes, the scenario-shock taxonomy, and the query-trust signals are new. **Scenario shocks are a `oneof` (`PriceShock`/`ParallelShift`)** mirroring the api/v1 concrete shock types, additive via new members. **Query-local `Mode` + `QualityFlag` enums** (NORMAL/DEGRADED, DEGRADED/STALE) — deliberately NOT envelope.v1.QualityFlag, whose REPLAYED/LATE values are event-stream concerns, not query-result trust. **RPC/message naming follows buf STANDARD** (the EvaluateScenario RPC needed `EvaluateScenarioRequest/Response`, not `Scenario*`, to satisfy `RPC_REQUEST_STANDARD_NAME`). CODEOWNERS: `/proto/query/` → risk-engine + architecture. buf-discovered (under the existing `proto` module); lint clean; **Go SDK regenerated via a Go-only buf template** (both `protoc-gen-go` + `protoc-gen-go-grpc`, so `risk_query.pb.go` + `risk_query_grpc.pb.go` emit) — gen/go + the kanz module both build against it. The server impl (API-01b inside risk-engine over the concrete EngineImpl + durable state) and the gateway BFF (API-01c) are the next subtasks · `kanz-schemas/proto/query/v1/risk_query.proto`

### Observability & Telemetry

- [x] **OBS-01e** Tests pinning the DATA-08 metric contract — `kanz/internal/integrity/metrics_test.go` + the OBS-01c slices in `kanz/pkg/bus/telemetry_test.go`. **`TestMetricsContract` gathers the registry and asserts the EXACT series names AND label sets (sorted) for all nine `kanz_data_*` families, plus a reverse guard: any `kanz_data_*` series NOT in the expected map fails as contract drift** — so a rename or a stray label on either side (exporter or dashboard/alert) breaks the test, not a panel at 3am. Values are checked too (lag/age math, gap-missing accumulation, non-gap/unknown no-ops). Bus telemetry tests cover OBS-01c (`kanz_bus_publish_total{result=ok}` + a latency sample) and OBS-01d (traceparent stamped on the envelope, round-trips to a valid span context with matching trace_id) + nil-metrics-is-safe. Runs on every `go test` (pure, no infra) · `kanz/internal/integrity/metrics_test.go`, `kanz/pkg/bus/telemetry_test.go`
- [x] **OBS-01d** W3C `trace_context` → OTel span propagation across every bus hop — new `kanz/pkg/observability/trace.go` + `kanz/pkg/bus/trace.go`, wired into Producer/Consumer. **The envelope already carries `trace_context` (EVT-17b); OBS-01d makes it a REAL OTel trace, not just a copied string.** `observability.TraceparentFromContext` injects the active span to a W3C traceparent (the envelope's single-field carrier); `ContextWithTraceparent` extracts an inbound traceparent into ctx as the remote parent. **Producer.Publish now opens a PRODUCER span and stamps ITS traceparent** (trace precedence: explicit Event field > active span > legacy string ctx — the string path kept for span-unaware callers); **Consumer extracts the inbound trace onto ctx + opens a child CONSUMER span** so a handler's logs/spans and any derived publish continue the same trace_id. **The bus needs no injected tracer**: `otel.Tracer(...)` is late-bound to the global provider, so spans are no-ops until `observability.New` installs one (zero-config, zero-overhead when telemetry is off) · `kanz/pkg/bus/`, `kanz/pkg/observability/trace.go`
- [x] **OBS-01c** Risk + bus RED/USE metrics + lag/pending gauges — new `kanz/pkg/bus/metrics.go` (`BusMetrics`) + `kanz/internal/risk/engine/metrics.go` (`Metrics`), wired into Producer/Consumer/Recomputer + the risk-engine main. **`kanz_risk_recompute_total{status}` + `kanz_risk_recompute_duration_seconds` match the CICD-01e canary analysis verbatim** — the names/`status` label were the contract that template already queries (seam-ahead-of-producer), so the rollout's success-rate + p99 gates now resolve against real series; recompute counts a store-miss as no-op (not a failure) and `status=error` only on an emit failure. **Bus RED**: `kanz_bus_{publish,consume}_total{result}` + `_duration_seconds`; consume records the handler outcome (DLQ-routed still counts `error`), unframe/validate failures count with 0 duration. **lag/pending gauges** (`kanz_bus_consumer_lag`, `kanz_bus_pending_messages`) are the KEDA scale signal — exposed with setters fed by a broker-stats poller (kafka-go `Reader.Stats().Lag` / NATS `ConsumerInfo.NumPending`); the per-message path doesn't carry them, so the poller is the documented wiring. **Both exporters are optional + nil-safe** (a nil `*BusMetrics`/`*Metrics` is a no-op on every method) so non-instrumented callers and the existing tests are untouched; wired via additive `ProducerConfig.Metrics`, `WithBusMetrics`, and a backward-compatible variadic `engine.WithMetrics` · `kanz/pkg/bus/metrics.go`, `kanz/internal/risk/engine/metrics.go`
- [x] **OBS-01b** Integrity metrics exporter — new `kanz/internal/integrity/metrics.go` (`Metrics`). **A thin translation layer wrapping the DATA-01..05 detector RESULTS into the exact nine `kanz_data_*` series the dashboards + DATA-09 alerts query** — the detectors stay pure in-memory classifiers (the README's detect-then-export discipline), the orchestrator that owns them calls `ObserveStaleness/Gap/Reconcile/Drift/Discrepancies/QualityEvent` per result. Labels reuse the detector vocabulary verbatim (`subject`/`partition_key`/`source`, `transport`/`seen_on`/`missing_on`, `feature`/`metric`, `kind`/`severity`). **Decisions**: `StalenessUnknown` (no subject) is dropped rather than emitting an empty-label series; `last_event_age` is set from the result at record-time via an injectable clock (a scrape-time frontier-aging collector is a noted follow-up); drift exports BOTH score and threshold so the alert's `> on(feature,metric)` self-tracks each detector's threshold; `quality_events` takes kind/severity as strings to keep the exporter decoupled from the observation schema · `kanz/internal/integrity/metrics.go`
- [x] **OBS-01a** Telemetry foundation — new `kanz/pkg/observability/` (`observability.go`), **begins the Observability epic**. One `Provider` bundling a Prometheus registry, an OTel `TracerProvider`, and a trace-correlated slog logger, plus `MetricsHandler()` (promhttp) and `Shutdown`. **Metrics go through Prometheus client_golang DIRECTLY, not an OTel→Prometheus exporter** (the load-bearing choice): the DATA-08 / `kanz_risk_*` contracts pin exact series names, and the otel exporter mangles them with unit/`_total` suffixes — so OBS-01b/c register their collectors on `Provider.Registry` and get the names verbatim. **Tracing uses the OTel SDK** (W3C TraceContext+Baggage propagator installed globally, OTLP/gRPC export only when `OTLPEndpoint` set so startup never blocks on a collector) so the envelope `trace_context` propagates (OBS-01d). **The "slog→OTel bridge" is a handler decorator that stamps the active span's trace_id/span_id onto every record** — a correlation bridge, not the OTel log SDK (kanz logs are stdout JSON shipped by the platform; the need is log↔trace join, not a second export path); the decorator preserves the wrapper through `WithAttrs`/`WithGroup`. Baseline Go+process collectors registered for USE panels. Resource schema pinned to semconv v1.40.0 to match the SDK's `resource.Default()` (a mismatch fails the merge). Wired into the risk-engine main (`/metrics`, trace-correlated logger, OTLP endpoint config). Tests verify trace injection, /metrics baseline, and the global propagator — run on every `go test` · `kanz/pkg/observability/observability.go`

### Delivery, Build & Observability

- [x] **CICD-01e** Progressive delivery + automated rollback — new `kanz/infra/deploy/` (`risk-engine-rollout.yaml`, `analysis-template.yaml`, README), **closes the CICD-01 epic**. **Argo Rollouts replica-based canary, NOT a mesh traffic-split (the load-bearing choice)**: risk-engine is a NATS consumer + gRPC query service with no ingress, so weight-based traffic routing would need a service mesh it doesn't have — Argo Rollouts shifts a fraction of *pods* (20%→50%→100% with pauses) and gates each step on a Prometheus `AnalysisRun`, no mesh required. **Auto-rollback = the analysis abort**: `failureLimit: 0` means one bad measurement aborts the rollout, which scales the canary to zero and leaves the stable revision serving — no human in the unhappy path. Analysis (`AnalysisTemplate`) scopes every query to the canary pods via the `rollouts-pod-template-hash` arg (a regression in NEW code can't hide behind stable traffic) and asserts recompute success-rate ≥99% + p99 ≤500ms (the ORCH-01f budget) against the **OBS-01 `kanz_risk_*` series** — which don't exist until OBS-01c ships, so the queries are the contract the canary asserts (seam-ahead-of-producer, cf. PRED-04/11). **The Rollout pod composes the whole platform stack**: the CICD-01c/d cosign-signed image (admission-verified, CICD-01d), the SEC-01a SVID (SPIFFE CSI socket + `risk-engine` ServiceAccount), and the SEC-01d CSI secret for the DB DSN (`RISK_ENGINE_DATABASE_URL_FILE`, no plaintext env), on the ORCH-01a `/healthz`+`/readyz` probes. schema-registry's Rollout, digest-pinning over `:latest`, and a consumer-lag abort metric are documented follow-ups. YAML validated · `kanz/infra/deploy/`
- [x] **CICD-01d** Supply-chain gate — new `.github/workflows/release.yml` + `kanz/infra/security/admission/` (`cluster-image-policy.yaml` + README). **On a `v*` tag, per service: build→push→scan→SBOM→sign→verify.** trivy scans the pushed digest with `exit-code:1 severity:CRITICAL ignore-unfixed` (fails the job before signing, so a vulnerable image lands unsigned and admission then refuses it); syft (anchore/sbom-action) emits an SPDX SBOM; cosign signs AND `attest --type spdxjson` attaches the SBOM. **Signing is KEYLESS (the load-bearing decision)** — GitHub OIDC → Fulcio short-lived cert → Rekor transparency log, no private key to store/rotate; the signer's *identity* is the credential, the same "identity not a stored secret" stance as SEC-01 (SPIRE SVIDs, Vault SPIFFE auth). Everything after build pins the **immutable digest** (`steps.build.outputs.digest`), never a tag. A `cosign verify` self-check (issuer = GitHub OIDC, identity-regexp = this workflow on a `v*` tag) gates the release on the same predicate the cluster enforces. **Admission verification is the cluster half** (task path names only release.yml, but the gate isn't real without enforcement): a sigstore policy-controller `ClusterImagePolicy` over `ghcr.io/kanz-eng/**` requires (1) a cosign signature from the release-workflow keyless identity (issuer + exact `…/release.yml@refs/tags/v*` subject pinned) AND (2) the SPDX SBOM attestation (provenance-or-reject), `mode: enforce` = fail-closed; webhook scopes to `policy.sigstore.dev/include: "true"` namespaces. SDK-gen/build steps reuse CICD-01a/c. Pinning the distroless base + actions by digest, and bumping trivy to fail-on-HIGH once base images are clean, are documented follow-ups. Not runnable locally (no GHCR/OIDC/docker daemon) — standard sigstore keyless + trivy + syft wiring; both YAML validated · `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `kanz/infra/security/admission/`
- [x] **CICD-01c** Per-service distroless container builds — new `kanz/services/{risk-engine,schema-registry}/Dockerfile`, root `.dockerignore`, and `.github/workflows/build.yml`. **Multi-stage: `golang:1.26.1` builder → `gcr.io/distroless/static:nonroot`.** `CGO_ENABLED=0` static binary (every dep — pgx/nats/kafka-go/go-spiffe — is pure Go, verified) is exactly what distroless/static needs: no shell, no libc, no package manager, runs as uid 65532. `-trimpath -ldflags="-s -w"` for a lean reproducible binary. **The load-bearing build decision — context is the repo ROOT, not `kanz/`**: the module's `replace … => ../kanz-schemas/gen/go` points outside the module, so the Dockerfile COPYs BOTH `kanz/` and `kanz-schemas/gen/go/` side by side, and `GOFLAGS=-mod=mod` lets the build resolve the local-replace module. The SDK is generated-not-committed (EVT-15a), so the **workflow runs `buf generate` on the runner first** (reusing the CICD-01a Go-SDK steps incl. protoc-gen-go-grpc) rather than dragging buf + plugins into the Docker builder; `build-push-action` then builds with **`context: .`** (the local checkout incl. the generated `gen/go`, NOT the git context which would omit the uncommitted SDK). Layer-caching split (go.mod + gen/go → `go mod download` → source) + GHA buildx cache per service. `.dockerignore` trims junk but **deliberately keeps `kanz-schemas/gen/go`** (the build needs it) while dropping `gen/python`/`gen/ts`/`.gotmp`/node_modules/pycache. **Workflow**: matrix over the two services, builds on every PR (validation, `push: false`) and pushes to `ghcr.io/<owner>/<service>` on push to main via `metadata-action` tags (sha + branch + latest-on-default). Distroless digest-pinning + SBOM/scan/sign/admission are the CICD-01d supply-chain follow-up. Image build unverified locally (no docker daemon on the dev box) but the static binary compile — the substantive builder step — is verified for both services; workflow + Dockerfile YAML/syntax checked · `kanz/services/*/Dockerfile`, `.github/workflows/build.yml`
- [x] **CICD-01b** Python + TypeScript jobs + buf schema gate — extends `.github/workflows/kanz-ci.yml` with `python`, `ts`, and `buf-breaking` jobs, plus trigger on `kanz-py/**`. **`python` job is self-contained via `grpc_tools.protoc`, NOT buf** — the kanz-py tests need the generated Python SDK (uncommitted EVT-15a, unpublished DEBT-01b), and running the full `buf.gen.yaml` in a Python job would drag in the Go local plugins; `grpc_tools.protoc --python_out --pyi_out --grpc_python_out` over all protos emits the whole tree in one tool with no Go/buf dependency. **Crucially `--grpc_python_out` closes the Python gRPC-stub gap** (the analog of the Go protoc-gen-go-grpc gap PRED-13 fixed): `kanz_inference.interactive.servicer` imports `inference.v1.inference_service_pb2_grpc`, which `protocolbuffers/python` alone never emits — so the PRED-08 servicer test (and the module at runtime) was unimportable. Wiring this into the buf *release* pipeline so the **published** SDK ships the stub stays a follow-up; the CI gate generates it directly. PYTHONPATH spans the SDK root + `kanz-py` (PEP-420 namespace pb2 packages); broker integration tests self-skip. **Also repaired `tests/test_consumer.py` (EVT-18d), 16 tests that never ran** (the recurring "done but never executed" failure, like PRED-07): 10 failed on `Message(body=…)` missing the required `subject` positional (added `subject="x.y.z"`); the other 6 set a custom `idempotency_key` on a FACT without matching `event_id`, which `validate()` rejects per the FACT `idempotency_key==event_id` invariant — fixed at root with `_envelope(event_id="evt-A")` (sets both), intent-preserving since dedup still keys on that value. Full suite now **159 passed, 3 skipped** (verified locally). **`ts` job** generates the Protobuf-ES SDK via a **TS-only buf template** (mirrors the pinned `bufbuild/es:v2.10.0`, avoids the Go plugins), assembles `gen/ts` into the `@kanz-eng/kanz-schemas` package, then `npm pkg set`s kanz-bus's dep to a `file:` path so npm resolves the unpublished SDK from disk, and runs the package's real test runner (`tsx --test`, i.e. `node:test` — the task's "vitest" is loose shorthand; the project doesn't use vitest). **`buf-breaking` job** runs `buf lint` + `buf breaking --against '../.git#branch=main,subdir=kanz-schemas'` (fetch-depth 0), enforcing the additive-only envelope discipline (EVT-21c) on proto PRs; a no-op when no proto changed. TS job unverified locally (no buf/network here); Python + YAML verified · `.github/workflows/kanz-ci.yml`
- [x] **CICD-01a** GitHub Actions Go pipeline — new `.github/workflows/kanz-ci.yml` at the **monorepo root** (where GitHub reads workflows and where it can see both `kanz/` and `kanz-schemas/`; the nested `kanz-schemas/.github/` workflows are for that companion repo when split out). One `go` job: checkout → `setup-go` with `go-version-file: kanz/go.mod` (CI tracks the pinned 1.26.1 toolchain, never drifts) → `buf-setup` → **install BOTH `protoc-gen-go@v1.36.11` and `protoc-gen-go-grpc@v1.5.1`** → **`buf generate` the kanz-schemas Go SDK + `go mod init`/`tidy` in `gen/go`** (the SDK is generated-not-committed per EVT-15a, and the `kanz/go.mod` `replace … => ../kanz-schemas/gen/go` resolves to it within the single checkout) → `go build` → `go vet` → `go test -race ./...` → `golangci-lint`. **`GOFLAGS=-mod=mod` is a job-level env, the load-bearing knob**: the kanz-schemas-go dep is a local `replace`, so the build must be allowed to update go.mod/go.sum for the freshly generated module rather than failing read-only (matches the documented local-build procedure). **protoc-gen-go-grpc is mandatory, not optional** — it emits the `inference.v1` gRPC stubs (PRED-06) that protoc-gen-go alone omits, so `buf generate` without it leaves the module uncompilable. **Arch + contract suites need no special invocation** — `test/arch` + `test/contract/*` are ordinary untagged `_test.go`, so `go test ./...` runs them; the Postgres/Kafka integration tests skip without `TEST_POSTGRES_URL`/`TEST_KAFKA_BROKERS` so the gate needs no live brokers. **`-race` needs cgo** (default-on on ubuntu runners; can't run on this Windows box, no gcc — but build/vet/`go test ./...`/arch+contract all verified green locally). **golangci-lint via `install-mode: goinstall`** so the linter is built with the job's Go 1.26 toolchain and its parser always understands the module's language version (a pre-pinned binary built with an older Go could choke on 1.26 syntax); `--timeout=5m`. Triggers on PRs touching `kanz/**` or the schema proto/buf config, and on push to main. CICD-01b extends this same file with the Python/TS jobs + `buf breaking` gate · `.github/workflows/kanz-ci.yml`

### Security & Identity

- [x] **SEC-01e** Tests: mTLS handshake + peer-identity assertion + plaintext/unauthorized rejection — new `kanz/pkg/transport/mtls_test.go`, **closes the SEC-01 zero-trust epic**. The integration half SEC-01b/c deferred ("need a real SVID pair"), with **no external SPIRE**: the test mints a real in-memory CA + leaf X509-SVIDs (real ECDSA keys, real URI-SAN SPIFFE IDs via `id.URL()`, digitalSignature + client/server ExtKeyUsage) and drives a real TLS handshake, so go-spiffe's `tlsconfig` verification + the AuthorizeMesh/AuthorizeServices policy run end-to-end on **every `go test`** — not behind a `TEST_*` gate (PERS-01e anti-rot rule; pure crypto, no infra). 5 cases: (1) **handshake asserts peer identity** — two valid in-domain SVIDs complete mTLS and EACH side reads back the peer's SPIFFE ID via `x509svid.IDFromCert` (the property the mesh relies on, not just "handshake ok"); (2) **unauthenticated** — a no-client-cert TLS client is rejected by `RequireAnyClientCert`; (3) **plaintext** — raw cleartext bytes at the TLS server fail (no cleartext downgrade); (4) **unauthorized** — a valid in-domain SVID NOT on the server's `AuthorizeServices` allow-list is rejected (auth necessary ≠ sufficient, deny-by-default); (5) **untrusted CA** — a leaf chaining to a foreign CA fails verification. **Load-bearing test-harness decision**: `handshake()` uses a loopback **TCP** listener, NOT `net.Pipe` — on a rejected handshake the server writes a TLS alert, and unbuffered `net.Pipe` blocks that write until the 5s ctx deadline (peer stopped reading), making every rejection test take 5s; TCP's socket buffer flushes the alert so the rejecting side returns its error immediately (full suite 21s → 1.3s). `-race` unavailable on this machine (no cgo/gcc) but the `sErr` write/read is channel-synchronized so there's no race; `go vet` clean. Updated the `tls_test.go` header that called these tests deferred · `kanz/pkg/transport/mtls_test.go`
- [x] **SEC-01d** Secrets via Vault/cloud KMS through CSI mounts — new `kanz/infra/security/secrets/` (`vault.yaml`, `csi.yaml`, `secretproviderclass.yaml`, `spire-upstream.yaml`, README runbook) + a `config.secret()` `<VAR>_FILE` seam in both Go services. **Secrets reach pods as tmpfs files via the Secrets Store CSI Driver + `vault-csi-provider`, NEVER as k8s `Secret`s (base64-in-etcd, broad `get secret` RBAC) or env vars (`/proc/<pid>/environ`, crash dumps, child procs).** **Vault auth is the SPIFFE JWT-SVID** (SEC-01a's `default_jwt_svid_ttl=5m`) against Vault's `jwt` backend via the SPIRE OIDC discovery endpoint — secret access gated on the SAME attested identity as mTLS transport (SEC-01b), no second static credential; the `jwt` role's `bound_subject` = the consumer's SPIFFE ID, so it's deny-by-default per-service (mirrors SEC-01b `AuthorizeServices`). **One cloud KMS key is the platform root**: it both auto-unseals Vault (`seal "awskms"`, no Shamir shares) AND backs the SPIRE `UpstreamAuthority` — neither root ever exists as recoverable plaintext. **Plaintext removed**: the Postgres DSN is the only genuine runtime secret (everything else is mTLS where the SVID *is* the credential, so there's no password). `config.secret()` reads it from `<VAR>_FILE` in preference to the plaintext `<VAR>` env (risk-engine + schema-registry); `spire-upstream.yaml` replaces SPIRE's plaintext `keys.json` disk root + sqlite `connection_string` with the KMS `UpstreamAuthority` + a CSI-mounted postgres DSN — closing the SEC-01a HA/KMS follow-up. Vault itself is a mesh workload (SVID-fronted TLS listener via the SEC-01c spiffe-helper bridge, since Vault isn't SPIFFE-aware). Bootstrap posture (1 Raft replica) matches NATS/SPIRE; HA ≥3 peers + Vault `database` dynamic-cred engine are documented follow-ups. Unit test on the `secret()` seam (file-beats-env, trim, unreadable-falls-back) runs every `go test` — the plaintext-preference regression is security-relevant; YAML-validated · `kanz/infra/security/secrets/`
- [x] **SEC-01c** TLS/SASL on NATS + Kafka clients + broker manifests — `kanz/pkg/bus/{nats,kafka}.go` gain optional transport security, and `infra/nats` + `infra/kafka` get mTLS listeners fed by the SEC-01a SVIDs. **`NATSConfig.TLSConfig`** (+ NATS-native `Username`/`Password`/`Token`) → `nats.Secure`; **`KafkaConfig.TLSConfig` + `SASL sasl.Mechanism`** wired to BOTH the writer `Transport` and a shared reader `Dialer` (nil/nil ⇒ kafka-go DefaultDialer, exact prior plaintext behaviour). **bus stays decoupled from go-spiffe** — it takes a ready `*tls.Config` (built by the caller from `transport.ClientTLSConfig`, SEC-01b) rather than a SPIFFE source, same injection style as the rest of bus; mTLS is transport encryption AND auth (the client SVID is the credential). **Infra**: each broker gets a dedicated ServiceAccount (distinct SVID identity `…/sa/{nats,kafka}`), a `spiffe-helper` init-container (fetch first SVID before boot) + sidecar (renew + reload), a memory-backed cert emptyDir, and the SEC-01a SPIFFE CSI socket volume. NATS: `tls { verify: true }` on the client listener (reload via `--signal reload` in a shared PID namespace). Kafka: new `SSL://:9094` listener, `ssl.client.auth=required`, PEM keystore (helper concatenates cert+key into the combined `keystore.pem` Kafka's PEM store needs) + bundle truststore, endpoint-identification disabled (verify the SPIFFE URI SAN, not DNS); PLAINTEXT:9092 kept for inter-broker + the bootstrap job during migration. Broker-free unit tests assert the Kafka TLS/SASL config reaches the writer Transport + reader Dialer (and nil-dialer on plaintext); handshake/rejection integration is SEC-01e. **Also fixed a pre-existing latent failure**: `test/arch/risk_boundary_test.go` (RISK-02) flagged the risk-engine SERVICE as a boundary violator for importing impl packages — but ORCH-01/PERS-01 made it the module's designated composition root (wiring the concrete engine needs engine/state/compute/publish/ingest; api/v* is interfaces only). The test had been red since ORCH-01b and only surfaced now that the full suite runs (the `%TEMP%` Application-Control exec-block had been silently failing test binaries; running with a project-local `GOTMPDIR` fixed it). Added a `riskComposerPrefix` carve-out for the service — the standard "main/wiring layer may import internals" exemption · `kanz/pkg/bus/`, `kanz/infra/nats/`, `kanz/infra/kafka/`
- [x] **SEC-01b** Reusable mTLS transport helpers — new `kanz/pkg/transport/tls.go`, the single seam turning SEC-01a SVIDs into mutual-TLS for every gRPC client/server (covers the PRED-06/07 inference path + the future API gateway). Wraps **go-spiffe/v2** (added as a direct dep — the canonical SPIFFE library matching the deployed SPIRE control plane; hand-rolling SVID verification + bundle rotation + the client `InsecureSkipVerify`+`VerifyPeerCertificate` dance would be more code and a bigger attack surface, so it's a focused security dep, not bloat). **`NewSource` keeps a live, auto-rotating Workload API handle** (`*workloadapi.X509Source`, socket from the SEC-01a CSI mount or `SPIFFE_ENDPOINT_SOCKET`) supplying BOTH the local SVID and the trust bundle — go-spiffe re-fetches at ~half the 1h TTL so long-lived servers swap fresh certs with zero reload code here. **`ServerTLSConfig`/`ClientTLSConfig` return `*tls.Config`** (the literal deliverable) via `tlsconfig.MTLS{Server,Client}Config`, plus `Server/ClientCredentials` (gRPC `TransportCredentials`) and `ServerOption`/`ClientDialOption` (grpc.ServerOption / DialOption) convenience wrappers. **Authorization is deny-by-default and explicit**: `AuthorizeMesh()` accepts any valid SVID in the `kanz.internal` trust domain (intra-mesh default; finer access control is AUTH-01's app-layer job), `AuthorizeServices(ids…)` narrows to an allow-list (e.g. gateway accepts only risk-engine) — there is deliberately NO "authorize any trust domain" helper. `ServiceID(ns, sa)` builds `spiffe://kanz.internal/ns/<ns>/sa/<sa>` matching the SEC-01a ClusterSPIFFEID template. **Enabling hook into PRED-07**: `SyncClientOptions` gained an optional `Credentials credentials.TransportCredentials` field (nil ⇒ plaintext as before, set ⇒ mTLS) so the inference sync client actually uses these — "covers PRED-07" is real, not aspirational, without a separate constructor. Unit tests cover the pure helpers (ID format + empty rejection, authorizer construction, server-config RequireAnyClientCert + VerifyPeerCertificate + TLS1.2 floor, client-config InsecureSkipVerify+verify, gRPC wrappers non-nil) and run on every `go test`; the mTLS handshake + peer-assertion + plaintext-rejection integration tests are SEC-01e (need a real SVID pair) · `kanz/pkg/transport/tls.go`
- [x] **SEC-01a** Workload identity issuance + rotation (SPIRE) — new `kanz/infra/security/spire/` (`namespaces.yaml`, `rbac.yaml`, `spire-server.yaml`, `spire-agent.yaml`, `registration.yaml`, README), **begins the Security epic**. SPIRE server (StatefulSet + controller-manager sidecar) + per-node agent DaemonSet + SPIFFE CSI driver issue auto-rotated **X.509-SVIDs** to every pod in a `kanz.internal/spiffe: enabled` namespace (kanz-messaging — opted in via a one-line label on `infra/nats/namespace.yaml` — plus a new `kanz-services`). **SPIRE chosen over cert-manager** because the requirement is workload *identity* (attest *what* a workload is — `k8s_psat` node + `k8s` pod attestation — before issuing), not certificate provisioning to whoever holds a secret. **Trust domain `kanz.internal`, SPIFFE ID `spiffe://kanz.internal/ns/<ns>/sa/<sa>` keyed on the ServiceAccount, not the pod** — a rolling deploy keeps a stable identity while leaf certs rotate underneath; this ID is what SEC-01b asserts on peers and AUTH-01c binds to the command issuer. **Rotation is the actual deliverable**: `ca_ttl=24h`, `default_x509_svid_ttl=1h` (go-spiffe re-fetches at ~30m half-life), `default_jwt_svid_ttl=5m` — a leaked leaf is valid ≤1h and no human ever touches a cert. **Declarative issuance via a `ClusterSPIFFEID` CR** (controller-manager reconciles it into registration entries + publishes the trust bundle to the `spire-bundle` ConfigMap) — GitOps-friendly vs imperative `entry create` drift. **Node attestation = projected SA token** (TokenReview, the only cluster-write RBAC the server needs); **SVID reaches workloads via the SPIFFE CSI driver** (read-only ephemeral volume mounting the agent socket) so SEC-01b's go-spiffe client dials `unix:///run/spiffe/spire-agent.sock`. **Bootstrap posture mirroring the NATS single-StatefulSet stance**: 1 server replica on a sqlite PVC + disk keys; HA (postgres DataStore reusing the PERS-01/registry cluster, ≥2 replicas, KMS-backed `UpstreamAuthority` root) is a documented SEC-01d follow-up. CRDs ship with spire-controller-manager, applied once per cluster before `registration.yaml`. All manifests YAML-validated · `kanz/infra/security/spire/`

### Platform Runtime

- [x] **PERS-01e** Tests: crash-recovery + idempotent-replay — new `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/postgres_test.go` (gated integration) + a DB-free `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/bootstrap_test.go`, **closes the PERS-01 durable-state epic**. **Two test files is the load-bearing decision, against the codebase's recurring "tests that never ran" failure** (PRED-07/13, test_consumer.py): `postgres_test.go` is gated on `TEST_POSTGRES_URL` (skips in CI without a DB, same convention as the Kafka `TEST_KAFKA_BROKERS` suites) — so the epic's headline property, idempotent-replay, is ALSO proven by a DB-free `bootstrap_test.go` that runs on every `go test`. Without the second file the "re-applied events don't double-count" guarantee would skip silently in CI. **The idempotent-replay test is designed around set-semantics**: risk applies are last-wins (SetPosition replaces, not accumulates), so re-applying the *same* event proves nothing — the meaningful test replays a SUPERSEDED OLDER event (value 1000, as_of t1) against restored newer state (value 2000, as_of t2) whose AppliedKeys already holds the old event's key, and asserts state does NOT regress to 1000 (positions have no as_of-monotonicity guard, so that regression is the real corruption the seeded dedup prevents). Contrast test: a genuinely-new key IS applied (gap recovery). **`bootstrap_test.go` is `package app` (internal)** because the `newSource`/`now` seams are unexported — injecting an in-memory replay log + deterministic clock requires being inside the package; 8 tests: restore-rehydrates, idempotent-no-regress, new-events-applied, malformed-frame-skip-and-continue, source-error-aborts, no-resume-position-skips-replay (factory never called), LoadAll-error-aborts, restore-doesn't-clobber-live-state. **`postgres_test.go` reads the real `0001_state.sql`** (relative path, `DROP … CASCADE` then apply) so the migration stays the single source of truth; crash-recovery parity proves committed state reloads byte-for-byte across a fresh pool (RPO=0). The Postgres suite still needs a one-time run against a live DB to confirm the SQL — flagged like the EVT-20a Kafka integration test · `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/postgres_test.go`
- [x] **PERS-01d** Restart-recovery bootstrap — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/bootstrap.go` (`Bootstrap`), plus `state.Store.Restore` and the main wiring that closes the ORCH-01e/01c seams. **`Bootstrap.Run` = restore (`StateStore.LoadAll` → `state.Store.Restore` each) → replay (Kafka forward from each snapshot's LogPosition via the EVT-20a `replay.Reader`) → return so main switches to the live NATS spine.** **Replay applies through the BARE `state.Store`, NOT the `TriggeringApplier`** — feeding replay through the recompute decorator would emit a storm of intermediate, immediately-superseded risk FACTs; the query path computes from state on demand and live ingestion re-arms recompute, so replay stays side-effect-free. **Idempotency is the seeded dedup window, not offset precision**: `state.Store.Restore` pre-seeds each portfolio's dedup window from the persisted AppliedKeys (no per-aggregate lock, no-op-if-present — single-threaded pre-ingest, and a snapshot is never newer than the running engine), so replay can start at-or-before the true resume point and already-applied events are recognized as Seen and skipped (RPO=0, no double-count). **Resume coordinate = per-(topic,partition) MIN(offset+1) across snapshots** (`log_position` resume-at-offset+1 semantics); one `replay.Reader` per resume partition, `Range{StartOffset, EndTime: bootstrapStart}` so the read terminates and the post-`now` gap is covered by the live spine + dedup absorbing overlap. **A (topic,partition) with NO recorded position is left to the live spine, never replayed from genesis** (logged warning) — and since the 01c `LogPositionSource` is still nil, records carry nil positions today, so replay is effectively skipped until that seam is wired; the restore phase is the live half of the epic. **`eventSource` interface + `sourceFactory` field** is the test seam (default wraps `replay.NewReader`). Error discipline mirrors the EVT-20b Pipeline (`errors.As` `*MalformedFrameError` ⇒ skip; ctx-cancel/source error ⇒ abort; per-event Handler error ⇒ skip). **Main wiring**: `RISK_ENGINE_DATABASE_URL` set ⇒ pgxpool → `persist.NewPostgres` → `Bootstrap.Run` before live ingest, `go snapshotter.Run(ctx)` + `App.Checkpoint = snapshotter.Checkpoint`; unset ⇒ in-memory only. New config `RISK_ENGINE_DATABASE_URL` + `RISK_ENGINE_KAFKA_BROKERS` (comma list; empty ⇒ skip replay) · `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/bootstrap.go`
- [x] **PERS-01c** Periodic durable snapshotter — new `kanz/internal/risk/engine/snapshot.go` (`Snapshotter`), plus `state.Store.SnapshotWithKeys` + `dedupWindow.Keys()`. **`Snapshotter` materializes every known portfolio's committed state into the PERS-01b StateStore on a cadence** (`DefaultSnapshotInterval=30s`) + a final pass on shutdown — each saved `PortfolioRecord` is the durable analog of an EVT-11 PortfolioSnapshot (aggregate + positions + LogPosition + applied-key tail). **"Emission" means persisting to the durable store, NOT publishing a `domain.v1.PortfolioSnapshot` to the bus** — the Postgres StateStore IS the snapshot store the 01d bootstrap `LoadAll`s; RISK-10's `publish.Publisher` only emits exposure/measures FACTs. **Periodic, not per-apply, is the load-bearing trade**: per-apply would put the SQL write on the hot ingest path; the authoritative copy is in-memory, so the durable copy only needs to bound restart replay. **`LogPositionSource` is a deliberate seam, nil for now** — ORCH-01 ingests from NATS where the durable Kafka offset isn't on the envelope, so the engine has no coordinate to stamp; 01d/a future task wires it over the Kafka consumer's committed offsets. nil ⇒ fall back to the portfolio's own LogPosition (usually nil during live NATS ingest ⇒ record carries no resume hint ⇒ bootstrap replays from log start, still correct). **`SnapshotWithKeys` captures the clone AND the applied idempotency keys under ONE per-aggregate lock** (new `dedupWindow.Keys()` reads the bounded tail), closing the 01a/01b-flagged gap where an apply could interleave to record a key whose state effect is missing from the clone — that pairing is what makes the persisted AppliedKeys a sound pre-seed for the bootstrap dedup window. Checkpoint is fail-soft per-portfolio (`errors.Join`); `Run` logs per-tick errors but never dies on a transient store blip · `kanz/internal/risk/engine/snapshot.go`
