- [x] **PERS-01b** Postgres `StateStore` impl — new `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/postgres.go` over the EVT-16a pgxpool pattern (caller owns the pool). **Per-aggregate row locking falls out of the upsert, not an explicit `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`**: `INSERT INTO portfolios … ON CONFLICT (portfolio_id) DO UPDATE` takes a row-level lock on that portfolio's row for the tx, so two concurrent Saves for the same portfolio serialize (the second blocks at the conflicting upsert) — the durable mirror of RISK-05's in-memory per-portfolio mutex; different portfolios never contend. **Read-committed (pool default) is deliberate**, NOT the registry's Serializable: the row lock already gives per-aggregate ordering and there's no cross-aggregate invariant to protect. **Save is full-replace within one tx**: upsert the portfolio row, then `DELETE … WHERE portfolio_id` + re-insert positions and applied_keys — matches snapshot hard-reset (a position dropped from the record is removed) and keeps the durable applied-key set the same bounded-tail shape as the in-memory dedup window. **Money/Decimal ↔ BYTEA via `proto.Marshal`** with a `reflect.IsNil` guard in `encode` (the typed-nil-in-interface trap: a nil `*commonpb.Money` passed as `proto.Message` is a non-nil interface over a nil pointer); nil ⇒ SQL NULL. **LogPosition decomposed into `log_{topic,partition,offset}` columns** (topic presence = set/unset discriminator). **Zero `time.Time` ↔ SQL NULL** (`nullTime`/`derefTime`) so "no state applied yet" round-trips as NULL, not the proto Unix epoch. **LoadAll is 3 table scans grouped in memory** rather than N+1 — it's the bootstrap read (PERS-01d). No Memory backend shipped (unlike the registry) — 01e's tests need a real Postgres anyway · `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/postgres.go`
- [x] **PERS-01a** Define `StateStore` persistence interface + Postgres migration — new `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/store.go` + `kanz/services/risk-engine/migrations/0001_state.sql`, **begins the durable-state epic**. **`StateStore` is a snapshot store, not an event store**: Save/Load/LoadAll/Ping over a flat `PortfolioRecord` (the third shape after proto-wire and domain-working, same two-layer reasoning as the domain package); `FromPortfolio`/`(PortfolioRecord).ToPortfolio` are the single canonical mapping so 01b/c/d never re-derive it divergently. PortfolioRecord reuses `domain.Position` directly (already a flat value struct) rather than a parallel position type. **RPO=0 hinges on atomic Save**: aggregate fields + full position set + LogPosition + AppliedKeys persist in one per-aggregate transaction, so the snapshot's LogPosition can never claim events it didn't include — that's what makes the offset+1 resume gap-free. **Idempotency is persisted as `AppliedKeys` data on the record, not checked per-event against the DB**: bootstrap (01d) pre-seeds the in-memory dedup window from them so a replayed boundary event is skipped by the *existing* in-memory machinery — the hot apply path never touches the DB. Needed because a portfolio is fed from multiple topic-partitions, so one scalar LogPosition is not a perfect watermark; the key tail covers the boundary. **Storage**: Money/Decimal columns are marshaled `common.v1` proto BYTEA (schema-registry opaque-descriptor pattern; `double` banned for money/sizes ⇒ no lossless native SQL column); LogPosition decomposed into text/bigint columns. Migration: `portfolios` + `positions` (FK CASCADE) + `applied_keys` (FK CASCADE, `(portfolio_id, applied_at)` index for the writer's age-based prune) · `kanz/internal/risk/state/persist/store.go`, `kanz/services/risk-engine/migrations/0001_state.sql`

- [x] **ORCH-01f** End-to-end harness over an in-memory `bus.Client` — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/app_test.go`, **closes the ORCH-01 runtime epic**. Introduces `memBus`: a looping in-memory `bus.Client` that routes every `Publish` to the handlers `Subscribe`d on that subject, with async per-subscription buffered delivery so the engine's own re-entrant publish (apply→recompute→publish) can't deadlock the publisher — the first shared in-memory broker, the seam a future `pkg/bustest` could promote. Proves the **whole ORCH-01b/c/d/e composition** against one broker (a regression in any seam surfaces here, not just in the isolated unit tests). 3 tests: (1) **publish→output FACTs** — a published PortfolioState+PositionState pair flows through ingest→TriggeringApplier→Store+recompute→publish and emits both `exposure_recomputed` and `measures_computed`, with the decoded `RiskMeasureSet` carrying `GrossExposure==1000` (one USD position of 1000) — end-to-end measure correctness, not just event presence; (2) **recompute p99 latency budget** — 100 portfolios published, per-portfolio publish→emit latency matched via the output envelope's `partition_key`, p99 asserted < 500ms (generous in-memory bound that still catches an O(n) recompute regression); (3) **degraded-on-stale query** — 10m-stale state ingested through the bus, then `EngineImpl.Exposure` (ORCH-01b) over the same store flags `DEGRADED`, tying the publish-path ingestion to the query-path degraded composition. **`memBus` retention gotcha handled**: it only delivers to subscribers present at publish time, so a `waitSubscribed` gate blocks until the engine's async `Subscribe` goroutines have registered before any publish (otherwise the startup race drops the events) · `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/app_test.go`

- [x] **ORCH-01e** Graceful shutdown lifecycle — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/lifecycle.go` (`App`), plus a `Recomputer.Drain()` and a `main.go` rewrite that wires the full ORCH-01b/c/d pipeline behind the ORCH-01a readiness gate + this lifecycle. **`App.Run(ctx)` runs the ordered drain** on a shutdown signal (or ingestion failure): (1) **readiness off first** so k8s/the LB pulls the pod from rotation before teardown — in-flight work finishes, no NEW traffic arrives; (2) **stop consumers** (the signal cancels the ingest ctx; `bus.Consumer` dispatches one delivery at a time synchronously, so a returning `Subscribe` means the in-flight apply already completed — that IS the apply-drain); (3) **drain recomputes** via the new `Recomputer.Drain()` so the LAST settled state publishes instead of being dropped; (4) **checkpoint** (PERS-01 seam, `func(ctx) error`, nil ⇒ skipped); (5) **flush + close transports last** so the publisher stays usable through step 3 and Kafka batches flush on close. The whole sequence runs under a watchdog (`DefaultShutdownTimeout=20s`) so a wedged broker can't hold the pod past its terminationGracePeriod. **`Recomputer` refactored from AfterFunc-per-portfolio to a single-worker debounce loop** (dirty map of id→deadline; a worker scans deadlines, dispatches due recomputes on their own goroutines for cross-portfolio parallelism, sleeps until the nearest deadline or a nudge) — the AfterFunc model could not drain race-free because Go can't distinguish a stopped timer from one whose func already started; the worker model makes `Drain` (flush pending, exact) and `Close` (drop pending, hard-stop) both clean, each blocking on a `done` channel + an in-flight `WaitGroup`. **The recomputer's `baseCtx` is app-scoped (Background), NOT the signal ctx** — load-bearing so `Drain` publishes after the signal cancels ingestion. **`main.go` gates ingestion on `RISK_ENGINE_NATS_URL`**: set ⇒ `DialNATS` → producer/publisher + store + recomputer + `TriggeringApplier` + consumer + `App.Run`; unset ⇒ HTTP-only scaffold (probes up, no broker) so the binary still boots locally. Config gained `NATSURL` + `Source`. Tests: a focused `Recompute_DrainFlushesPending` (10s debounce that would never fire on its own; Drain flushes + returns promptly + is idempotent + Trigger-after-Drain no-ops) and an `App_FullChainThenGracefulShutdown` integration test driving bus→Ingestor→TriggeringApplier→Store+recompute→publish then a signal-driven shutdown asserting readiness-off + checkpoint-called + transport-closed-once + an exposure FACT emitted by the drain · `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/lifecycle.go`

- [x] **ORCH-01d** Recompute trigger policy — new `kanz/internal/risk/engine/recompute.go` (`Recomputer` + `TriggeringApplier`), plus the locked-read primitive the ORCH-01b note deferred to "the recompute trigger": `state.Store.Snapshot(id)` (clones the portfolio under the per-aggregate lock — a race-free point-in-time view) backed by a new `domain.Portfolio.Clone()`. **`Recomputer.Trigger(id)` debounces per portfolio** (trailing-edge, `DefaultDebounceInterval=250ms`, tunable): repeated triggers within the window collapse to one recompute fired after the burst settles — a portfolio's revalue + N position changes recompute once against the settled state instead of publishing N immediately-superseded FACTs. On fire it `Snapshot`s → `compute.ComputeExposure`/`ComputeMeasures` → `Cache.Store{Exposure,Measures}` → RISK-10 `Publisher.EmitExposure`/`EmitMeasures`. **Concurrency**: different portfolios recompute in parallel, a portfolio's own recomputes never overlap, and reads are `Snapshot` so a recompute never races a concurrent apply. (Originally one `time.AfterFunc` timer per portfolio; ORCH-01e refactored this to a single-worker debounce loop so graceful `Drain` could flush race-free — the AfterFunc model couldn't.) **`baseCtx` scopes the publish + timer lifecycle** — `Close()` stops all pending timers and blocks further triggers (ORCH-01e drain), and an in-flight recompute past the timer still updates the cache but skips the emit when `baseCtx` is canceled (publish failures are logged, not retried — the `bus.Producer` owns delivery durability and the next apply re-triggers anyway). **`publisher` may be nil** (cache-only mode for tests / read-replicas). **`TriggeringApplier` is the seam connecting ORCH-01c ingestion to recompute**: an `ingest.Applier` decorator that delegates to the inner applier (the `state.Store`) and, only on success, fires `Trigger(portfolioID)` — so `app.NewIngest(consumer, NewTriggeringApplier(store, recomputer), …)` makes applies flow to state AND fan out a debounced recompute, with a compile-time `var _ ingest.Applier` assertion. **Also retrofitted EngineImpl (ORCH-01b) to read via `Store.Snapshot`** instead of `Lookup` (Exposure/Measures/Scenario/Health) — removes the documented data race in the query path now that the locked-read boundary exists, which `go test -race` (CICD-01a) would otherwise catch. 6 tests: burst-coalesces-to-one-emit (+ cache populated for both exposure & measures), cache-only-when-no-publisher, TriggeringApplier-fires-recompute-after-apply, Close-stops-pending-timer + Trigger-after-Close-no-ops, empty-portfolio-id-ignored · `kanz/internal/risk/engine/recompute.go`

- [x] **ORCH-01c** Wire RISK-04 `Ingestor` → `state.Store` via `bus.Consumer` — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/` package (`ingest.go`), the service's composition root (risk *logic* stays in `internal/risk/*`; this package owns process concerns — subscription fan-out, lifecycle, later bootstrap + recompute). **`Ingest` wraps a `bus.Consumer` + the RISK-04 `ingest.Ingestor`** (built over the `state.Store` as `Applier`) and subscribes the three risk state subjects (`risk.portfolio.revalued` / `risk.position.changed` / `risk.portfolio.snapshot` — subject == event_type, so the Ingestor's `env.EventType` dispatch matches the subscription scope). **Per-aggregate ordering is upheld downstream, not here**: the three subjects are subscribed on independent goroutines (each `Consumer.Subscribe` blocks), so a portfolio's PortfolioState and PositionStates arrive concurrently with no cross-subject bus ordering — but `state.Store`'s per-portfolio mutex (RISK-05) serializes every apply for one portfolio regardless of delivery subject, so this wiring needs no coordination of its own (documented as the load-bearing reason concurrent subscribes are safe). **`Run(ctx)` is fail-fast**: one goroutine per subject under a derived cancelable ctx; the first non-`context.Canceled` subscribe error fires a `sync.Once` that records it and cancels the siblings, so a single broken subscription brings the ingester down rather than running silently degraded; clean ctx cancellation (graceful shutdown, ORCH-01e) returns nil. **Consumer is injected** (caller picks transport + retry/DLQ policy) so `Ingest` stays transport-agnostic and testable. **One durable consumer group** (`DefaultConsumerGroup="risk-engine"`) across all three subjects — each (subject, group) is its own durable consumer, so N replicas spread partitions within the group. 5 tests (external `app_test`, real `bus.Consumer` over a `fakeSub` that replays `bus.Producer`-framed wire bytes then blocks on ctx — exercises the true Unframe→Validate→dispatch path): events route into the store (portfolio base-currency + position landed, market value intact), nil-consumer + nil-applier rejection, clean-shutdown-returns-nil, and subscribe-failure-surfaces-and-cancels-siblings (asserts Run returns rather than hanging) · `kanz/services/risk-engine/internal/app/ingest.go`

- [x] **ORCH-01b** Implement concrete `EngineImpl` behind `risk/api/v1.Engine` — new `kanz/internal/risk/engine/` package (`engine.go`), the production form of RISK-13's `computeExposureWithFlags`. **Composes the four RISK-04..11 units**: `state.Store` (reader-side `Lookup`/`IDs`) + `compute` (pure `ComputeExposure`/`ComputeMeasures`) + `risk.Cache` (last-known-good fallback) + `risk.Detector` (staleness → `Mode`/`QualityFlags`), satisfying all four `v1.Engine` methods with a compile-time `var _ v1.Engine` assertion. **Canonical query pattern lifted into production**: try live (`Lookup` → compute → `Cache.Store` on success), fall back to the cached value on a store miss, tag via `Detector.Assess(result.AsOf())`; a true miss (neither store nor cache) → `ErrPortfolioNotFound`, empty `PortfolioID` → `ErrInvalidRequest`. The RISK-13 integration tests still pass unchanged and can now be rewritten against these methods (separate cleanup). **Three composition decisions worth keeping**: (1) **Measures always computes + caches the FULL set** (nil filter) then narrows to `req.Measures` via `filterMeasures` — caching a filtered set would let a later cache hit under a different filter serve a wrongly-narrowed result; unknown filter names are dropped (matches `ComputeMeasures`/`Lookup` miss semantics). (2) **`EvaluateScenario` has NO cache fallback** — the cache holds derived `ExposureSet`/`MeasureSet` values, not a `Portfolio` to shock, so a scenario needs live state; no portfolio ⇒ `ErrPortfolioNotFound`. Its flags are assessed against the *underlying* state's `AsOf` (a scenario on stale state is itself stale). (3) **The query path does not lock** — `Lookup` returns the live pointer and `compute` reads it through the copy-returning domain accessors; serializing a recompute against a concurrent apply is owned by the ORCH-01d recompute trigger (per-aggregate boundary), not the read path. `Health.AsOf` = max `AsOf` across all portfolios (a single live portfolio keeps the engine NORMAL); every method short-circuits on `ctx.Err()`. 13 tests (external `engine_test`, real `Detector` with now-relative `AsOf` since its clock is unexported): fresh/no-flags, stale/degraded, unknown→not-found, empty-id→invalid, repeat-query-stable, measures filter-narrows + empty-returns-all, scenario shock-projects-and-leaves-live-state-unmutated, scenario unknown→not-found, health latest-across-portfolios + no-state-degraded, cancelled-ctx · `kanz/internal/risk/engine/engine.go`

- [x] **ORCH-01a** Scaffold `risk-engine` service — new `kanz/services/risk-engine/` (`cmd/risk-engine/main.go` + `internal/config/` + `internal/server/`), the first tenant of the Platform Hardening roadmap and the second Go service after schema-registry. **Follows the schema-registry service skeleton exactly** (EVT-16a layout): `package main` entrypoint under `cmd/<name>/`, per-service `internal/` packages, stdlib `net/http` + `log/slog` JSON handler, `signal.NotifyContext` SIGINT/SIGTERM + 15s graceful `Shutdown`, env-sourced `config.Load` with per-field `envOr`/`parseLevel` fallback (prefix `RISK_ENGINE_`, default listen `:8081` so it doesn't collide with schema-registry's `:8080`). **Config is deliberately minimal** — only `Listen` + `LogLevel`; state/bus/recompute knobs (ORCH-01c/01d) and `DatabaseURL` (PERS-01) are added as those layers land, so `Load` returns no error today (no required fields yet). **The one design decision worth keeping — the readiness gate**: liveness (`/healthz`) and readiness (`/readyz`) are split per their k8s-probe meanings. schema-registry's `/readyz` pings storage; risk-engine has no storage yet, so readiness is an injectable `server.Readiness` (an `atomic.Bool` gate, starts NOT ready) rather than a dependency check. main flips it ready once the listener is up and clears it on shutdown. This is the seam ORCH-01c/01d move behind real signals (consumers subscribed + PERS-01 bootstrap-replay caught up) and ORCH-01e uses to stop a draining pod from receiving work — same lands-before-impl discipline as RISK-01/PRED-11. 2 tests: healthz-always-ok regardless of gate state, and readyz reflecting the gate across closed→ready→draining (503/200/503) · `kanz/services/risk-engine/`

### Data Integrity Layer

- [x] **DATA-11** Tests: late-data path + reconciliation correctness — new `kanz/internal/integrity/latereconcile_test.go`, 6 scenario tests; **closes the Data Integrity epic (DATA-01..11)**. Ties together the DATA-03 watermark/late primitive and the DATA-05 reconciler over simulated out-of-order + divergent streams (seeded rand, reproducible), the composition angle vs the per-call unit tests. **Late-data path (2)**: after 61 in-order events advance the frontier (watermark base+55s), a burst of 10 stragglers at event_time base+10s are all flagged Late, `MarkLate`'d, and observable via `IsLate` (the detect→route→payload-blind-tooling-sees-it path, DATA-06), and a subsequent base+61s event is still OnTime — proving the stragglers never rewound the frontier (the load-bearing DATA-03 invariant) end-to-end; a jittery feed (baseline +10s/step, ±3s jitter < 5s allowedLateness) raises zero late flags, proving normal intra-partition reordering within grace is never late. **Reconciliation correctness (3)**: a complete run with NATS-leads-then-Kafka-shuffled matches all 100 with zero discrepancies after a far-future sweep; a run where Kafka (log of record) silently drops ~10% surfaces exactly those keys as discrepancies after the deadline, each `SeenOn=nats/MissingOn=kafka`; the healthy case where the durable log lags NATS by 10s (< 30s deadline) raises NO false discrepancy on a mid-lag sweep and matches all 50 once Kafka catches up with `MatchLatency=10s` (the load-bearing "one-sided is not a discrepancy until aged" property under realistic skew). **Interplay (1)**: a LATE-flagged event still reconciles across transports (the reconciler keys on idempotency_key and is flag-blind — lateness and cross-transport completeness are orthogonal) and reconciliation never strips the LATE flag · `kanz/internal/integrity/latereconcile_test.go`

- [x] **DATA-10** Tests: gap/staleness/drift detection under simulated bad data — new `kanz/internal/integrity/baddata_test.go`, 7 scenario tests. **Distinct from the per-detector unit tests** (gap_test/staleness_test/drift_test, one classification call each): these drive a detector over a *realistic event sequence with faults injected deterministically* (seeded `math/rand`, reproducible) and assert the layer surfaces exactly the injected faults — the composition angle, RISK-12's seeded-loop approach applied to the integrity detectors. **Gap (3 tests)**: across 5 seeds, drop ~15% of sequences 2..200 (keep seq 1 so FirstSeen baselines at 1 and every later miss is a true gap) and assert `Σ MissingCount == len(dropped)` exactly — every dropped sequence accounted for, none double-counted; a clean 1..500 stream raises zero gaps; two sources writing a clean 1..N on the SAME partition_key produce no phantom gaps (the (source,event_type,partition_key) keying decision from DATA-01, exercised end-to-end rather than asserted per-call). **Staleness (2 tests)**: a feed whose ingestion lag grows 0→119s drives exactly 11 Fresh / 50 Stale / 59 Critical, pinning the exclusive-above boundaries (lag≤10 fresh, 10<lag≤60 stale, lag>60 critical) over a real sweep; a flood of out-of-order older events after the frontier advanced never rewinds the reported `LastEventTime`. **Drift (2 tests)**: a window sampled ~uniformly across the 4 bins (matching the uniform baseline) scores below threshold (not drifted), then after `Reset` a window collapsed entirely into the top bin drifts with a strictly-higher score — quiet-then-alarm over the realistic "feed starts returning a stuck/scaled value" failure; and a strongly-shifted but 20-sample window is withheld (not Drifted, not Sufficient) against MinSamples=100, proving the noise gate holds under bad-but-sparse data · `kanz/internal/integrity/baddata_test.go`

- [x] **DATA-09** Configure alerting on data-quality event thresholds — new `kanz/infra/observability/alerts/` (`data-quality.rules.yaml` + README), Prometheus alerting rules over the same `kanz_data_*` metric contract the DATA-08 dashboards render. **10 rules in 4 groups**: freshness (`DataStalenessWarning` lag>10s/2m, `DataStalenessCritical` >60s/1m), completeness (`DataSequenceGap` any-missing-in-5m, `DataReconcileDiscrepancy` any-in-10m, `DataReconcileBacklogGrowing` pending>1000/5m, `DataReconcileMatchLatencyHigh` p99>30s/5m), drift (`DataInputDriftWarning` score>threshold/10m, `DataInputDriftCritical` >2×threshold/5m), and quality-events (`DataQualityCriticalEvent` catch-all over the DATA-07 emission stream + `DataQualityMetricsMissing` exporter-liveness via `absent()`). **Thresholds mirror the detectors' own defaults** so a firing alert means "the detector would flag this": 10s/60s = `DefaultStalenessConfig`, 0.25 = `DefaultPSIThreshold`, 30s = `DefaultMatchDeadline` — no magic numbers invented. **Drift rules compare against the exported `kanz_data_drift_threshold` via `> on (feature, metric)`** so the alarm point tracks each detector's configured threshold automatically — the threshold is never duplicated between detector and rule (the one decision worth keeping: gaps/discrepancies have NO detector threshold to mirror because any loss/divergence is intrinsically critical, so those fire on `> 0`). `severity` (warning⇒ticket, critical⇒page) + `layer="data-integrity"` labels drive Alertmanager routing (README shows the route block + `promtool check rules` + the rule_files wiring). Same exporter dependency as DATA-08 — the rules target the metric surface a future exporter (wrapping the detectors or consuming the DataQualityEvent stream) must expose · `kanz/infra/observability/alerts/`

- [x] **DATA-08** Build data-observability dashboards (freshness, completeness, drift) — new `kanz/infra/observability/dashboards/` (first tenant of `kanz/infra/observability/`): three Grafana dashboard JSON models + a README. **Freshness** (`freshness.json`, DATA-02): max/streams-stale stats + staleness-lag and stale-frontier-age timeseries by subject/partition. **Completeness** (`completeness.json`, DATA-01 + DATA-05): missing-events + reconcile-discrepancies + pending stats, gap-rate and discrepancy (seen-vs-missing) bar timeseries, NATS↔Kafka match-latency p50/p99 (histogram_quantile), pending-by-transport. **Drift** (`drift.json`, DATA-04): features-over-threshold + max-score + drift-events stats, per-feature score timeseries, and a current-score-vs-threshold table. All three query a Prometheus datasource via a `datasource` template var + a free-text `subject`/`feature` regex filter; schemaVersion 39. **The README documents the metric contract** — `kanz_data_staleness_lag_seconds`, `kanz_data_last_event_age_seconds`, `kanz_data_gap_missing_total`, `kanz_data_reconcile_pending`, `kanz_data_reconcile_discrepancies_total`, `kanz_data_reconcile_match_latency_seconds` (histogram), `kanz_data_drift_score`, `kanz_data_drift_threshold`, `kanz_data_quality_events_total{kind,severity,subject}` — with label values reusing the detector vocabulary (subject = upstream stream; kind ∈ gap/staleness/drift; transport/seen_on/missing_on ∈ nats/kafka). **Detection-lands-before-instrumentation**: the detectors are pure in-memory classifiers and DATA-07 emits events (not Prometheus metrics) today, so the dashboards (and DATA-09 alerts) define the stable metric surface a thin exporter — wrapping the detectors or consuming the DataQualityEvent stream — must expose; wiring that exporter is a separate task, the same discipline as RISK-01's interface-before-impl. Provisioned via a Grafana file-provider (README shows the provider YAML) or hand-imported. The `data.market_stream`/`data.feature` Kafka topics carrying DATA-07's events already exist (EVT-09) · `kanz/infra/observability/dashboards/`

- [x] **DATA-07** Emit data-quality events (gap, staleness, drift) to the bus — new `kanz/internal/integrity/publish.go`. **`Publisher` is the emit half of the layer's detect-then-emit split** (the counterpart to the DATA-01/02/04 detectors and the DATA-06 flag emitter): `EmitGap`/`EmitStaleness`/`EmitDrift` translate a detector result into an `observation.v1.DataQualityEvent` (EVT-14's purpose-built schema) and publish via a shared `bus.Producer` (RISK-10 pattern — producer owned by the orchestrator so producer_sequence stays monotonic; DATA-07 owns translation only, not WHEN-to-emit). **Event-type = `data.{entity}.{kind}_detected`** (subject-taxonomy §1: `data.market_stream.gap_detected`, `data.feature.drift_detected`); the `{entity}` segment classifies the monitored stream and is **orthogonal to the kind** (staleness can hit a market_stream or a feature), so it's a per-emit caller parameter, not derived. **Crucial subject distinction**: the `DataQualityEvent.subject` FIELD names the upstream stream the problem was found on (e.g. `market.equity.trade`), NOT the `data.*` subject the report itself flows on. **OBSERVATION class, FACT-grade**: envelope `EVENT_CLASS_OBSERVATION` (so the producer auto-stamps idempotency_key = event_id); the must-not-shed/durable-retention property is a bus-topology policy, not the envelope class. **Result→detail mapping**: gap → `GapDetail{producer_source=Key.Source, expected=MissingFrom, received=Sequence, missing_count=MissingCount()}` with envelope partition_key = affected partition; staleness → `StalenessDetail{observed_lag, threshold, last_event_time}`; drift → `DriftDetail{feature, metric, score, threshold, window}` with **empty partition_key** (window-wide problem, so the auto-stamped producer_sequence is 0 and Validate's empty-pk⇒seq-0 rule holds) and subject = the feature name. **Each Emit guards its precondition** (StatusGap / Stale() / Drifted) and rejects with an error rather than re-detecting — the Publisher reports detected problems, it does not classify. **Severity is an explicit per-emit parameter** (rejected if UNSPECIFIED) because triage level is deployment/alerting policy (DATA-09), not a detector output. **Injectable clock** (`NewPublisherWithClock`) stamps event_time = detection time, deterministic in tests (DATA-05 convention). 9 tests cover nil-producer rejection, all three happy paths (envelope passes `bus.Validate` + correct event_type/domain/class + detail round-trip + clock-stamped event_time + the gap/drift partition_key contracts), the three precondition rejections (non-gap status, fresh staleness, not-drifted), and empty-entity + unspecified-severity rejection (nothing published) · `kanz/internal/integrity/publish.go`

- [x] **DATA-06** Wire `quality_flags` emission across ingestion + producers — new `kanz/internal/integrity/flags.go`. **`Mark(env, flag) bool` + `Has(env, flag) bool`** are the consolidated flag-stamping primitive: idempotent, nil-safe, order-preserving (the package convention previously hand-rolled in DATA-03's `MarkLate` and `replay.StampReplayed`). This is the **producer/ingestion-side half of the detect-then-emit split** the whole layer is built on — the DATA-01..05 detectors classify a condition, the producer/ingestor that owns the envelope stamps the matching flag here, so payload-blind tooling (DATA-07 reporting, observability, any consumer) reads the condition off `quality_flags` without re-running detection or parsing the payload. **Two flags are deliberately not stampable**: `QUALITY_FLAG_REPLAYED` (owned exclusively by replay tooling and forbidden on the live path by `bus.Validate` — stamping it from producers would let a live event masquerade as replayed and corrupt the live/replay boundary) and `QUALITY_FLAG_UNSPECIFIED` (the zero value); both are no-op-returning-false rather than errors so a misrouted stamp never aborts an otherwise-valid publish. **Five intent-revealing wrappers** for the producer/ingestion-settable conditions — `MarkSynthetic`/`MarkBackfilled`/`MarkDegraded`/`MarkRevised`/`MarkShed` — each a thin call to `Mark` so call sites read as the condition and a rename surfaces as a compile error (same alert-stability discipline as PRED-04's named degraded-reason constants). **DATA-03's `MarkLate`/`IsLate` refactored to delegate** to `Mark`/`Has` (lateness is still detected in watermark.go, but the stamp mechanism is now centralized). **Wiring choice — flags live in the integrity layer, not baked into `bus.Producer`**: the generic transport library must not depend on the integrity layer (a layering inversion), so ingestion/producer-orchestration code calls these helpers before publish; the concrete ingestion services are still stubs, so DATA-06 ships the canonical emission API they will call. 7 tests cover add-once/idempotent, order-preserving flag preservation, REPLAYED + UNSPECIFIED rejection, nil-safety, all five named wrappers, and MarkLate-still-works-after-delegation · `kanz/internal/integrity/flags.go`

- [x] **DATA-05** Implement NATS-vs-Kafka reconciliation for correctness-sensitive consumers — new `kanz/internal/integrity/reconcile.go`. **`Reconciler.Observe(transport, env) → ReconcileResult`** matches the same logical event delivered over the NATS live spine (EVT-08, hot path) against the Kafka log of record (EVT-09), so a correctness-sensitive consumer reading the fast path can be told when the durable log diverged. **Identity = `idempotency_key`, not `event_id`** — load-bearing: it's the one identifier guaranteed byte-identical across both publishes of a logical event and is already the broker dedup key (EVT-17d: rides `Nats-Msg-Id` on NATS, a user header on Kafka; == `event_id` for FACTs). **Four statuses**: `NotApplicable` (nil env / empty idempotency_key / `TransportUnspecified` — touches no state), `Pending` (first sight on this transport; awaiting the other — explicitly NOT a discrepancy yet, the two paths have independent latencies and the spine normally leads the log), `Matched` (the counterpart was already pending → confirmed on both; clears the entry, reports `FirstTransport` + `MatchLatency` = wall-clock gap between sightings, a not-lost-but-badly-lagging signal), `Duplicate` (same-side redelivery before the counterpart — keeps the original `firstSeen` so the deadline still measures from true first sight, doesn't add a second entry). **`Sweep() → []Discrepancy`** returns every pending event aged past `MatchDeadline` (default 30s, must exceed normal NATS→Kafka skew so a healthy lag never false-positives) carrying `SeenOn`/`MissingOn`/`Age`, and removes it — **report-once** (mirrors the gap detector advancing its mark); a later straggler on the missing side re-enters as Pending. **Boundary is above the deadline** (age==deadline is not yet a discrepancy — same off-by-one discipline as RISK-11 / staleness). **Match-and-forget**: on a match the key is dropped (can't remember forever), so the reconciler must be fed the POST-dedup stream on each side (each key ~once per transport); documented. **Injectable clock** via `NewReconcilerWithClock` (codebase convention, cf. PRED-07's `NewSyncClientWithStub`) so the deadline timing is testable from the external `integrity_test` package. Detection-only (DATA-06 wires flags, DATA-07 emits the DataQualityEvent), in-memory, per-process, concurrent-safe, re-baselines on restart — the fifth integrity primitive (gap/staleness/watermark/drift/reconcile). 11 tests cover NATS-first + Kafka-first match, MatchLatency-from-first-sighting, same-side duplicate (no second entry) + duplicate-keeps-original-deadline, unmatched-ages-into-discrepancy + boundary-at-deadline, report-once, matched-never-sweeps, independent-keys-isolation, three not-applicable inputs, and non-positive-deadline fallback · `kanz/internal/integrity/reconcile.go`

- [x] **DATA-04** Implement input distribution drift detection — new `kanz/internal/integrity/drift.go`. **`PSIDetector`** (one per feature) bins observed feature values over the current window and scores them against a fixed baseline distribution via **PSI (Population Stability Index)** — the canonical input-drift metric. `Observe(value)` accumulates into bins (via `sort.SearchFloat64s` over the configured edges → len(edges)+1 bins); `Assess(windowStart, windowEnd) → DriftResult` computes `PSI = Σ (actual_i − expected_i)·ln(actual_i/expected_i)` and is non-destructive; `Reset()` rolls the window. **DriftResult maps onto `observation.v1.DriftDetail`** (DATA-07 emit target): feature/metric/score/threshold/window_start/window_end. **`DriftMetric` is a string label + the result shape is metric-agnostic** so KL-divergence / KS-statistic detectors can plug in later carrying their own label without changing the result contract. **Three correctness decisions**: (1) **epsilon flooring** (`1e-6`) on both actual and expected bin proportions so an empty/collapsed bin can't produce `ln(0)`/NaN/Inf — a fully-collapsed distribution scores high-but-finite and drifts; (2) **`MinSamples` sufficiency gate** — PSI on a handful of samples is noise, so a window below MinSamples is never `Drifted` regardless of score (`Sufficient` reported separately, `SampleCount` exposed so the caller/DATA-07 can gate); (3) **`Drifted = Sufficient && Score > Threshold`** with `DefaultPSIThreshold=0.25` (the conventional "significant shift" cutoff: <0.1 negligible, 0.1–0.25 moderate, >0.25 alarm). **Baseline normalized to proportions in the constructor** (caller passes counts or proportions); validation rejects feature-empty, baseline/edges length mismatch (`len(Baseline)==len(Edges)+1`), <2 bins, non-ascending edges, negative/zero-mass baseline. Caller owns window timing (the detector tracks samples, not wall clock); concurrent-safe, in-memory per-process — the fourth detector in the package (gap/staleness/watermark/drift), detect-then-emit split preserved. 11 tests cover constructor validation matrix, default threshold, identical-to-baseline ~0 PSI, a hand-computed known PSI (60/40 vs 50/50 ≈ 0.0405), significant-shift drift, empty-bin no-NaN, insufficient-samples-never-drifts, zero-samples, Reset, window/metadata propagation, and multi-bin placement · `kanz/internal/integrity/drift.go`

- [x] **DATA-03** Implement watermarking + late-data routing (`LATE` flag) — new `kanz/internal/integrity/watermark.go`. **`WatermarkTracker.Observe(env) → WatermarkResult`** maintains a per-stream event-time watermark = `(max event_time seen) - AllowedLateness` and classifies each event. **Four statuses**: `Unknown` (nil env / missing event_time), `FirstSeen` (baseline — seeds the watermark, can't be late with no prior watermark), `OnTime` (event_time ≥ watermark — advances the frontier if newer), `Late` (event_time < watermark — arrived after the consumer provably moved past its event-time window; `QUALITY_FLAG_LATE`). **`AllowedLateness` is the bounded-out-of-orderness grace**: an event up to that far behind the frontier is still on-time (normal intra-partition reordering); older than that is late. **Late events never pull the watermark backward** (the load-bearing invariant — otherwise one straggler would re-open the late window for everything after it) and are flagged-not-dropped (the layer never black-holes). Boundary is inclusive at the watermark (event_time == watermark is OnTime, not late). **Distinct from DATA-02**: staleness = a single event's ingestion lag (`ingestion_time - event_time`, source freshness); lateness = an event's position vs the stream's *event-time progress* (ordering/window-completeness). Same `frontierKey` (event_type, partition_key) keying as DATA-02. **`MarkLate(env)` / `IsLate(env)` flag helpers** mirror EVT-20c's `StampReplayed`/`IsReplayed` — idempotent, nil-safe, preserve existing flags; this is the concrete "route late data" primitive DATA-06 wires into producers so payload-blind tooling (DATA-05 reconciliation, DATA-07 reporting) sees LATE without re-running watermark logic. Detection-only, concurrent-safe, in-memory per-process (re-baselines on restart). 11 tests cover first-seen seeding, advancing on-time, within-grace + at-watermark-boundary on-time, late + Lateness math + Watermark value, late-doesn't-move-frontier-backward, per-subject/partition isolation, non-positive-lateness default fallback, nil/missing-event_time unknown, and MarkLate idempotency + flag-preservation + nil-safety · `kanz/internal/integrity/watermark.go`

- [x] **DATA-02** Implement staleness monitoring (`event_time` vs `ingestion_time`) — new `kanz/internal/integrity/staleness.go`. **`StalenessMonitor.Observe(env) → StalenessResult`** computes per-event ingestion lag = `ingestion_time - event_time` (the source-to-edge latency signal per envelope.proto §event_time) and classifies it. **Five levels**: `Unknown` (nil env or missing event_time/ingestion_time — both Required, but a defensive monitor reports unknown rather than fabricating a lag from an epoch-zero `AsTime()` on a nil Timestamp), `Fresh` (lag ≤ WarnAfter), `Stale` (WarnAfter < lag ≤ CriticalAfter), `Critical` (lag > CriticalAfter), and `Future` (event_time ahead of ingestion_time beyond SkewTolerance — clock skew / bad source timestamp; the *opposite* of staleness, classified distinctly rather than silently read as fresh). **Boundaries exclusive-above** (lag==threshold is the lower level — RISK-11's "above the budget" discipline). **Result maps directly onto `observation.v1.StalenessDetail`** (DATA-07 emit target): `Lag`→observed_lag, `Threshold`→threshold (the *exceeded* one — WarnAfter for Stale, CriticalAfter for Critical; 0 for Fresh/Unknown/Future), `LastEventTime`→last_event_time. **Tracks the stale frontier** — the running max event_time per (subject=event_type, partition_key), updated each Observe; an out-of-order older event does NOT move it backwards. Frontier keyed by an unexported `frontierKey` (the package's exported `StreamKey` from DATA-01 has a `Source` the staleness frontier doesn't use). **`StalenessConfig` with per-field default fallback** (override one threshold without restating the rest); defaults WarnAfter=10s/CriticalAfter=60s/SkewTolerance=1s are conservative placeholders — staleness tolerance is per-source (tick feed sub-second vs EOD batch minutes), deployment-tuned. **`Stale()` helper** = Stale||Critical, the cases DATA-07 turns into events. Detection-only; concurrent-safe (mutex over frontier map); in-memory per-process, re-baselines on restart — same shape + detect-then-emit split as DATA-01. 12 tests cover fresh/stale/critical, boundary exclusivity, future-dated + small-skew-tolerated-as-fresh, nil-env + missing-timestamp unknowns, frontier advance + no-backward-move + per-subject/partition isolation, zero-field config fallback, and result subject/partition propagation · `kanz/internal/integrity/staleness.go`

- [x] **DATA-01** Implement `producer_sequence` gap detection — new `kanz/internal/integrity/` package (first tenant of the data-integrity layer), `gap.go`. **`GapDetector.Observe(env) → GapResult`** tracks the high-water `producer_sequence` per stream and classifies each observation. **Stream key = (source, event_type, partition_key)** — load-bearing: the Producer (EVT-17b) keeps a 1-based counter per (event_type, partition_key) and each Producer instance carries one `source`, so a sequence is only comparable within all three. Two sources writing the same partition_key each run their own 1,2,3…; keying on partition_key alone (as envelope.proto's stale "per-(source, partition_key)" comment suggests) would interleave them and manufacture phantom gaps. The implementation is authoritative over the proto comment. **Six statuses**: `NotApplicable` (seq==0 — producer didn't sequence, e.g. no partition_key; also nil envelope), `FirstSeen` (baseline — a first observation of seq>1 is NOT a gap because a consumer legitimately starts mid-stream via late join / partition reassignment / replay window; the caller inspects `Sequence` if it bootstraps from 1), `OK` (seq==prev+1), `Gap` (seq>prev+1, with `MissingFrom..MissingTo` inclusive + `MissingCount()`), `Duplicate` (seq==prev, redelivery — does NOT advance the mark), `Regression` (seq<prev, out-of-order/stale redelivery — flagged because per-partition ordering (event-class-rules §1) says it shouldn't happen; mark held). **Gap advances the high-water mark** so it's reported exactly once, then contiguous sequences resume OK. **In-memory, per-process, concurrent-safe** (sync.Mutex over the per-key map): reports gaps in the stream this detector sees from first event; re-baselines on restart via FirstSeen; cross-instance reconciliation is a downstream concern (DATA-07 emits per-detector findings). Detection-only — wiring `quality_flags` (DATA-06) and emitting data-quality events (DATA-07) are later tasks. 11 tests cover baseline+contiguous, gap range + single-missing + report-once-then-resume, duplicate-doesn't-advance, regression-holds-mark, stream isolation across source/event_type/partition_key, seq-0 + nil not-applicable, and concurrent multi-stream correctness · `kanz/internal/integrity/gap.go`

### Go ↔ Python Prediction Layer

- [x] **PRED-14** Tests: streaming worker backpressure (consumer-lag scaling) — new `kanz-py/tests/test_streaming_backpressure.py`, 6 tests. **The streaming worker (PRED-04) has no explicit backpressure code — it's structural** in how the worker is driven: `NATSClient.subscribe` does `fetch(batch=1)` → `await handler` → `ack`, so a slow model bounds in-flight to 1 per worker and unprocessed events pile up server-side as consumer lag (the signal to scale out by adding workers to the durable group). Tests model that delivery contract with a shared backlog `asyncio.Queue` (stand-in for the durable group — each event handed to exactly one worker via `get_nowait`) + a `ConcurrencyGate` (a shared in-flight counter + `asyncio.Event` release) so concurrency is asserted **deterministically with no sleeps** — the gated model blocks in `predict`, the test polls until the expected in-flight count, asserts, then releases. **6 tests**: (1) single worker holds the backlog while processing one event — pulls exactly one, the other 4 wait in the queue as lag, nothing published until predict completes (the core backpressure property); (2) publish-before-pull lockstep — one predict ↔ one publish, `max_in_flight==1`; (3) **scaling**: 3 workers sharing one backlog reach `in_flight==3` (adding workers raises aggregate concurrency — how lag is worked down); (4) the group delivers each of 20 events exactly once across 4 workers (no loss, no dup — `subject_id`s unique and complete); (5) single worker preserves per-subject ordering (the sequential loop is what guarantees the per-`subject_id` ordering the engine relies on end-to-end); (6) **backpressure delays but never degrades** — a slow model yields a NORMAL prediction once it returns, contrasting the streaming path's block-and-lag with the interactive servicer's (PRED-08) admission-control load-shedding (DEGRADED + `pool_saturated`). **Dev-env note**: this is the first task run with a working `kanz-py` test environment — generated the Python schema SDK via `buf generate` (remote `protocolbuffers/python`+`pyi` plugins) into the gitignored `gen/python`, importable as PEP-420 namespace packages via `PYTHONPATH`, and installed `pytest`/`pytest-asyncio`/`protobuf`/`nats-py`/`aiokafka`. Confirmed PRED-11's 18 feature-store tests (which had never run) pass. **Surfaced pre-existing breakage (out of scope, flagged)**: `tests/test_consumer.py` (EVT-18d) has 16 stale tests constructing `Message(body=...)` without the required `subject` positional arg → TypeError; and `tests/test_interactive_servicer.py` (PRED-08) needs `grpcio` + a generated python gRPC stub (not produced by the `protocolbuffers/python` plugin alone). Both predate PRED-14 and are unrelated to it · `kanz-py/tests/test_streaming_backpressure.py`

- [x] **PRED-13** Tests: degraded-mode fallback + sync timeout/circuit-breaker — new `kanz/internal/prediction/resilience_test.go` (`package prediction` internal-test, so it can inject the `CircuitBreaker.now` clock — same time-mocking approach as RISK-13's `Detector.now`) + corrections to 3 PRED-07 tests in `sync_client_test.go`. **Unblocked by fixing a real pipeline gap**: `kanz/internal/prediction` never compiled because `buf.gen.yaml` had only `protoc-gen-go` (message types), no `protoc-gen-go-grpc`, so `inference/v1` carried no `InferenceServiceClient` stub — PRED-06/07 referenced a type that was never generated. Added `protoc-gen-go-grpc` (local plugin, `paths=source_relative`) to `buf.gen.yaml`, regenerated → `inference_service_grpc.pb.go` now exists; the sync path builds end-to-end for the first time. **Latent test bugs surfaced**: with the package finally compiling, 3 PRED-07 tests failed — they asserted the live trigger reason (`inference_unavailable`/`inference_timeout`/`circuit_open`) on the cache-MISS fallback path, but the documented contract (sync_client.go `fallback` comment + BRAIN + PRED-04's Python `_fallback`) is **cache hit → trigger reason; cache miss → `no_cached_prediction`** (the trigger stays observable via the returned `err`). Corrected the 3 to assert `no_cached_prediction` on the miss path (+ the timeout one now also asserts `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)`); renamed two to `…NoCache…` for honesty. **7 new tests**: (1) **cache-hit × trigger-reason matrix** — timeout-with-cache-hit reports `inference_timeout` while serving the cached value, open-circuit-with-cache-hit reports `circuit_open` without hitting the wire — the reason mapping the no-cache tests structurally cannot reach; the circuit-open case also asserts the **trust-laundering guard** (a cached NORMAL is re-served as DEGRADED, never NORMAL, per PRED-02 §1); (2) **feature-translation failure** (a `FeatureValue{}` with unspecified Kind) returns DEGRADED and never calls the stub, mirroring the empty-SubjectID validation path; (3) **no-cache fallback propagates the request `as_of`** + subject_id; (4) **deterministic circuit-breaker timing** via injected `now`: failed half-open probe re-opens AND resets the cooldown window (next probe waits another full cooldown — documented behavior previously unreachable without controlling the clock), cooldown boundary is exclusive (`Sub > cooldown`: at exactly cooldown still open, 1ns past → half-open — RISK-11's "above the budget" discipline), and a success below threshold resets the consecutive-failure count so interrupted failures never accumulate to open · `kanz/internal/prediction/resilience_test.go`, `kanz/internal/prediction/sync_client_test.go`, `kanz-schemas/buf.gen.yaml`

- [x] **PRED-12** Contract tests: feature/prediction schema versioning — new `kanz/test/contract/inference/schema_versioning_test.go` (`package inference_test`, external — exercises only the public `inferencepb` + `pkg/bus` surface like the other EVT-21/PRED contract trees). Proves the runtime invariants the prediction layer's versioning + cross-path design depend on, for the two PRED-01 payloads (FeatureVector in, PredictionEnvelope out), complementing the static gates (`buf breaking` EVT-07 + CODEOWNERS review on `proto/inference/`) which can't prove live-binding behavior. **Six contract areas, 9 tests**: (1) **version-identifier conventions** — `feature_set_ref` is `"{name}:{version}"`, `model` is `"{name}@{version}"`; the distinct separators are the routing-layer's load-bearing signal (PRED-03/04 routing, PRED-09 lookup), asserted to `strings.Cut` cleanly and to NOT cross-contaminate (`:` never in model, `@` never in feature_set_ref) + round-trip byte-stable across marshal (routing happens after the payload crosses the bus/gRPC boundary) + the PRED-05 `feature_vector_event_id` join key survives; (2) **forward compat** — an unknown field (tag 1000, outside 19000–19999 reserved, EVT-21c's choice) appended to FeatureVector + to PredictionEnvelope survives unmarshal + re-marshal (replay re-marshals payloads, so a dropped field corrupts replay of newer-schema features — the determinism property PRED-06 extends across paths); (3) **forward-compat of a future FeatureValue oneof arm** — an unknown oneof tag (99, not 1–4) reads as unset `Value` but its bytes survive re-marshal, so quant-ml can add a new value type (e.g. sparse-vector) without a consumer flag-day; (4) **backward compat** — minimal v0-shaped FeatureVector (no source_event_ids/values) + NORMAL PredictionEnvelope (no class_label/explanation/degraded_reason) decode with absent fields at zero; (5) **oneof tagged-union discrimination** — scalar/ordinal/categorical/vector round-trip preserving their Go type, pinning PRED-01's "a categorical can never be misread as a scalar magnitude"; (6) **open-enum semantics** — a future PredictionMode value (3) is preserved as its raw number (not clamped) AND must not decode as NORMAL, enforcing PRED-02 §4's "non-NORMAL ⇒ DEGRADED" so a current consumer in front of a newer producer never acts on unrecognised full-trust; (7) **cross-path payload identity (PRED-06)** — the same FeatureVector marshaled as a sync gRPC body equals the payload recovered from a streaming EventFrame via `bus.Unframe`, and decodes back `proto.Equal`, proving a bus-logged feature can be replayed into the sync RPC. **Infra note**: required regenerating `kanz-schemas/gen/go` (the committed-locally Go SDK was stale — missing the `inference/v1` package entirely) via a Go-only `buf generate` template + `go mod init/tidy`; surfaced a pre-existing gap that `buf.gen.yaml` has no `protoc-gen-go-grpc` plugin, so `inference_service.pb.go` carries no `InferenceServiceClient` stub and `kanz/internal/prediction` (PRED-07 sync_client.go) does not build — out of PRED-12 scope (this test targets only the message schemas) but flagged for a follow-up to wire go-grpc into the release pipeline · `kanz/test/contract/inference/`

- [x] **PRED-11** Define feature-store boundary interface (trivial first impl) — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/featurestore/` package, `store.py` + `__init__.py`. **`FeatureStore` Protocol + `InMemoryFeatureStore` trivial impl**. Lands-before-impl (same pattern as PRED-04's `Model`/`PredictionPublisher`, RISK-04's `Applier`): both inference paths today already receive a full `FeatureVector` (streaming decodes it from `inference.feature.computed`; interactive gets it as the gRPC body), so nothing consumes the store yet — the boundary is fixed first so a real store (Feast/Redis/point-in-time warehouse) plugs in behind it without touching callers and PRED-12's schema-versioning contract tests have a target. **Point-in-time `get` is the load-bearing decision**: `get(subject_id, feature_set_ref, as_of)` returns the vector effective AT OR BEFORE `as_of`, never a later one. This is the single property separating a feature store from a cache — a "latest wins" store would silently leak future data into replay/backtest (breaking EVT-21d-style determinism) and create training/serving skew. `as_of=None` ⇒ most recent; `as_of` before all stored marks ⇒ `None` (no future leakage), and the caller treats a miss as missing-features → DEGRADED per PRED-02, NOT zero features. **Keyed by `(subject_id, feature_set_ref)` jointly** — a subject carries features under several feature sets at once (different models read different sets), so feature_set_ref is part of identity not just routing; same routing key as the registry + worker. **Async-everywhere** to match the eventual remote-store contract + the `Model` protocol; the trivial impl never awaits, so lock-free under the single event loop (same reasoning as PRED-04's `LastKnownCache`). **Ordering via `(seconds, nanos)` tuple** (proto Timestamps aren't directly orderable) + `bisect` over a parallel sorted keys list: `put` inserts keeping history sorted (out-of-order puts still resolve correctly), `bisect_right` on the target finds the rightmost at-or-before entry. **`put` idempotent-by-as_of**: same as_of overwrites (re-materialise one point in time), new as_of extends history. **Defensive copy on both sides** (`CopyFrom`) so caller mutation can't bleed into stored state or vice versa — RISK-03 immutability discipline. **Trivial impl deliberately omits**: persistence, TTL/eviction, online/offline split, distributed state (process-local, like the PRED-09 registry) — each is a real-store concern behind the same contract. **pyproject needs no change** — `kanz_inference*` already auto-discovers the new subpackage. 18 tests cover protocol shape, put/get round-trip, miss + unknown-feature-set miss, point-in-time (between-marks returns earlier not latest, exact-match inclusive, before-all returns None, nanos ordering, out-of-order put correctness), same-as_of overwrite, subject + feature-set isolation, defensive copy both directions, and missing subject_id/feature_set_ref/as_of rejection · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/featurestore/`, `kanz-py/tests/test_feature_store.py`

- [x] **PRED-10** Implement shadow / canary inference path — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/shadow/`. **`ShadowExecutor` satisfies Model protocol**, wrapping PRED-09's Registry: routes inbound features to primary + shadows by `fv.feature_set_ref`. Workers (PRED-04/PRED-08) construct one ShadowExecutor per process and use it as their Model — no worker-side awareness of shadows needed. **`model_id = "shadow-executor"` composite name** — single ShadowExecutor handles many feature_sets, so no single canonical id; per-response `PredictionEnvelope.model` carries the actual primary model_id for traceability. **Shadows run AFTER primary, not in parallel — load-bearing decision**: if shadows ran via asyncio.gather, response latency would be bounded by slowest task, defeating canary's "risk-free evaluation" premise. Awaiting primary first then firing shadows via `asyncio.create_task` (no await) keeps primary latency budget unaffected by shadow throughput. Trade-off: a shadow depending on something the primary's execution side-effected (cache warmup, GPU pre-alloc) sees different state — acceptable because such coupling indicates a stateful Model, itself a correctness concern PRED-04/08's per-call contract doesn't permit. **Shadow failure isolation**: each shadow's `predict` + each observer's `observe` are wrapped in try/except in `_run_shadow_and_observe`; exceptions logged but never re-raised. By the time a shadow can fail, the primary's response has been returned — no path for shadow exception to affect consumer. **Task tracking via `_shadow_tasks` set + `add_done_callback`** prevents GC from reclaiming fire-and-forget tasks before they complete; also enables `wait_for_shadows()` for graceful shutdown and deterministic test completion. **`ShadowObserver` Protocol with `@runtime_checkable`**: async `observe(fv, primary, primary_pred, shadow, shadow_pred)`. Async to allow remote sinks (metrics push, database write) without blocking the shadow path. Default `LoggingShadowObserver` logs each pair at INFO with delta — fine for low-volume canary; high-volume production swaps for a metrics-store observer. **No primary registered for feature_set_ref → DEGRADED response** with `inference_unavailable` reason (PRED-02 §2.1 reused identifier from PRED-04/08). 12 tests cover constructor nil rejection, primary-only happy path, no-primary degraded, shadow fan-out to same fv, observer records pairs, shadow exception isolated, observer exception isolated, no-shadows no-observer-calls, shadows-after-primary ordering (slow shadow doesn't block primary), routing by feature_set_ref, LoggingShadowObserver smoke + protocol compliance · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/shadow/`

- [x] **PRED-09** Implement model versioning + artifact registry — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/registry/` package. **`ModelMetadata` frozen dataclass** carries the four contract fields: `model_id` (`"{name}@{version}"`, same shape as PredictionEnvelope.model + DecisionLog.decider), `feature_set_ref` (`"{name}:{version}"`, the routing key matching FeatureVector.feature_set_ref), `confidence_threshold` (per PRED-02 §2.3 — per-MODEL, not global; uncalibrated models pick 1.0 so every prediction marks DEGRADED), `artifact_uri` (traceability only; registry doesn't fetch). Frozen so it's safely hashable + can be a dict key in higher-level structures. **`Registry` with explicit primary/shadow distinction at registration time** — load-bearing for PRED-10's shadow/canary flow. Alternative ("single map, caller picks the right one") would spread routing policy across every caller; explicit primary/shadow at `register(metadata, model, primary=True/False)` concentrates policy in one place and gives workers two intent-revealing lookup methods (`primary_for_feature_set`, `shadows_for_feature_set`). **Three indexes maintained**: `_by_id` (canonical lookup), `_primary_for_fs` (1:1 per feature_set), `_shadows_for_fs` (1:N per feature_set). Redundancy keeps both lookups O(1). **Re-registering an existing model_id replaces the entry** (hot-reload pattern); `_remove_from_role_indexes` scrubs old role so a "primary → shadow" demotion is consistent. **`ModelLoader` Protocol** for pluggable artifact loading — registry stores constructed Model instances; how artifact_uri becomes a Model is a deployment concern (pickle/ONNX/torchscript/remote-server differ wildly). Protocol exists so loaders plug in without changing the registry contract. **Validation at register time**: model_id must contain `@`, feature_set_ref must contain `:`, both non-empty, model non-nil. Strict parsing deferred but format hint enforced. **What the registry deliberately does NOT do**: artifact loading (deferred to ModelLoader plugin); lifecycle (warmup, LRU eviction — long-running worker keeps all registered, PRED-09b can add if memory pressure becomes real); distributed state (process-local — each worker has its own; cross-process coordination is config management + rolling restarts, not runtime registry). 14 tests cover register+lookup round-trip, miss, primary lookup, shadow collection, latest-wins primary displacement, promote-shadow-to-primary rollout pattern, list_models sorted, unregister removes from all indexes + returns true/false, all validation rejections (empty/no-@/no-:/nil model), ModelMetadata hashability · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/registry/`

- [x] **PRED-08** Implement isolated interactive inference worker pool + admission control — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/interactive/servicer.py` (gRPC servicer + admission control + `serve` helper). **`InferenceServicer` subclasses `InferenceServiceServicer`** (generated from PRED-06 proto) and implements `Predict(FeatureVector) → PredictionEnvelope` on the grpc-aio async path. Reuses PRED-04's `Model` protocol — same model artifact serves both streaming and interactive paths. **Counter-based admission control, not Semaphore**: asyncio Semaphore's `acquire()` blocks indefinitely, defeating "fail fast on overload"; counter-with-immediate-reject gives the exact "in_flight ≥ max ⇒ reject now" semantic admission control needs. Race-free under asyncio (single event loop, no await between read and increment). **`pool_saturated` added to PRED-02 §2.1** as a fourth identifier under "inference unavailable" trigger (additive doc edit). Distinct from generic `inference_unavailable` so observability can distinguish "overload" from "downstream failure" — same alert-stability discipline as the other PRED-02 identifiers. **Process isolation from streaming**: per KANZ_BRAIN anti-decision "market ingestion, AI inference, batch compute must be process-isolated". Interactive servicer runs in its own process; admission rejections show up as DEGRADED responses (not bus consumer-lag), preserving the isolation contract that streaming bursts don't starve interactive. **Cancellation propagates** (asyncio.CancelledError re-raised, NOT caught and synthesised) — when client deadline expires, gRPC framework cancels the server coroutine and the client (PRED-07) sees DEADLINE_EXCEEDED → translates to `inference_timeout`. Adding a server-side timeout would race the client deadline. **`finally` decrements in_flight** so cancellation + exceptions both release the slot. **Server-side timeout deliberately NOT enforced**: PRED-07's client deadline is the canonical timeout; adding a server-side one creates a race. If client doesn't set a deadline (a bug), server runs to completion — surfaced in observability, not silently capped. **`serve(model, address, max_in_flight) → server`** is a small helper for the common case: construct grpc.aio.Server, register servicer, bind, start. Caller awaits `server.wait_for_termination()`. **`grpcio>=1.62`** added to pyproject (first grpc dep). **DEFAULT_MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 32** — conservative for a single Python worker on typical container size; tune per deployment based on observed p99 × target QPS. 11 tests cover constructor validation, happy-path predict, in_flight increments/decrements correctly on success + exception + cancellation, model exception → DEGRADED inference_unavailable with would-have-been model_id, admission rejection at saturation (model.predict NOT called for rejected request), admission recovers after slot frees, cancellation propagates, AsOf propagated through degraded response · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/interactive/`, `kanz-schemas/docs/inference-degraded-contract.md`

- [x] **PRED-07** Implement Go-side sync inference client — new `kanz/internal/prediction/sync_client.go`; adds `google.golang.org/grpc v1.69.0` to go.mod. **Three resilience primitives in one file**: SyncClient (gRPC stub wrapper), CircuitBreaker (consecutive-failure breaker, half-open probe), PredictionCache (per-subject_id last-known NORMAL prediction — same shape as PRED-04's LastKnownCache, separate instance because Go orchestrator and Python worker are different processes). **Predict ALWAYS returns non-nil envelope** — transport failure / timeout / open circuit → DEGRADED envelope per PRED-02 §1 "never black-hole". Returns `(*PredictionEnvelope, error)` where envelope is always populated; error is non-nil for OBSERVABLE FAULTS (transport, validation) so callers can log/metric them. Application code branches on `env.Mode` regardless of err. **Four degraded-reason constants matching PRED-02 §2**: `ReasonInferenceUnavailable` / `ReasonInferenceTimeout` / `ReasonNoCachedPrediction` / `ReasonCircuitOpen`. Named bindings (not free-form strings at call sites) so renames surface as Go compile errors — same alert-stability discipline as PRED-04's Python constants. **Fallback logic mirrors PRED-04 verbatim**: cache hit → `proto.Clone(cached)` with `Mode=DEGRADED + DegradedReason=<reason>`; cache miss → zero-value DEGRADED with `no_cached_prediction`. **Cache rejects DEGRADED responses** — same trust-laundering protection from PRED-02 §1. **Circuit breaker is custom, ~50 lines**: consecutive-failure count + cooldown, half-open probe. Trade-off vs sony/gobreaker or hashicorp/go-resiliency: zero deps, no rolling windows or success-ratio thresholds yet — promote to a real library if those become needed. **State() method** returns "closed"/"open"/"half_open" for tests and future observability. **`NewSyncClientWithStub`** lets tests inject a fake `inferencepb.InferenceServiceClient` so the test suite runs without a gRPC server. **Defaults** (`DefaultSyncClientOptions`): timeout=200ms (more generous than BRAIN's <50ms server target — allows occasional spikes without firing breaker), breaker threshold=5, cooldown=10s. 11 tests cover normal-path, cache-only-NORMAL discipline, transport-error fallback, cache-hit-reuses-cached, timeout fallback, circuit opens after N failures, circuit closes after successful probe, empty-SubjectID rejection (stub not called), cache overwrite + nil-store + empty-id-noop, breaker state transitions · `kanz/internal/prediction/sync_client.go`, `kanz/go.mod`

- [x] **PRED-06** Define gRPC interface for interactive inference — new `kanz-schemas/proto/inference/v1/inference_service.proto`. Single RPC: `Predict(FeatureVector) returns (PredictionEnvelope)`. **No wrapper request/response messages** — uses PRED-01's payloads directly. This is the "same proto on both paths" property: a FeatureVector logged from the streaming path can be replayed into this RPC without field migration; a PredictionEnvelope captured from either path is consumable by the same downstream code. Replay-determinism (EVT-21d-style) extends across transports. **Deadline semantics**: callers MUST set a gRPC deadline. Per PRED-02 §2.2, exceeding it returns a DEGRADED PredictionEnvelope with `degraded_reason = "inference_timeout"` — NOT a gRPC DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. Returning an error would force callers to handle two failure paths (gRPC status + degraded envelope); unified-response keeps PRED-02 §1's "never black-hole" rule on the sync path. **OK/non-OK split is intentional, reserved for transport-vs-application failures**: `OK + DEGRADED envelope` = inference layer reached + decided it couldn't serve full-trust (caller branches on PredictionEnvelope.mode); `non-OK gRPC status` = transport failure (UNAVAILABLE/INTERNAL/RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED — inference layer unreachable, caller never saw the model's opinion). PRED-07's client library translates non-OK to a synthesized DEGRADED envelope with `degraded_reason = "inference_unavailable"` so application code sees one shape. Split lets observability distinguish transport-vs-application failures while keeping the application contract uniform. **Target latency p99 < 50ms** (KANZ_BRAIN); PRED-07's circuit breaker fails over to the streaming-path cache when the inference layer can't hold that budget. **Service location**: implementation lives in Python (PRED-08 worker pool); client lives in Go (PRED-07) · `kanz-schemas/proto/inference/v1/inference_service.proto`

- [x] **PRED-05** Implement prediction-event publishing from Python workers — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/publish.py`. **Class named `Publisher`, not `PredictionPublisher`** (which is the Protocol name in `streaming.worker`) — module-scoped name `kanz_inference.publish.Publisher` avoids the collision while structural typing makes the class duck-compatible with the protocol without an explicit `class Publisher(PredictionPublisher)` declaration. **`publish_prediction(source_envelope, prediction)` flow**: validate inputs → derive QualityFlag list from `prediction.mode` → enter `propagation_context` with lineage from source envelope → `producer.publish(Event(...))`. **Lineage propagation via contextvars** (EVT-18c): the Producer's `_stamp` reads correlation/causation/trace from contextvars; `propagation_context` sets them. Causation_id = source_envelope.event_id (NOT source_envelope.causation_id) — same chain-linking rule as EVT-17c, the next event's causation points at the CURRENT event. **PRED-02 §3 producer obligation honored**: `prediction.mode == DEGRADED` → envelope `quality_flags = [QUALITY_FLAG_DEGRADED]`. The flag exists at the envelope layer so payload-blind observability (data-integrity layer DATA-*) sees the signal without parsing the payload. **Subject + schema constants**: `inference.prediction.scored` + `inference.v1.PredictionEnvelope:1` (subject taxonomy §1 example, PRED-01 payload). **partition_key = subject_id** structural — per-subject ordering features→prediction must hold end-to-end. **Validation at publish boundary**: nil envelope/prediction + missing subject_id + missing as_of all rejected with ValueError. Other invariants (FACT idempotency_key == event_id, all envelope-policy §7 required fields) caught by `kanz_bus.validate` inside the Producer. **`@runtime_checkable` added to the protocol** in worker.py so the cross-module compliance test can `isinstance(Publisher(), PredictionPublisher)` to verify the duck-typing holds. 11 tests cover constructor + happy-path normal + DEGRADED-sets-flag + lineage propagation (correlation/causation/trace) + partition_key=subject_id + payload round-trip + nil-envelope/nil-prediction/missing-subject_id/missing-as_of rejection + FACT idempotency invariant + protocol compliance · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/publish.py`, `kanz-py/tests/test_prediction_publish.py`

- [x] **PRED-04** Implement Python streaming inference worker — new `kanz-py/kanz_inference/` package (Python counterpart to Go `kanz/internal/prediction/`), first subpackage `streaming/`. pyproject.toml updated to include both `kanz_bus*` + `kanz_inference*` under the existing distribution (future task pairs with PRED-09 model-registry packaging to split into separate distributions). **`StreamingWorker.handle(env, payload)`** is the canonical EventHandler shape — decodes FeatureVector payload, calls `Model.predict`, hands result to `PredictionPublisher`. Public method name (not underscore-prefixed) so tests drive it directly without spinning up a fake Subscriber loop; `run(group)` wraps subscription wiring for production. **Three boundary protocols** (lands-before-impl pattern from RISK-04): `Model` (async predict + model_id attr; implementations live in quant model artifacts), `PredictionPublisher` (PRED-05 will satisfy — boundary kept here in worker.py since the worker is the only caller), `Subscriber` (already exists in kanz_bus). **`LastKnownCache`** implements PRED-02 §2.1 fallback: per-subject_id dict of most-recent NORMAL prediction. **Two structural rules from PRED-02**: only NORMAL predictions seed the cache (caching a DEGRADED prediction would let it serve later requests as if fresh — trust laundering forbidden by §1); model exception → cache lookup → on hit re-emit cached value with `mode=DEGRADED + degraded_reason=inference_unavailable`; on miss synthesize `value=0/confidence=0/degraded_reason=no_cached_prediction`. **Degraded-reason identifiers** are constants from PRED-02 (`REASON_INFERENCE_UNAVAILABLE`, `REASON_NO_CACHED_PREDICTION`) — string-typed but with named bindings so a future rename surfaces as a Python import error rather than silent alert-rule breakage. **Lineage**: `feature_vector_event_id` on the prediction = envelope.event_id of the originating feature event — propagated through the fallback path explicitly (the normal path lets the Model set it). **No asyncio.Lock on cache**: single event loop ⇒ dict read/write is GIL-atomic; mirrors RISK-11 Cache's simplicity. 11 tests cover constructor nil-arg rejection, normal-path publish, cache-only-NORMAL discipline, cache-rejects-DEGRADED-storage, fallback-cache-hit + fallback-cache-miss + lineage-through-fallback paths, malformed-payload-raises (model + publisher not called), and cache overwrite + miss + empty-subject-noop · `kanz-py/kanz_inference/streaming/`, `kanz-py/tests/test_streaming_worker.py`

- [x] **PRED-03** Implement Go-side feature event publishing — new `kanz/internal/prediction/` package (first PRED-go file). **Go-native types mirror inference.v1 proto**: `SubjectID`, `FeatureSetRef`, `FeatureName` typed-string aliases; `FeatureVector` struct (SubjectID, FeatureSetRef, Values map, AsOf, SourceEventIDs); `FeatureValue` tagged-union (Kind enum + per-kind field). Same two-layer split RISK-03/RISK-10 chose — proto is wire shape, Go-native is in-memory engine shape; translation at publish boundary. Future Go consumers (PRED-11 feature store) see Go-native types only. **Convenience constructors `Scalar`/`Ordinal`/`Categorical`/`Vector`** — `prediction.Scalar(1.5)` reads better than struct-literal-with-Kind. `Vector` defensive-copies the input slice so caller mutations don't bleed into already-constructed FeatureValues. **`Publisher.PublishFeatures(ctx, fv)`** wraps a shared `bus.Producer` (same RISK-10 pattern — shared Producer keeps producer_sequence monotonic across event types from the same source). Validates Go-native input (SubjectID + FeatureSetRef + Values non-empty + AsOf non-zero + every FeatureValue.Kind != UNSPECIFIED), translates to proto, hands to Producer (which auto-stamps envelope identity + lineage from ctx). **partition_key = SubjectID** so per-subject ordering holds end-to-end: features for one subject arrive at the inference worker in order, predictions back to Go preserve the same ordering — same per-aggregate ordering discipline RISK-04→RISK-10 establishes for portfolios. **Event-type + schema-ref**: `inference.feature.computed` + `inference.v1.FeatureVector:1` (subject taxonomy §1 example, PRED-01 payload). **Module-private** under `kanz/internal/`; lifts to `kanz/internal/prediction/api/v1/` if a cross-module consumer (replay tooling, scenario harness) appears, matching the RISK-01 promotion pattern. 9 tests cover NilProducer rejection, happy-path (envelope validate + payload round-trip + all 4 FeatureValue kinds), each missing-required-field rejection (SubjectID + FeatureSetRef + Values + AsOf + UNSPECIFIED-kind), defensive-copy of Vector input, and partition_key = SubjectID contract · `kanz/internal/prediction/feature_publish.go`

- [x] **PRED-02** Document degraded-mode contract — new `kanz-schemas/docs/inference-degraded-contract.md`, 7 numbered sections matching the existing kanz-schemas/docs style (envelope-policy, event-class-rules). **Core rule (§1)**: the prediction layer NEVER returns "no prediction" — always a PredictionEnvelope with honest `mode`. Black-holing is a correctness regression because the caller can't distinguish "model is silent" from "model said no" and has no signal to fall back on its own logic. **Three triggers (§2)** mapped to stable `degraded_reason` identifiers: 2.1 inference unavailable (`inference_unavailable`/`bus_lag_exceeded`/`circuit_open` — falls back to PRED-04 cache; no cache → `no_cached_prediction` with value=0 confidence=0); 2.2 inference slow (`inference_timeout` — sync gRPC budget exceeded falls back to cache); 2.3 low confidence (`low_confidence` — per-model threshold from artifact metadata, NOT global constant). Models without calibrated confidence are ALWAYS DEGRADED (the "unknown confidence as no confidence" rule from PRED-01's envelope semantics, repeated as a contract obligation here). **Producer obligations (§3)**: always return envelope, set mode honestly, machine-readable degraded_reason (free-form context goes in explanation sidecar), emit one DecisionLog per degraded response (audit trail independent of consumer logging), set envelope `QUALITY_FLAG_DEGRADED` on the bus envelope so payload-blind observability sees it. **Consumer obligations (§4)**: branch on mode BEFORE reading value (ignoring mode = correctness bug); document per-use-case fallback; treat UNSPECIFIED as DEGRADED (defensive — broken producer must not silently get full-trust); never elevate DEGRADED to NORMAL based on value alone. **§5 — mode is per-response, not per-subject**: rules out the "this subject is degraded" caching anti-pattern. **§6 — cross-path uniformity**: streaming and sync paths share one decision function (PRED-04 + PRED-07), so a consumer can't route around degraded mode by switching transport. **§7 explicitly scopes out** what the doc does NOT govern: timeout values (per-deployment tuning), feature-staleness signalling (DATA-02/03's territory), model-version pinning + shadow policy (PRED-09/10), and the risk engine's RISK-11 degraded mode (independent — risk can be NORMAL with DEGRADED prediction inputs and vice versa) · `kanz-schemas/docs/inference-degraded-contract.md`

- [x] **PRED-01** Define feature-vector + prediction-envelope proto schemas — new `kanz-schemas/proto/inference/v1/` package, two files. CODEOWNERS already maps `/proto/inference/` to `@kanz-eng/quant-ml`; buf.yaml auto-discovers anything under `proto/` — no infra changes needed. **`FeatureVector`**: subject_id (instrument/portfolio/counterparty — same identifier used as envelope partition_key when published as `inference.feature.computed`), feature_set_ref (`{name}:{version}` for routing to matching model version per PRED-09), values (name → FeatureValue map), as_of (effective domain time of the INPUTS, not computation time), source_event_ids (lineage to upstream events — same role as `RiskMeasure.source_event_ids`). **`FeatureValue` tagged union** (oneof): scalar (double — model inputs, BRAIN's banned-double rule doesn't apply since these aren't money), ordinal (sint64 for integer-encoded categoricals), categorical (string label), vector (DoubleArray wrapper — proto3 doesn't allow repeated fields directly in oneof). Categorical distinct from scalar so a model can't accidentally treat a class id as a magnitude. **`PredictionEnvelope`** (name has "Envelope" because it bundles prediction + model identity + confidence + mode + explanation + lineage): subject_id (mirrors FeatureVector), model (`{name}@{version}` — same shape as `DecisionLog.decider`), value (double prediction output), confidence (0..1 calibrated; set to 0 + DEGRADED mode when uncalibrated, forces consumer to handle degraded path explicitly rather than blindly trust), class_label (set for classification, empty for regression), explanation (per-feature SHAP-like contributions), mode (NORMAL/DEGRADED enum — consumers MUST branch before acting), degraded_reason (short operator-readable trigger), feature_vector_event_id (back-pointer for join-on-replay), as_of (effective domain time). **`PredictionMode` enum** with explicit UNSPECIFIED zero value (never set, per kanz-schemas enum convention). Both payloads transport on both the async streaming path (bus message body) and the sync gRPC path (PRED-06). Subject taxonomy: `inference.feature.computed` and `inference.prediction.scored` (already documented in subject-taxonomy.md §1 examples) · `kanz-schemas/proto/inference/v1/feature_vector.proto`, `kanz-schemas/proto/inference/v1/prediction.proto`

### Risk Engine

- [x] **RISK-13** Tests for degraded-mode behavior + uncertainty propagation — new `kanz/internal/risk/integration_test.go` (`package risk` internal-test so it can drive `Detector.now` for time mocking without expanding the public surface). **Cross-package integration scope distinct from individual-component tests**: compute/, state/, degraded_test.go each exercise one package; this file exercises the *composition* (state.Store → compute → Cache → Detector → response flags) that the future orchestrator will perform. **`computeExposureWithFlags` helper** inlines the canonical orchestrator composition pattern — try live, on success cache+tag, on failure fall back to cache; on no-cache return degraded "not found" signal.
