Doubles as documentation of the pattern; when the orchestrator EngineImpl lands, these tests get rewritten against it directly and the composition shifts from test code to production code. **9 integration scenarios**: fresh state (no flags), over-budget (STALE only), over-degraded (DEGRADED+STALE), cache-fallback-after-time-advance (uses two Detectors at different wall-clock times to demonstrate cache+Detector composing under failure), unknown-portfolio-returns-degraded-not-found, Engine.Health aggregating latest-AsOf across portfolios (single healthy portfolio carries the engine — intentional, ingestion-alive-somewhere semantic), uncertainty propagation through compute (positions post-applied with MarketValueUncertainty → measures carry √(σ_1²+σ_2²)), no-uncertainty-inputs leaves all UncertaintyAbs nil (not zero — the "no signal" vs "calibrated zero" distinction documented at api/v1 boundary), mixed uncertainty inputs skip nil (defends PropagateSumIndependent's skip-nil rule from a regression that would falsely tighten the band). **`applyState` test helper** wraps the state.Store apply path with realistic envelope construction so each test reads as "apply this state, do this query" without spelling out envelope plumbing. **Uncertainty post-apply pattern** (`p.SetPosition(withUnc)` after `store.Lookup`): the wire proto doesn't yet carry MarketValueUncertainty, so tests simulate the future RISK-11-staleness-synthesis path by populating the field directly on the live Portfolio — proves the algorithm is wired even though the production pipeline currently always leaves it nil. **Risk Engine epic complete (RISK-01..13)**: interface + boundary + domain + ingest + state + exposure + measures + uncertainty + scenario + publish + degraded + property tests + integration tests · `kanz/internal/risk/integration_test.go`

- [x] **RISK-12** Unit + property tests for measure correctness — new `kanz/internal/risk/compute/correctness_test.go` adds 11 property tests on top of RISK-06/07/08's unit tests. **Property iteration**: 50 seeded random portfolios per test (deterministic seeds 1..50 so failures are reproducible by re-running with the same seed). **Why not `testing/quick`**: stdlib quickcheck generates via reflection — works for plain types but cannot synthesise `*commonpb.Decimal` proto pointers without a custom Generator on a type we don't own. Seeded-loop approach gives the same property-test ergonomics without fighting the proto-type constraint. **Mathematical invariants** asserted: triangle inequality `|Net| ≤ Gross` (sum-of-abs vs signed-sum); `Gross ≥ 0` (always non-negative); `VaR99 / Gross = 0.01` exactly (RISK-07 placeholder formula — when a real VaR model replaces the placeholder, this test INTENTIONALLY breaks as failure-as-documentation that the placeholder is gone); `Delta == NetExposure` (same placeholder-identity contract); permutation invariance (positions order doesn't change measures — pins against any future change that conflates "Position exists" with "Position contributes"); zero-value-position no-op; empty portfolio yields zero for every measure; currency-bucket Nets sum to total Net (RISK-06 aggregation reconciliation). **Uncertainty invariants**: Cauchy-Schwarz bound `√(Σσ²) ≤ Σ|σ|` (independent never exceeds perfectly-correlated — defends the conservative-vs-textbook semantics from accidental reversal); scalar propagation exact `|c|×|σ|`; propagation sum is order-invariant. **Determinism**: same portfolio computed twice yields numerically identical measures (catches accidental map-iteration nondeterminism). **`dValue` helper** converts Decimal → float64 for property comparisons — uncertainty bands and order assertions don't need money-grade precision, sidesteps writing a Decimal comparator · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/correctness_test.go`

- [x] **RISK-11** Implement degraded mode — new `kanz/internal/risk/degraded.go` (first file at the top-level `kanz/internal/risk/` directory; all prior risk work lived in sub-packages — this establishes `package risk` as the home for cross-cutting concerns). **Two pieces**: `Detector` (staleness assessment, pure function) and `Cache` (last-known ExposureSet/MeasureSet per portfolio). **Detector.Assess(asOf) → (Mode, []QualityFlag)** uses two thresholds: `DefaultFreshnessBudget = 30s` (above → +QualityFlagStale, mode stays Normal) and `DefaultDegradedThreshold = 5m` (above → ModeDegraded + QualityFlagDegraded + QualityFlagStale). **Zero-AsOf is a special case**: a portfolio with no state ever applied returns ModeDegraded + QualityFlagDegraded regardless of the wall clock — caller must not read "no data" as "zero risk". **Why staleness drives mode, not health-checks**: the engine has no exotic dependencies to poll (no external risk service, no model server). The single operationally-meaningful observable is `now - portfolio.AsOf` — a healthy ingest keeps it small; any stall (broker outage, applier crash, network partition) shows up as growing staleness. Cheap signal, locally diagnosable cause. **Boundary semantics**: staleness EQUAL to threshold is FRESH (the boundary is "above the budget" not "at the budget") — off-by-one regression caught by test. **`Detector.Health(latestAsOf) → v1.Health`** composes Mode + AsOf + Staleness for the api/v1 Engine.Health implementation; zero-AsOf yields zero-staleness (meaningless to report wall-clock gap when no state ever existed). **Cache** is a per-portfolio map of last-known ExposureSet and MeasureSet, `sync.RWMutex` for cheap concurrent reads. No TTL: the cached value carries its own AsOf, Detector handles staleness — chronological cache is a different feature. Eviction = overwrite-on-next-store. Nil stores are no-ops (won't crash, won't write phantom entries). **Integration pattern for orchestrator (later task)**: on successful compute, `Cache.StoreX(id, result)`; on compute failure, fall back to `Cache.LookupX` + tag via `Detector.Assess(cached.AsOf())`. 14 tests cover Detector (fresh, over-budget, over-degraded, zero-AsOf, boundary-at-budget, custom thresholds, fallback-on-zero-thresholds, Health composition, zero-AsOf health) and Cache (store+lookup × 2 types, overwrite, miss, nil-store, AsOf round-trip through Detector) · `kanz/internal/risk/degraded.go`

- [x] **RISK-10** Emit risk-output events — new `kanz/internal/risk/publish/` package + two kanz-schemas additions: new `proto/domain/v1/measure.proto` with `RiskMeasureSet` + `RiskMeasure` (companion to ExposureSet, FACT-class, exact Decimal not double — `observation.v1.MetricObservation` uses double which violates BRAIN's banned-double rule for money/risk numbers), and additive enum extension `EXPOSURE_DIMENSION_INSTRUMENT = 6` (schema-evolution §4 "add enum value" non-breaking change). **Publisher.EmitExposure + EmitMeasures** wrap a shared `bus.Producer` and translate domain → proto at the boundary (symmetric to RISK-04's Ingestor handling proto → domain). Shared Producer is load-bearing: producer_sequence stays monotonic across event types from the same source. **Per-aggregate ordering preserved end-to-end**: `partition_key = portfolio_id` on both event types — same value RISK-05 keys on for state ordering, RISK-04 keys on for ingest — so the per-portfolio ordering guarantee holds ingest → state → compute → publish → consumer. **`RiskMeasure.source_event_ids[]` carries the lineage** to the inputs that produced each measure: envelope causation_id is single-valued and only meaningful when there's one triggering event; aggregated measures derived from many position-state events use the list. Caller (orchestrator) decides which inputs to attribute — Publisher just propagates. **`UncertaintyAbs` propagates as nil-or-decimal**: a measure with no propagated uncertainty (RISK-08 returned nil) marshals as absent, preserving the api/v1 "nil ⇒ no uncertainty propagated" signal across the wire; a measure with a propagated value marshals as the Decimal. Spurious-zero round-trip avoided. **Two event-type names**: `risk.portfolio.exposure_recomputed` (payload domain.v1.ExposureSet:1), `risk.portfolio.measures_computed` (payload domain.v1.RiskMeasureSet:1). Both follow taxonomy §1 ({domain}.{entity}.{event_type}, past-tense FACTs). **Dimension mapping**: domain.ExposureByInstrument → wire EXPOSURE_DIMENSION_INSTRUMENT (the new enum value), ExposureByCurrency → CURRENCY, ExposureBySector → SECTOR. Domain values without a wire counterpart map to UNSPECIFIED so a future domain-side addition that hasn't yet propagated to the schema still publishes (consumers see UNSPECIFIED per enum-unknown semantics, event-class-rules §3). **6 tests** cover NilProducer rejection, EmitExposure happy-path (envelope validate + payload round-trip + partition_key + FACT idempotency invariant), dimension mapping (3 dimensions), Nil rejection on both methods, EmitMeasures happy-path (source_event_ids propagation + per-measure UncertaintyAbs nil-vs-set), partition_key = portfolio_id contract · `kanz/internal/risk/publish/`, `kanz-schemas/proto/domain/v1/measure.proto`, `kanz-schemas/proto/domain/v1/exposure.proto`

- [x] **RISK-09** Implement scenario / what-if engine — new `kanz/internal/risk/scenario/` package + extension of api/v1 with concrete shock types. **Architectural tension resolved**: RISK-02's arch test forbids outsiders from importing `kanz/internal/risk/scenario/`, but external callers need to CONSTRUCT shocks to drive `Engine.EvaluateScenario`. Resolution — shock value types (`PriceShock`, `ParallelShift`) live in `api/v1/scenario.go` where outsiders can reach them; the scenario package owns the DISPATCH + APPLY (type-asserting on the api/v1 types it knows). Adding a shock = struct in api/v1 + case in scenario dispatch. **`Evaluate(p, shocks, registry) → *MeasureSet`** is a pure function: deep-clones the portfolio, applies shocks in order, returns measures computed against the shocked state. Original portfolio is never mutated — critical because the orchestrator holds RISK-05's per-portfolio lock during Evaluate, and mutation under-lock would falsify subsequent queries in the same lock window. **Two baseline shock types**: `PriceShock` (one instrument, percentage change) and `ParallelShift` (all positions, same percentage). PriceShock against an unheld instrument is a silent no-op so watchlist-wide batches against multiple portfolios succeed regardless of which instruments any given portfolio holds. **Unknown shock types are silent no-ops**: a custom shock the engine doesn't dispatch on must not abort the run — large batches of mixed shock types are best-effort. **`compute.ShockMoney(m, pct) = m × (1 + pct)`** exported as the boundary helper (vs package-private addDecimal/mulDecimal) so scenario reaches the high-level operation without depending on internal arithmetic primitives. **`InstrumentID` lifted to api/v1**: necessary for external scenario-shock construction; `domain.InstrumentID` becomes a type alias (`= v1.InstrumentID`) so the two packages share one canonical identifier with zero conversion. **Decimal representation note in tests**: shocks change exponents through multiplication (1000×10^0 × 90×10^-2 = 90000×10^-2 — same value as 900×10^0, different fields). Tests assert the actual representation, with comments referencing decimal.proto's "1.0 and 1.00 are equal in value but differ in fields" contract. 9 tests cover single-shock + immutability of original + no-op-on-unheld + parallel shift + shock composition (10% then -10% ≠ 0%) + unknown-shock skip + empty list + AsOf propagation + custom registry · `kanz/internal/risk/scenario/`, `kanz/internal/risk/api/v1/scenario.go`

- [x] **RISK-08** Implement uncertainty propagation through measure computations — new `kanz/internal/risk/compute/uncertainty.go`. **Three propagation primitives** keyed off correlation assumption: `PropagateSumIndependent(σ_i...) = √(Σσ_i²)` (uncorrelated inputs, standard textbook), `PropagateSumPerfectlyCorrelated(σ_i...) = Σ|σ_i|` (conservative when correlations unknown), `PropagateScalar(c, σ) = |c|×|σ|` (exact, no assumption — for VaR=0.01×Gross-style measures). **nil-semantics is load-bearing**: per-input nil ⇒ skip; all-nil ⇒ return nil (not zero) so the api/v1 `Measure.UncertaintyAbs` "nil ⇒ no uncertainty propagated" signal stays meaningful — a spurious zero would falsely claim a calibrated zero confidence interval. **Wired into the four baseline measures** (RISK-07): GrossExposure + NetExposure + Delta all propagate via `sumUncertaintyInBaseCurrency` (independent sum of per-position MarketValueUncertainty in BaseCurrency); VaR99 propagates via `PropagateScalar(0.01, σ_gross)`. The independence assumption is the default for cross-position aggregates; a future iteration can offer per-measure mode or a correlation matrix. **Domain extension**: added `MarketValueUncertainty *commonpb.Money` to `domain.Position` — populated from staleness signals (RISK-11) or a future per-instrument uncertainty source (the wire proto `domain.v1.PositionState` does not yet carry a matching field, so engine output has UncertaintyAbs nil today; RISK-08's propagation paths are exercised by tests until a producer ships uncertainty values). **`decimalSqrt` via float64 round-trip**: confidence intervals don't need money-grade precision; fixed output exponent (`uncertaintyExp = -6`) prevents propagation chains from drifting toward extreme exponents; negative input clamps to zero (sqrt of underflow recovers to "no uncertainty" rather than NaN). New helpers `decimalToFloat`/`floatToDecimal` use `math.Round` (not truncation) to minimize round-trip bias. **Currency rule preserved**: uncertainty inputs must be in BaseCurrency to count — same no-FX-in-compute discipline as RISK-06/07. 13 tests cover all three propagation primitives + nil semantics + classic Pythagorean triple (3-4-5) + integration with GrossExposure / VaR99 / Delta / NetExposure + the foreign-currency-skip rule · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/`

- [x] **RISK-07** Implement first concrete risk measure set — new `kanz/internal/risk/compute/measures.go` ships the **registry framework + 4 illustrative measures**. Honest scope: the engine has no historical return time series, no volatility model, no factor model, no per-instrument Greek data — real VaR / sensitivities need all of those. The four measures here (`GrossExposure`, `NetExposure`, `VaR99`, `Delta`) are deliberate placeholders; the architectural deliverable is the **Registry pattern** that lets a quant team plug in real measure implementations later WITHOUT changing the api/v1 contract, the MeasureSet shape, or any caller code. **`MeasureFunc = func(*Portfolio) v1.Measure`** — pure function, no side effects/I/O/errors; missing data signalled by zero-value Measure plus quality flag at response level (not per-measure error branches that complicate the api/v1 surface). **`Registry`** with Register/Names; **`DefaultRegistry()`** seeds the four baseline measures; **`ComputeMeasures(p, registry, filter) → *MeasureSet`** runs registered measures, narrowed by optional filter (unknown filter names silently dropped, matching `MeasureSet.Lookup` miss semantics). **Currency convention**: Measure values are dimensionless `*common.v1.Decimal` (api/v1 carries no currency field); money-valued measures are documented to be in BaseCurrency and only sum positions whose MarketValue currency matches — other-currency positions skipped (degraded-mode signalling is RISK-11's). **VaR99** = 1% × GrossExposure (clearly labelled placeholder); **Delta** = NetExposure (trivial "$1 position = $1 spot delta" placeholder, correct for cash equities, wrong for derivatives). **Quants replace via `r.Register(MeasureVaR99, realImpl)`** — one-line override; no other code changes. **Measure names follow api/v1 doc convention** (`VaR99`, `Delta`, `GrossExposure`, `NetExposure` — short, CamelCase, dimensional suffix); same one-way-door discipline as event_type names since rename breaks every dashboard/alert. **`sumInBaseCurrency(p, abs bool)`** is the single helper backing all four baseline measures — future "what counts as same-currency" change edits one function, not four. **`mulDecimal` added to compute/decimal.go** (coefficient product + exponent sum) — overflow flagged for math/big upgrade at extreme scales, fine for placeholder formulas. 10 tests cover each measure, registry registration + replacement, filter narrowing, unknown filter dropping, name ordering, AsOf propagation · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/`

- [x] **RISK-06** Implement exposure computation — new `kanz/internal/risk/compute/` package; `exposure.go` + `decimal.go` (first compute helpers — every risk computation needs `common.v1.Decimal` arithmetic and proto's representation-only type has no methods). **`ComputeExposure(Portfolio) → *ExposureSet`** emits two dimensions today: `ExposureByInstrument` (one Exposure per position with a MarketValue; Gross = `absMoney(MarketValue)`, Net = MarketValue signed) and `ExposureByCurrency` (positions bucketed by MarketValue.CurrencyCode, Gross/Net summed per bucket). **`ExposureBySector` deliberately deferred**: in the closed `domain.ExposureDimension` enum but needs an instrument→sector reference table the engine does not yet own; a RISK-06b can inject a sector-lookup function later without changing the existing signature. **No cross-currency conversion**: bucketing on currency first makes per-bucket sums well-defined without an FX layer; cross-currency aggregation needs an FX-conversion layer outside compute. **Skip semantics**: positions with nil MarketValue are skipped (no mark yet means no expressible exposure); zero-quantity positions retained for audit lineage flow through with zero MarketValue producing zero-row exposures (informative — position still exists — without distorting aggregates). **AsOf propagated** from Portfolio so api/v1 callers see the freshness of underlying state. **Decimal helpers** (`addDecimal`/`negateDecimal`/`absDecimal`/`zeroDecimal` + Money wrappers): exponent alignment to the more precise operand (1.50 + 0.025 = 1.525 = 1525×10^-3); nil operands treated as zero for accumulator initialization; `addMoney` requires matching currency codes (caller's responsibility); `pow10` is unguarded — bounded by typical exponent differences in financial data, switch to math/big if RISK-07/08 ever hit extreme scales. 8 exposure tests cover empty, single position, short (signed Net + absolute Gross), same-currency aggregation, mixed currencies, nil-MarketValue skip, exponent alignment, AsOf propagation; 7 decimal tests cover sum + align + nil + negate + abs + money-nil-handling + pow10 · `kanz/internal/risk/compute/`

- [x] **RISK-05** Implement per-aggregate ordered, idempotent state application — new `kanz/internal/risk/state/` package, two files (`store.go`, `dedup.go`) + tests. **Store** holds three lazy-created maps keyed by PortfolioID: `portfolios` (`*domain.Portfolio`), `locks` (per-portfolio `*sync.Mutex`), `dedup` (per-portfolio `dedupWindow`). The map-wrapping mutex (`Store.mu`) is held briefly only for lookup-or-create; per-portfolio locks then serialize the actual apply. **Per-aggregate ordering primitive**: each portfolio's mutex is the cheapest primitive that gives cross-event-type serialization. The bus only guarantees per-partition ordering (event-class-rules §1), but a portfolio receives PortfolioState on one partition and PositionState on others — per-aggregate ordering across all of them is the engine's correctness invariant; alternatives (per-aggregate goroutine + channel) add complexity without changing the contract. **Per-aggregate dedup window** (defense-in-depth alongside the bus.Consumer's global window EVT-17d): 2m TTL / 10k entries (matching the bus defaults so layers reinforce). Catches duplicates that arrive via different Consumers feeding the same Applier, and survives bus dedup-window loss on restart. Per-aggregate scoping (not global) matches event-class-rules §1 and avoids global-map contention. **Snapshot semantics** (event-class-rules §3, latest-wins-by-event-time): hard reset — clear positions, apply snapshot's positions, set aggregate fields, record `LogPosition` for RISK-04 bootstrap. Stale snapshot (older than current portfolio.AsOf) is silently skipped + dedup-recorded (it's a real envelope we've decided not to apply). **Lazy portfolio creation** with empty BaseCurrency: a PositionState arriving before its PortfolioState lazy-creates the portfolio; the first PortfolioState or PortfolioSnapshot fills BaseCurrency. The alternative ("reject unknown portfolios") would reject many valid sequences during rolling deployments. **`Lookup` returns the live pointer** (not a clone) — internal contract for compute layers (RISK-06/07) which read under appropriate locks; the api/v1 surface will clone before returning to external callers. **Domain extension**: extended `domain.Portfolio` with `displayName`/`cashBalance`/`totalMarketValue`/`positionCount` (mirroring PortfolioState aggregate fields) + `SetAggregate(AggregateUpdate)` bulk setter + `ClearPositions()` snapshot-reset hook; extended `domain.Position` with `MarketValue`/`RealizedPnL`/`UnrealizedPnL` and fixed `AveragePrice` type from `Money` → `Decimal` to match the proto. **`tsTime` nil-safety**: uses concrete `*timestamppb.Timestamp` (not an `AsTime() time.Time` interface) — calling AsTime on a typed-nil pointer returns Unix epoch, not Go zero time, which would falsely look like "state applied" to AsOf checks. **Compile-time `var _ ingest.Applier = (*Store)(nil)`** catches contract drift at build time. 8 tests cover happy-path (3 event types), missing aggregate id, idempotency (same key blocked / distinct keys both applied), snapshot hard reset, stale snapshot rejection, concurrent applies to different portfolios (parallel), concurrent applies to same portfolio (serialized, no lost updates) · `kanz/internal/risk/state/`, `kanz/internal/risk/domain/portfolio.go`
- [x] **RISK-04** Implement state ingestion consumer — new `kanz/internal/risk/ingest/` package. **Single-responsibility design**: Ingestor is the bus→engine bridge that takes `bus.EventHandler`-shaped deliveries (envelope + payload bytes — already validated by `bus.Consumer`), discriminates by `env.EventType`, unmarshals into the right `domain.v1` proto, and dispatches to an `Applier` interface (RISK-05 will implement). Three event types routed: `risk.portfolio.revalued` (→ `domain.v1.PortfolioState`), `risk.position.changed` (→ `PositionState`), `risk.portfolio.snapshot` (→ `PortfolioSnapshot` STATE_SNAPSHOT). **Scope-split with RISK-05 is load-bearing**: Ingestor does pure routing — no translation, idempotency, or ordering, because those need per-aggregate state RISK-05 owns; doing them here would split state across two packages and make the per-aggregate guarantee impossible. **Applier takes proto directly** (not pre-translated domain types): RISK-05 needs envelope metadata for dedup/ordering plus the typed payload, and translating to domain here would force Applier to receive both — simpler to let RISK-05 own the proto→domain mapping. **Applier interface lives in ingest, not state**: RISK-04 lands before RISK-05 (interface declared here, satisfied there) so tests + the orchestrator can wire a fake. **Live-only consumer**: REPLAYED-flagged events are already rejected by `bus.Validate` before reaching Handler (EVT-20c); any future replay-scoped state consumer constructs a separate Ingestor wired to `bus.WithValidator(bus.ValidateReplay)`. **Unknown event_type surfaces as `ErrUnknownEventType`** (errors.Is-able) → bus retry+DLQ picks it up; misconfiguration must be loud, not silently dropped. Six unit tests cover happy-path dispatch (3 event types), unknown event_type rejection, unmarshal error surfacing, and applier error surfacing — all via a 4-line fakeApplier · `kanz/internal/risk/ingest/`
- [x] **RISK-03** Define core domain model types — new `kanz/internal/risk/domain/` package, split across `portfolio.go` (Portfolio + Position + InstrumentID + CurrencyCode), `exposure.go` (Exposure + ExposureDimension + ExposureSet), `measure.go` (MeasureSet wrapping api/v1's Measure value type). **Two-layer separation from kanz-schemas proto types** (domain.v1.PortfolioState etc.): proto is the WIRE shape (serialization, evolution, cross-language); domain is the engine's WORKING in-memory shape (fast access, immutability of snapshots, natural Go ergonomics). RISK-04 will ingest proto → construct domain; RISK-10 will serialize domain → proto for publishing. Cost of duplication = ability to evolve either side without churning the other. **Implements the api/v1 opaque interfaces**: `*ExposureSet` satisfies `v1.ExposureSet` (AsOf), `*MeasureSet` satisfies `v1.MeasureSet` (AsOf + Lookup). Compile-time interface assertions (`var _ v1.ExposureSet = (*ExposureSet)(nil)`) catch any contract drift at build time. **Immutability discipline**: every accessor returns a fresh slice/map copy (Positions(), Items(), Names()) so the api/v1 caller cannot mutate the engine's internal storage; RISK-05's state-apply layer is the single writer. **Money/sizes/prices are common.v1 types** (no `float64`/`double` per BRAIN's banned-double rule): Quantity is `*common.v1.Decimal`, AveragePrice + Exposure.Gross/Net are `*common.v1.Money`. **Closed ExposureDimension enum for v1**: ExposureByInstrument (canonical), ExposureByCurrency, ExposureBySector — new dimensions take next constant, existing values never repurposed (mirrors envelope-policy §4 field-number discipline). **Position retention**: zero-quantity positions are kept for audit lineage; explicit removal only via Portfolio.Forget when a state event retires the instrument. **Snapshot bootstrap hook**: Portfolio.SetSnapshot records the `*common.v1.LogPosition` from the most recent snapshot so RISK-04's bootstrap path knows where to resume the log (event-class-rules §3) · `kanz/internal/risk/domain/`

- [x] **RISK-02** Add import-boundary architecture test — new `kanz/test/arch/risk_boundary_test.go` (first tenant of `kanz/test/arch/`, the convention future modules' arch tests will follow). Build-time enforcement of the RISK-01 contract: shells out to `go list -json ./...` from the module root (resolved via `go env GOMOD` → `filepath.Dir`), streams the per-package JSON via `json.Decoder`, walks `Imports + TestImports + XTestImports` (test code is held to the same rule — a test-only consumer of an impl package is still a hidden coupling). Two rules: `TestRiskBoundary_OutsidersUseAPIOnly` (any package outside `kanz/internal/risk/` may import only `kanz/internal/risk/api/v*`; impl sub-packages — compute, state, ingest, scenario, publish, degraded, domain — are private), `TestRiskBoundary_APIDoesNotImportImpl` (api/v* must not depend on impl sub-packages — a contract leak that would defeat the api-as-narrow-surface design). **Why `go list` over a third-party arch-lint library**: zero new deps, runs as a regular `go test`, output is structured JSON suitable for streaming. **Why direct + test imports both**: a hidden test-only import past the boundary creates a phantom consumer that breaks the moment the impl symbol is renamed — exactly the kind of slow rot the arch test exists to prevent. **Land-before-impl is OK**: with only `api/v1` shipped today (RISK-01) and no impl packages yet, both tests pass trivially — they become enforcing as RISK-04..11 land. The architecture-test pattern (`pkgInfo` struct, `loadPackages` helper, `allImports` helper) is reusable for future modules (PRED, DATA) which will add sibling `*_boundary_test.go` files under the same package · `kanz/test/arch/`
- [x] **RISK-01** Define risk-engine module interface — first tenant of `kanz/internal/` (greenfield; layout convention follows BRAIN's "promote to top-level when 2nd consumer appears" rule). New `kanz/internal/risk/api/v1/engine.go` is the **narrow external surface** module siblings (orchestrator, HTTP API, scenario tooling, degraded-mode probe) call through — never through `compute/`, `state/`, `ingest/` directly (RISK-02 wires the architecture test that enforces this). **Query + scenario + health only**, no Apply/Mutate/Command — per the anti-decision "all state changes must be event-driven", state arrives via the bus (RISK-04) and risk outputs leave via the bus (RISK-10); the api/v1 surface is read-side + scenario only. **Engine interface, 4 methods**: `Exposure(ExposureRequest) → ExposureResponse`, `Measures(MeasuresRequest) → MeasuresResponse`, `EvaluateScenario(ScenarioRequest) → ScenarioResponse`, `Health() → Health`. **Versioning under envelope-policy discipline**: additive-only within v1, breaking changes cut a sibling `v2/`; new method on Engine only when every plausible implementation can supply a non-degraded answer (else it goes on a separate interface). **Type-surface trade-offs**: rich domain types (`ExposureSet`, `MeasureSet`, `ScenarioShock`) declared as minimal-method interfaces (e.g. `ExposureSet.AsOf()`, `MeasureSet.Lookup(name)`) — RISK-03 provides concrete impls, api/v1 keeps surface narrow so domain types can evolve without churning callers; `Measure.Value` uses `*common.v1.Decimal` directly (already a transitive dep via event ingestion, banned-double rule from BRAIN, avoids string→decimal parsing at every read site); QualityFlag is api-local string enum (DEGRADED, STALE) rather than the envelope QualityFlag proto so api/v1 doesn't tangle with envelope-versioning churn for purely Go-API concerns. **Degraded mode surfaced two ways**: explicit Mode on `Health()` + per-response QualityFlags — callers cannot accidentally consume degraded data without seeing the signal. **Sentinel errors**: `ErrPortfolioNotFound`, `ErrInvalidRequest` (transport/runtime errors are distinct so callers branch correctly) · `kanz/internal/risk/api/v1/engine.go`

### Event System

- [x] **EVT-21d** Contract tests: replay determinism (same log → identical output) — new `kanz/test/contract/replay/determinism_test.go` proves the runtime claim from schema-evolution.md §8 that ties the EVT-20 replay pipeline to the EVT-21c compat contract. **Wire-determinism**: two Pipeline runs over the same fresh source-log slice (canonicalLog rebuilds per call so each run gets independent envelope pointers — Pipeline mutates `QualityFlags` in place via `StampReplayed`) with the same RunID produce byte-identical `bus.Message` captures (Subject, Key, Body, Headers); a regression introducing nondeterminism (per-run wall-clock metadata, map-field reordering, etc.) fails loudly. **RunID-orthogonality**: same log with different RunIDs produces identical Body + Key + Headers, differing only in Subject — proves the RunID is the only run-scoped wire input and isolates the determinism property from accidental coupling. **Replay-of-replay convergence**: feed Run1's captured output back as Run2's source (via Unframe → Event), with the same RunID — outputs converge byte-identically because `StampReplayed`'s idempotency (`IsReplayed` early return) means the chained run does not re-stamp. **Envelope identity preservation**: clone every source envelope pre-run via `proto.Clone`, run Pipeline, Unframe each output, assert every identity field (`EventId`, `IdempotencyKey`, `ProducerSequence`, `CorrelationId`, `CausationId`, `Source`, `ProducerVersion`) is byte-equal to the cloned original — only `QualityFlags` is permitted to differ, and only by adding exactly one `QUALITY_FLAG_REPLAYED`. **Compat-during-replay link**: simulate a future publisher's event landing in an old Kafka log by appending an unknown varint field at tag 1000 to envelope wire bytes, unmarshal so the unknown field lands in `ProtoReflect().GetUnknown()`, replay the event, Unframe the output, assert the unknown field is preserved — proves the §8 link that replay determinism depends on proto3 unknown-field preservation, the exact property EVT-21c tested in isolation. Tests sit in `package replay_test` (external) so they consume only the public `tools/replay` + `pkg/bus` surface, matching the other EVT-21 contract trees · `kanz/test/contract/replay/`
- [x] **EVT-21c** Contract tests: schema compatibility both directions — new `kanz/test/contract/compatibility/envelope_compat_test.go` proves the proto3 wire-runtime invariants the envelope-policy + schema-evolution docs depend on, complementing two static gates that cannot prove live decoder behavior: `buf breaking` (EVT-07, schema-text level) and CODEOWNERS review (semantic). **Forward compatibility** (older code reads newer bytes): an unknown field appended via `protowire.AppendTag/AppendVarint` at field number 1000 (well above the 1–19 currently allocated; outside the 19000–19999 reserved range) survives unmarshal — verified via `m.ProtoReflect().GetUnknown()` containing the tag — and re-marshal preserves it (scanned via `protowire.ConsumeField`); the same property is asserted at the EventFrame layer for `bus.Unframe` (with the deliberate scope note that replay re-marshals from `Envelope + payload` and so drops EventFrame-level unknowns on egress — only the "doesn't reject" half of the contract holds there); `bus.Validate` stays indifferent to unknown fields so a future publisher's new field cannot synthetically fail a current consumer's validator. **Backward compatibility** (current code reads older bytes): proto3 wire-omits zero-valued scalars, so an envelope marshaled with `CausationId/TraceContext/QualityFlags` unset is wire-indistinguishable from a v0 producer that never had them — asserted by round-trip + a wire-byte equality check that an explicitly-zero envelope and an unset envelope produce identical bytes under `Deterministic: true`. **Payload opacity**: `EventFrame.payload` round-trips identically for nil, empty, ASCII, arbitrary high-bit bytes, large blobs, parseable Timestamps, and Timestamps with appended unknown fields — proving the payload-blind tooling promise. **Payload-side forward/backward compat** using `timestamppb.Timestamp` as the stand-in domain payload: a v2 timestamp (Timestamp + unknown field 99) decoded by a v1 Timestamp binding preserves the unknown field through marshal + re-marshal; a v0 timestamp (only `seconds` set in raw wire bytes) decodes with `nanos=0` · `kanz/test/contract/compatibility/`
- [x] **EVT-21b** Contract tests: serialization round-trip parity (Go/Python/TS) — Go is the canonical generator: `kanz/test/contract/serialization/fixtures.go` defines the catalog (6 fixtures covering FACT root + lineage variants, COMMAND with caller-set idempotency_key, STATE_SNAPSHOT, OBSERVATION with no partition_key, OBSERVATION with a repeated QualityFlag), `cmd/genfixtures/main.go` marshals each via `proto.MarshalOptions{Deterministic: true}` and writes `<name>.bin` + `manifest.json` (language-agnostic, snake_case, enums as string names, timestamps split into seconds/nanos so any language can compare without an RFC-3339 parser). Three parity test suites load the same `.bin` + `manifest.json` and assert byte-identical payload + field-identical envelope: Go (`parity_test.go`) runs both in-memory parity and a drift-guard test that fails with the regen command if committed fixtures diverge from `BuildAll()`; Python (`kanz-py/tests/test_serialization_parity.py`) and TS (`kanz/ts/kanz-bus/test/serialization-parity.test.ts`) load via repo-relative paths, skip cleanly with regen hint if fixtures absent. Each language uses its native enum/timestamp APIs (Python `EventClass.Value`, TS `EventClass[name]`, Go `.String()`) so the contract is wire-form, not Go-specific. Every fixture also passes the EVT-21a `Validate` contract after the round-trip — fixtures cannot ship invalid envelopes · `kanz/test/contract/serialization/`, `kanz-py/tests/`, `kanz/ts/kanz-bus/test/`
- [x] **EVT-21a** Contract tests: envelope invariants — new `kanz/test/contract/envelope/` tree sits ABOVE the bus package and treats it as a black box: drives `Producer.Publish` through the real stamp → frame → wire path, then `Unframe` + `Validate` on the captured wire bytes. Tests are the wire-level spec (not internals): (1) end-to-end publish round-trip passes Validate + carries the `Nats-Msg-Id` broker-dedup header (EVT-17d); (2) every required field from envelope-policy §7 is rejected on receive — hand-framed bad envelopes assert what a misbehaving (non-Go, future-language, or buggy) publisher's wire bytes look like and that the consumer-side rejection holds; (3) `Unframe` rejects malformed bytes and frames missing the envelope (downstream nil-deref guarantee); (4) per-class invariants on the wire: FACT round-trip preserves idempotency_key == event_id; FACT mismatch rejected; COMMAND round-trip preserves caller-supplied idempotency_key and may differ from event_id; STATE_SNAPSHOT/OBSERVATION accepted with matching keys (only FACT carries the explicit mismatch rule per event-class-rules §1); (5) live vs replay sink contract: `Validate` rejects REPLAYED, `ValidateReplay` requires REPLAYED. Tests use the same `captureClient` + `validEnvelope` patterns as `kanz/pkg/bus` unit tests, but in the external `envelope_test` package so they exercise only the public surface · `kanz/test/contract/envelope/`

New `envelope.v1.EventFrame {Envelope envelope = 1; bytes payload = 2}` proto defines the wire frame so Envelope stays metadata-only and payload-blind tooling can deserialize the Envelope without the payload schema. `Validate` enforces every required-field rule from envelope-policy §7 + event-class-rules (FACT idempotency_key == event_id; live publish rejects QUALITY_FLAG_REPLAYED; producer_sequence==0 when partition_key empty). `Unframe` is the consumer-side counterpart. - [x] **EVT-17c** Go bus client lineage propagation: `Consumer` wraps a `Subscriber`, unframes + validates each inbound message, and stashes `correlation_id`, `event_id` (as the next event's `causation_id`), and `trace_context` onto the handler's `context.Context` via `With*`/`*FromContext` helpers. `Producer.stamp` reads those fields when the corresponding `Event` field is empty (precedence: explicit > ctx > root default). End-to-end test asserts a Consumer→Producer chain inherits all three fields; unit tests cover unframe failure, validation failure, handler-error surfacing, empty-trace skip, and explicit-beats-ctx precedence · `kanz/pkg/bus/`
- [x] **EVT-17d** Go bus client dedup: two reinforcing layers. **Publish side** — Producer stamps `Nats-Msg-Id: <idempotency_key>` on every outbound `bus.Message`; NATS JetStream uses this for broker-side dedup within its 2m window (EVT-08), Kafka carries it as a benign user header. **Consumer side** — bounded sliding `DedupWindow` (`Seen`/`Record`, 2m / 10k default; functional option `WithDedupWindow` to override or disable). Pipeline: `Unframe → Validate → Seen ⇒ skip ack` else `dispatch → Record on success`; failure leaves the window untouched so retries work. Methods are nil-safe so the zero-config (disabled) path needs no nil-checks. Tests cover Seen/Record split, TTL expiry, capacity eviction (oldest first), nil-safety, dedup-on-redelivery, no-record-on-handler-failure, dedup-disabled, distinct-keys-distinct-dispatch, and publish-side header stamping · `kanz/pkg/bus/`
- [x] **EVT-17e** Go bus client retry + DLQ: `RetryConfig {MaxAttempts, InitialBackoff, MaxBackoff}` drives an in-handler retry loop with exponential backoff (capped, ctx-cancelable; no jitter at this layer). `WithRetry(cfg)` and `WithDLQ(Publisher)` are independent options — either composable on its own. Terminal failures (retry exhausted, or `Unframe` / `Validate` failure before dispatch) republish the original message to `dlq.<original-subject>` with `Kanz-DLQ-{Original-Subject,Attempts,Error}` headers attached and the original wire body/headers preserved, then ack. DLQ-publish failure surfaces so the broker holds the message. DLQ recording into the dedup window treats DLQ as terminal so subsequent duplicates skip. Tests cover retry-until-success, retry-then-surface (no DLQ), retry-then-DLQ, unframe→DLQ, validate→DLQ, DLQ-records-dedup, ctx-cancel-during-backoff, and DLQ-publish-failure surfaces · `kanz/pkg/bus/`

- [x] **EVT-18c** Python bus client lineage propagation: lineage rides `contextvars.ContextVar` (PEP 567) — the Python analog of Go's `context.Context`. `kanz_bus.propagation` exposes `propagation_context(*, correlation_id="", causation_id="", trace_context="")` (sync context manager that sets non-empty fields and resets via the contextvars Token on exit, even in async code) and three `get_*` readers. `Consumer` wraps a `Subscriber`, unframes + validates each delivery, then stashes `env.correlation_id`, `env.event_id` (as the *next* event's causation_id), and non-empty `env.trace_context` for the handler's duration — so `Producer.publish` inside the handler auto-inherits via the contextvars. `EventHandler = Callable[[Envelope, bytes], Awaitable[None]]` has no explicit ctx arg (idiomatic Python; lineage is implicit). `Producer.stamp` precedence is explicit Event field > contextvar > root default. End-to-end Consumer→Producer chain test asserts inheritance; unit tests cover contextvar set/reset/nest semantics, empty-arg non-overwriting, asyncio.create_task copy semantics, and unframe/validate failure surfacing · `kanz-py/`
- [x] **EVT-18d** Python bus client dedup + retry + DLQ: combines Go EVT-17d+17e into one subtask per the Python plan. `DedupWindow{ttl_seconds, max_entries}` (default 2m/10k, `ttl_seconds<=0` ⇒ disabled-mode no-op instance — Python's nil-safe-method analog). `RetryConfig{max_attempts, initial_backoff_seconds, max_backoff_seconds}` exponential backoff capped at `max_backoff_seconds`; ctx-cancelable via `asyncio.sleep`. `ConsumerConfig{dedup_window, retry, dlq}` dataclass — composable; any field omitted defaults to the safe value. Pipeline: `unframe → validate → seen ⇒ skip ack` else `retry-loop(handler) → record on success / DLQ-then-record on exhausted`. Pre-dispatch failures (`unframe`/`validate`) route to DLQ with `Kanz-DLQ-Attempts: 0`. DLQ subject = `dlq.<original>`, `Kanz-DLQ-{Original-Subject,Attempts,Error}` headers + original body preserved. DLQ publish failure surfaces (broker holds the message). `asyncio.CancelledError` propagates out of the retry loop without being swallowed by `except Exception`. Tests: dedup seen/record + TTL + eviction + disabled-mode; retry backoff math; consumer retry-until-success / surface-after-retry / route-to-DLQ-after-retries / unframe→DLQ / validate→DLQ / dedup-on-redelivery / no-record-on-failure / dedup-disabled / DLQ-records-dedup / DLQ-publish-failure-surfaces · `kanz-py/`

- [x] **EVT-20c** Replay: `QUALITY_FLAG_REPLAYED` stamping + live-sink hard-reject. **Producer side**: new `kanz/tools/replay/replayed.go` with idempotent `StampReplayed(env)` + `IsReplayed(env)` predicate; Pipeline (EVT-20b) now calls `StampReplayed(ev.Envelope)` once per event before re-marshaling the EventFrame, so the wire bytes carry the flag. Idempotent so replay-of-a-replay (degenerate but possible) does not double-stamp. **Consumer side**: refactored `bus.Validate` — extracted `validateEnvelopeFields` (unexported, all field/class checks) + split the REPLAYED guard in two: `rejectReplayed` (live mode) and `requireReplayed` (replay mode). `bus.Validate` = fields + rejectReplayed (existing live-path behavior preserved; an event flagged REPLAYED that leaks past namespace isolation is hard-rejected at the consumer boundary → DLQ if `WithDLQ`, surface otherwise). `bus.ValidateReplay` = fields + requireReplayed — replay-scoped consumers reject *un-flagged* events because an un-flagged event on a replay subject means a publisher skipped 20c stamping (defect, not data). New `bus.WithValidator(fn)` ConsumerOption swaps the per-message validator; replay-scoped consumers use `bus.WithValidator(bus.ValidateReplay)`. Tests: validate (ValidateReplay requires flag / accepts flagged / still enforces field rules), consumer (default validator rejects REPLAYED + never dispatches / WithValidator(ValidateReplay) accepts + dispatches / WithValidator(ValidateReplay) still rejects un-flagged), replayed (IsReplayed nil/empty/flagged, StampReplayed idempotency, preserves other flags, nil-safe), pipeline (every published message carries REPLAYED, stamping idempotent over pre-flagged source). EVT-20 epic complete: replay is now end-to-end runnable — Reader (20a) → Pipeline namespace + group isolation (20b) → flag stamping + bidirectional validator switch (20c) · `kanz/tools/replay/`, `kanz/pkg/bus/`
- [x] **EVT-20b** Replay: isolated consumer-group / `replay.{run_id}.*` namespace wiring. `RunID` is a UUIDv7 hex-encoded as 32 lowercase chars (single subject segment, no dash-escaping on either transport) — time-sortable so operator audit trails sort chronologically. `SubjectFor(runID, original) → "replay.{runID}.{original}"` enforces both reserved-prefix rules from subject-taxonomy.md §6 (rejects originals already under `replay.` or `dlq.`). `ConsumerGroup(runID) → "replay-{runID}"` is the durable-consumer / consumer-group ID replay-scoped subscribers MUST use so their offsets can never collide with live consumer groups — the consumer-group half of EVT-20b's isolation. `Source` interface (just `Next`) decouples Pipeline from the concrete EVT-20a Reader so it's fakeable in unit tests. `Pipeline {RunID, Source, Publisher, Logger}` drains Source → re-marshals each event into a fresh `EventFrame` → republishes to the SubjectFor-rewritten subject via the raw `bus.Publisher` (not Producer — we're replaying pre-stamped envelopes, not stamping fresh ones). Headers (notably `Nats-Msg-Id`) ride through so destination-stream dedup catches duplicate deliveries from the source range, while being per-stream means no cross-stream interference with the original live publication. Retrofit of EVT-20a: per-message unframe failures are now wrapped as `*MalformedFrameError` so Pipeline cleanly discriminates skippable-and-log from fatal-source-error via `errors.As`. Tests cover RunID format/distinctness/sortability, SubjectFor validation matrix, ConsumerGroup, Pipeline happy-path (with full envelope round-trip + header propagation), malformed-skip, fatal-source-abort, publish-error-abort, field validation, and wrapped-MalformedFrameError still routes to skip · `kanz/tools/replay/`
- [x] **EVT-20a** Replay: Kafka log reader. New `kanz/tools/replay/` package. `Reader` is a one-shot iterator (`Next` → io.EOF) over a finite Kafka log window; `Range` requires an explicit end bound (`EndOffset` inclusive, or `EndTime` exclusive) so the read terminates without a high-water-mark probe. Optional `StartOffset` / `StartTime` (mutually exclusive; default = `kafka.FirstOffset`). Per-partition readers under `kafka-go` with **no `GroupID`** — replays never mutate live consumer-group positions. Partition discovery via `kafka.Dialer.DialContext` + `conn.ReadPartitions`; optional `Config.Partitions` short-circuits the dial. Per-partition goroutine fans into a buffered channel multiplexed by `Next`. Each delivered `Event` carries the unframed `*envelopepb.Envelope`, `Payload` bytes, and Kafka coordinates (`Topic`/`Partition`/`Offset`/`KafkaTime`/`Key`/`Headers`); malformed frames surface as `(Event{kafka-coords-only}, err)` so investigators can log + skip without aborting the run. Per-message `Validate` is deliberately not run — replay must surface what's *in* the log, not filter it. Unit tests cover `Range` validation matrix + `NewReader` defaults + explicit-partition dedup/sort; integration test gated on `TEST_KAFKA_BROKERS` exercises offset-range/time-range/partition-restriction and the malformed-frame error path · `kanz/tools/replay/`
- [x] **EVT-19c** TS bus client lineage propagation: `withPropagation(ctx, fn)` wraps `AsyncLocalStorage.run` with the same "non-empty fields only" rule as Go's `WithTraceContext` skip-on-empty and Python's `propagation_context` partial-set. TS API shape is a function-taking wrapper (not a context manager / decorator) because `AsyncLocalStorage.run(store, callback)` already binds the store to a callback — the wrapper just computes `next = {explicit || outer || ""}` per-field and forwards. `Consumer` wraps a `Subscriber`, runs `unframe → validate → withPropagation({correlationId, causationId: env.eventId, traceContext}, () => handler(env, payload))`; pre-dispatch errors propagate out so the underlying NATSClient/KafkaClient triggers broker redelivery (nak / skip-commit) — bounded retry + DLQ are outside EVT-19 scope per the task board, so handlers must be idempotent (event-class-rules §1) and broker redelivery is the only recovery primitive. `EventHandler = (Envelope, Uint8Array) => Promise<void>` has no explicit context arg — lineage is implicit via the async-store, same idiom as Python's `EventHandler`. End-to-end Consumer→Producer chain test asserts inheritance of correlation/causation/trace; tests also cover explicit-beats-store precedence, empty-trace-doesn't-clobber-outer, unframe/validate failure surfacing with handler-not-called, async-store reset after handler, and propagation persisting across `await` + a `.then()`-chained child task (TS analog of asyncio.create_task copy semantics). EVT-19 epic complete: TS client is now end-to-end runnable and wire-compatible with Go EVT-17 + Python EVT-18 · `kanz/ts/kanz-bus/`
